share of non-negative $2\times2$ minors is only $0.51$--$0.88$), so the Gantmacher--Krein
oscillation-matrix route is closed; nor is $T$ an M-matrix. Second, differencing $Tu=\tilde t$
once gives an \emph{exact} discrete boundary-value problem for the increments alone,
$Kv=s-u_0G\mathbf 1$ with $K=D_1TL$ (relative residual $2.4\times10^{-12}$), with a smooth,
positive right-hand side --- the correct classical shape for a maximum-principle argument ---
but $K^{-1}$ is \emph{not} entrywise positive (positive share $0.44$--$0.51$, minimum entry
$-333$): the discrete maximum principle is not unproved, it is \emph{false}. The reason is
visible in the solution itself: $u$ is monotone at the corner and oscillatory in the bulk ---
that is the prime-power comb. The corner block of $K^{-1}$ \emph{is} entrywise positive
($0.9961$--$1.0000$), so a localised statement is not excluded --- but the corner components
of $v$ contract full rows of $K^{-1}$ carrying a genuine cancellation (no-cancellation ratio
$10^{-7}$--$10^{-3}$), so what the sign hypothesis needs is a corner-localised
Fisher--Hartwig decay estimate (Widom $1974$; B\"ottcher--Silbermann), not a sign pattern.

\emph{The $\alpha$-audit: (D2) is not vacuous but total.} The frame's window is not a free
parameter: in frame A the handover at the atom $u_k=\log n_k$ uses $D=g_k/(2\nu)$ and
$M_o=\lceil u_k/D\rceil+1$, hence $\alpha_o=u_k/2+O(D)$ \emph{exactly} (maximum deviation
$1.5\times10^{-2}$ over all $732$ admissible handovers) --- the consumed half-width \emph{is}
half the log of the zone, and only $1.4\%$ of the handovers have $\alpha\le2$. Because
$B=-1.0474$ is a property of the archimedean kernel alone and hence universal (spread
$1.2\times10^{-6}$), the criterion $\log(1/D_{\mathrm{frame}})\ge\Xi(\alpha)+B$ can be
evaluated on the whole zone table: it holds on exactly $3$ of $1492$ zones ($n=2,3,4$), i.e.\
for $\alpha\le\alpha^*=0.693$ --- \emph{below} the first admissible handover $n=7$. Not one
step of the induction is covered by the unconditional $D_0$ route (the resolution it would
need explodes to $M\sim10^{2}$--$10^{188}$). The fallbacks, measured and labelled: the comb
supremum is only $0.038$--$0.603$ of its certified cap $\Xi$ --- an incoherence gain of up to
$1.7\times$, but a grid supremum, hence a measurement and never a certificate (a certified
version would be an exponential-sum estimate over prime powers) --- and the large-sieve
narrow-dip floor (Montgomery--Vaughan $1974$) is positive on $2/6$ surveyed zones, at $n=32$
where $\inf\sigma_z=-0.45$ --- the dip route genuinely buys something --- and dies beyond
$\alpha\approx2$.

\emph{The $\gamma$ formula, and the wrong direction.} The CBS constant has an exact local
symbol identity: with $f_1=f(\theta)$, $f_2=f(\theta+\pi)$,
$\sigma_c\sigma_z-\tau^2=f_1f_2$ (relative residual $3.9\times10^{-16}$), hence
$1-\gamma(\theta)^2=f_1f_2/(\sigma_c\sigma_z)$ --- (D3) is no longer a bare measured constant
but a symbol infimum, the same class of object as $\inf\sigma_z$. Two honest caveats, both
measured: the naive symbol infimum is $25$--$210\times$ \emph{below} the matrix constant (the
finite odd section never sees the worst aliasing pair --- a finite-section transfer is
needed, not the infimum), and the measurement itself moved: $1-\gamma^2$ \emph{falls} with
$\alpha$ (fitted slope $-0.297\pm0.013$, range $0.079$--$0.283$, flat in the resolution at a
fixed zone) --- T119's $\ge0.181$ is reproduced on its own $\alpha$-range but is \emph{not}
uniform.

\begin{keybox}[The theorem after T120 --- eighteen links, four defects]
\emph{Candidate (V4).} Eighteen links ($7$ identities --- among them, new in T120, the
increment system $Kv=s-u_0G\mathbf 1$ and the symbol identity
$\sigma_c\sigma_z-\tau^2=f_1f_2$ --- $4$ certified, $2$ classical, $2$ measured, and the
chain), and the one hard statement of V3 is \emph{replaced}: (D1) is no longer a bare
inequality but a proved two-line implication --- the pairing certificate (C1), unconditional
given one sign of the corner increments. What a lemma paper could claim today: \emph{let $T$
be the finite odd Weil form on a window, $u=T^{-1}\tilde t$, and suppose the increments of
$u$ have one sign on the outer $n_C$ coarse cells; then $|R-1|\le\delta(n_C)$ with the
explicit pairing ratio $\delta$, hence $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}\ge1/(2+\delta)$ and
$\|y\|^2\ge(u_{\mathrm{end}}-u_0)^2/(2n_C(2+\delta)^2)$} --- everything after ``suppose'' is
proved; measured, $\delta\le0.0964$ for $n_C\ge8$, falling like $n_C^{-0.58}$.\\[3pt]
\emph{The defect list --- four items (was three):}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item[\textbf{(E1)}] \emph{one sign of the corner increments} --- the single hypothesis of
(C1)/(C2), measured on $100\%$ of the corner cells on all $3915$ rows; the two obvious
structural routes are refuted (the corner of $T^{-1}$ is positive but not TP2; the global
discrete maximum principle is false), so what remains is a corner-localised Fisher--Hartwig
estimate (Widom $1974$; B\"ottcher--Silbermann);
\item[\textbf{(E2)}] \emph{a uniform bound on the pairing ratio} $\delta$ --- a discrete
gradient estimate for the increments of $T^{-1}\tilde t$, strictly weaker than the old
Harnack statement (any finite $\delta$ suffices, $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}\ge1/(2+\delta)$);
\item[\textbf{(E3)}] \emph{$D_0$ for the whole ladder} --- (D2), enlarged: the frame consumes
$\alpha=u_k/2$ by construction and the criterion fails at $\alpha^*=0.693$, below the first
admissible handover; the open routes are a certified comb-incoherence bound or the
large-sieve dip geometry, which works on the first zones and dies beyond $\alpha\approx2$;
\item[\textbf{(E4)}] \emph{the $\alpha$-dependence of $\gamma$} --- (D3) with the direction
now known: $1-\gamma^2$ shrinks like $e^{-0.30\alpha}$ (a fit), so uniformity is not merely
unproved but questionable in the direction that matters.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Honest movement:} the one hard statement got much easier --- a two-line implication
with one classical hypothesis --- and the side defects got sharper and both got worse; the
count went three $\to$ four, and that is the honest direction.\\[3pt]
\emph{Disposition after T121:} (E3) is retired \emph{as stated} --- the chain link is a
section eigenvalue, not a symbol infimum, and the section stayed positive on the whole real
ladder --- and is replaced by the section lemma (E3$^*$) with the Hankel term; (E4) is
replaced by one named poly-log, (E4$^*$); (E1) and (E2) keep their kind, with the corner
certificates restated at $n_C=h/16$ (T121 found the $h/8$ convention used here too wide);
and the symbol-to-section transfer this list implicitly relied on is refuted (the odd
sector's Hankel term). The next subsection reports the measurements.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{Section positivity survives the real ladder; the net balance names one poly-log (T121)}
T121 (\texttt{wide\_window\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{WIDE.WINDOW}, $21/21$,
\textsc{wide-restructured}) asks the question the T120 defect list left sharpest: does the
$\sigma_z$ route survive the \emph{real} ladder --- the one whose window grows with the zone,
$\alpha_o=u_k/2$? It does, but not as the statement T120 wrote down. The route survives as a
\emph{section} statement and dies as a \emph{symbol} statement; the net $\alpha$-balance of the
whole chain loses only a single poly-log to a demand that itself falls like $\alpha^{-6}$, and
that poly-log splits without residue into two repairable steps; one link of the chain, filed by
T120 as classical, is honestly refuted on the way.

\emph{Section positivity instead of symbol positivity.} The chain never needed
$\inf\sigma_z>0$; it needs $\lambda_{\min}$ of the \emph{section} $T_h(\sigma_z)$ at the frame's
own resolution. Over $16$ frame windows of the real ladder ($n=7\ldots283\,303$,
$\alpha=0.985\ldots6.277$, $M_{\mathrm{frame}}=118\ldots431\,162$, $h=29\ldots107\,790$) the
section is \emph{positive on $16/16$} while the symbol infimum is negative on $8$ of them ---
and where it is negative, the section eigenvalue sits at $0.64$--$3.23\times|\inf\sigma_z|$ on
the \emph{positive} side. Direction kept honest: the dense rows ($h\le1700$) carry a
shifted-Cholesky certificate with the Wilkinson--Higham floor; the large rows are matrix-free
FFT-Lanczos \emph{measurements} (a Ritz value is an upper bound for $\lambda_{\min}$, so it can
refute positivity but never prove it). The mechanism is quantified at its classical address ---
the Christoffel function (Szeg\H{o} $1939$; Grenander--Szeg\H{o} $1958$, Ch.~5): a degree-$h$
polynomial cannot concentrate below the Fej\'er bandwidth $2\pi/h$, and every comb dip is only
$0.156$--$0.720$ of one Fej\'er width. The certified per-dip moment budget
$\lambda_{\min}\ge\mathrm{thr}-\sum_j(\mathrm{thr}+\delta_j)\,w_j\,(h+1)$ is positive on $8/16$
rows (up to $\alpha=3.446$, sharpness $0.20$--$0.76$) and tears above, for a computed reason:
the dip \emph{count} grows like $e^{2.41\alpha}$ (a fit) against the budget's factor $h$. So
(E3) is reduced from a symbol-infimum requirement that no ladder step satisfies to a
\emph{section lemma} the measurement satisfies everywhere: a Christoffel lower bound surviving
$O(e^{\alpha})$ Fej\'er-subresolved dips --- a Szeg\H{o}/Fisher--Hartwig question about the
comb, no longer a symbol question.

\emph{The net $\alpha$-balance --- the core number of the part.} Every factor of the chain is
measured on one two-dimensional $(D,\alpha)$ lever ($14$ zones $\times$ $3$ resolutions, $42$
rows). The individual links do fall with $\alpha$, exactly as T120's (E4) recorded:
$1-\gamma^2\sim\alpha^{-0.79}$, $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)\sim\alpha^{-1.07}$,
$\|y\|^2\sim\alpha^{-5.63}$, so the assembled supply falls like $\alpha^{-7.49}$. But the
\emph{demand} falls almost as fast ($\alpha^{-5.93}$ on this lever; T116 quoted $-6.04$): $79\%$
of the decay (E4) complained about is the demand's own. What is left is the only $\alpha$
deficit of the chain,
\[
\mathrm{SUPPLY}/\varepsilon_c\;\sim\;D^{-0.177\pm0.096}\,\alpha^{-1.565\pm0.135}
\]
(fits; the measured ratio is $4.9\times10^{-3}$--$0.15$ over $\alpha=0.98$--$6.31$) ---
net-negative in $\alpha$ by one to two powers (the refit per resolution lands in
$-1.88\ldots-1.62$ --- a range, not a decimal) but \emph{uniform in $D$}: the $D$ exponent is
zero within its own band, so refining the resolution neither helps nor hurts. The deficit is
then decomposed \emph{exactly}: the identity
$y^{\mathsf T}Sy/\mathrm{SUPPLY}=r_{\mathrm{ray}}\,r_{\mathrm{cbs}}/(1-\gamma^2)$ holds to
$2.8\times10^{-14}$, so $\alpha^{-1.57}$ splits without residue into $\alpha^{-1.02}$ from the
\emph{Rayleigh step} (using $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)$ on a $y$ that is nowhere near the minimal
eigenvector; measured slack $1.29$--$13.19$), $\alpha^{-0.51}$ from the CBS step (measured slack
$3.42$--$16.67$) and $\alpha^{-0.04}$ from discarding $\varepsilon_f$ --- \emph{nothing} of it
sits in the value of $\gamma$. The named rebuild is therefore structural, not a new constant:
bounding $y^{\mathsf T}A_zy$ directly (legitimate, because $y$ is the oscillation part of the
solution, not an arbitrary vector) recovers $65\%$ of the deficit by itself, and the CBS step's
own slack covers the rest.

\emph{Link (5) refuted: the Hankel term.} The section comparison
$\lambda_{\min}(A_z)\ge\lambda_{\min}(T_{h_c}(\sigma_z))$ --- which T120's assembly used as a
classical Grenander--Szeg\H{o} transfer --- is \emph{false} on $42/42$ rows (ratio
$0.357$--$0.916$, drifting with the window). The reason is structural and was hiding in the odd
coordinates: $A_z=Z^{\mathsf T}(\mathrm{Toeplitz}-\mathrm{Hankel})Z$, and Grenander--Szeg\H{o}
carries the even Toeplitz part only; the reflection Hankel term is not sign-definite. A defect
made visible rather than created: the section lemma (E3$^*$) must be about $A_z$, not about
$T_{h_c}(\sigma_z)$.

\emph{The corner sign, and the inconclusive Fisher--Hartwig comparison.} The one-sign run of
the increments covers the corner region the (C1)/(C2) certificates need on $24/24$ (zone,
resolution) rows at $n_C=h/16$ (run share $0.21$--$0.42$ of the section against the required
$0.125$ --- a safety factor of $1.68$--$3.38$, the sign uniformly negative); at the $h/8$
convention T119/T120 used it holds on only $21/24$, so the certificates are restated at $h/16$
--- which costs nothing, because $\delta$ grows like $n_C^{+0.29}$ (a fit): halving $n_C$
\emph{improves} $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}$. The $\alpha$-slope of the run share is
$-0.0258\pm0.0042$ (a fit); extrapolated linearly, the $h/16$ margin would be exhausted only
near $\alpha\approx11.7$ (an extrapolation, not a prediction). The Fisher--Hartwig comparison
itself is \emph{inconclusive, and that is the result}: the local exponent of the corner row of
$T^{-1}$ drifts by $-0.008\ldots+2.934$ between dyadic windows where the log-corrected FH shape
would allow only $-0.120$ --- the corner is in no asymptotic power regime at any resolution a
factorisation reaches, and an FH exponent quoted from these numbers would be a fit masquerading
as a structure. What the block does deliver: the no-cancellation ratio
$4.6\times10^{-8}$--$7.1\times10^{-5}$ settles that (E1) can \emph{never} be a sign-pattern
argument, and the minimal sufficient statement (E1$'$) is written out as an explicit
head-versus-tail inequality (head share $1/(1+1.63)$ to $1/(1+11.73)$ of the mass at $J=h/16$),
with the tail exponent as the one unknown a Widom-type estimate must supply.

\emph{$\delta$ over the full ladder.} The pairing quotient stays in $0.0126$--$0.1331$ over
$360$ rows spanning $n=7\ldots299\,983$ and three corner fractions --- always below $1$, no
outlier --- so $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}\ge1/(2+\delta)\ge0.4688$ everywhere measured, and
$\delta\sim n_C^{+0.29}$ confirms the $h/16$ restatement is free.

\begin{keybox}[The theorem after T121 --- the count stays four, every entry changes kind]
\emph{Candidate (V5).} Nineteen links: the identities (Schur/Haynsworth, CBS/Yserentant, the
T120 symbol identity, the aliasing form of $\sigma_z$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}=1/(1+R)$), the
certified pieces (the pairing certificate (C1); the shifted-Cholesky section floors; the
Christoffel/Fej\'er per-dip budget up to $\alpha=3.446$), the classical steps (CBS + Rayleigh,
lower; Cauchy--Schwarz, lower), the measured/fitted entries (section positivity on the ten
Lanczos rows; the one-sign run; the net balance) --- and one link \emph{refuted}: (5), the
symbol-to-section transfer, killed by the odd sector's Hankel term.\\[3pt]
\emph{The defect list --- four items, each with a changed kind:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item[\textbf{(E1)}] \emph{one sign of the corner increments} --- unchanged in kind, sharpened
twice: the certificates move to $n_C=h/16$ (clean on all rows there, only $21/24$ at $h/8$; the
move is free since $\delta\sim n_C^{+0.29}$), and (E1$'$) is now an explicit head-versus-tail
inequality whose tail exponent a corner-localised Fisher--Hartwig/Widom estimate must supply
--- the FH comparison is inconclusive at reachable $h$, and no sign-pattern argument can ever
do the job (no-cancellation ratio $4.6\times10^{-8}$--$7.1\times10^{-5}$);
\item[\textbf{(E2)}] \emph{a uniform bound on the pairing ratio} $\delta$ --- unchanged: no
outlier over $360$ rows to $n=299\,983$, $\delta=0.0126$--$0.1331$, any finite $\delta$
suffices;
\item[\textbf{(E3$^*$)}] \emph{the section lemma, with the Hankel term} (replaces (E3)) ---
$\inf\sigma_z>0$ at frame resolution is \emph{retired as a requirement}: the chain link is a
section eigenvalue, positive on $16/16$ frame windows up to $\alpha=6.277$, $h=118\,115$,
including $8$ windows with a negative symbol infimum. What replaces it is strictly smaller but
has two parts: (a) a Christoffel-function lower bound surviving $O(e^{\alpha})$
Fej\'er-subresolved dips (the certified budget delivers it for $\alpha\le3.446$ and tears
above); (b) the odd sector's Hankel term, which refutes link (5) as stated --- the lemma must
be about $A_z=Z^{\mathsf T}(\mathrm{Toeplitz}-\mathrm{Hankel})Z$, not about
$T_{h_c}(\sigma_z)$;
\item[\textbf{(E4$^*$)}] \emph{one residual poly-log in $\alpha$} (replaces (E4)) --- the net
balance carries $\alpha^{-1.565\pm0.135}$ and $D^{-0.177\pm0.096}$: net-negative by one to two
powers of $\alpha$ (per-resolution refits $-1.88\ldots-1.62$), \emph{uniform in $D$}. Strictly
better than (E4) as T120 wrote it: $79\%$ of the supply's $\alpha$-decay is the demand's own,
and the deficit sits in the Rayleigh step ($\alpha^{1.02}$) and the CBS step ($\alpha^{0.51}$),
none of it in $\gamma$ --- the address is a structural lower bound for
$y^{\mathsf T}A_zy$, which alone recovers $65\%$.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Honest movement:} the counter stays at four --- the target of getting back below three
was not met, because the residual $\alpha$ deficit is real --- but every entry changed kind,
and one link recorded as classical was found refuted, a defect made visible rather than
created.\\[3pt]
\emph{Disposition after T122:} both named repairs landed. Link (5) is retired by an
\emph{identity} --- $A_z=W^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)\,W$ with $W=BZ$ isometric, so the
Hankel term is the reflection half of an isometry and not an error term --- and the certified
band bound that replaces it is non-vacuous to $\alpha=6.31$, where the Christoffel budget tore
at $3.45$; the Rayleigh step is made structural and is \emph{sharp} (residual slack
$1.00$--$1.03$, flat in $\alpha$); the certified deficit halves to $\alpha^{-0.73}$, exactly
uniform in $D$, and with the measured CBS coupling the balance closes to within the chain's own
ceiling. (E3$^*$)/(E4$^*$) are thereby absorbed into one band-margin estimate (F3) plus one
structural CBS estimate (F4). The next subsection reports the measurements.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The Hankel term is an isometry half; the certified deficit halves (T122)}
T122 (\texttt{net\_balance\_repair\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{NET.BALANCE.REPAIR}, $20/20$,
\textsc{net-improved}) attacks the two worst-case steps the T121 decomposition named --- the
Rayleigh step and the link-(5) transfer --- and both repairs land. The headline is structural:
the odd sector's Hankel term, the object that refuted link (5), was never an error term to be
estimated.

\emph{The Hankel repair is an identity, not an estimate.} The compressed Hankel term has a
closed form, $(Z^{\mathsf T}HZ)_{jk}=b_{j+k}$ with $b_s=(c_{K-2s}-2c_{K-2s-1}+c_{K-2s-2})/2$
--- half a second difference of the lag sequence, exactly like the Toeplitz-side lags ---
verified to $7.0\times10^{-16}$ on all $36$ (zone,\,resolution) rows. So the oscillation
injection $Z$ suppresses \emph{both} halves by the same DTFT factor $|2\sin(\theta/2)|^2$: the
measured norm ratio is $0.123$--$0.781$, i.e.\ $O(1)$ --- the T118 mechanism does not make the
Hankel term small. The honest anatomy (the archimedean half numerically of rank $10$--$14$, the
Hartman/Peller smoothing; the comb half corner-localised on few anti-diagonals) is structure,
not a bound: the valid Weyl floor $\lambda_{\min}(T_{h_c})-\|Z^{\mathsf T}HZ\|$ is positive on
only $12/36$ rows (up to $\alpha=2.44$) and nowhere near sharp. The valid replacement for link
(5) is an \emph{identity}: with $W=BZ$,
\[
A_z\;=\;W^{\mathsf T}\,\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)\,W,\qquad W^{\mathsf T}W=I\ \ \text{(exact to $0.0$)}
\]
--- the Hankel term \emph{is} the reflection half of an isometry. From this and a certified
cell envelope ($g_{\mathrm{thr}}=(\mathrm{thr}-\ell)_+\ge0$; Parseval is exact because $|Wv|^2$
has degree $<M$) follows the certified band bound
\[
\lambda_{\min}(A_z)\;\ge\;\mathrm{thr}-\lambda_{\max}(G),\qquad
G=W^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(g_{\mathrm{thr}})\,W\succeq0,
\]
non-vacuous on $36/36$ rows up to $\alpha=6.31$ --- where the T121 Christoffel budget tore at
$\alpha=3.45$ --- recovering $0.429$--$0.995$ of the true $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)$; the
$e^{2.41\alpha}$ dip count no longer enters anywhere (measured gain over the per-dip Nikolskii
budget: $8.4$--$1.1\times10^{2}$). The honest caveat is where the threshold sweep puts its
optimum: $\mathrm{supp}(g)=0.95$--$1.00$ of the circle --- one certified end of a one-parameter
family whose other end (small $\mathrm{thr}$) is the dip budget that tore.

\emph{The structural Rayleigh step is sharp.} ``$y$ is high-band'' is a statement about $Wy$ at
the window resolution ($99.3$--$100.0\%$ of its mass above $|\theta|=\pi/2$, with
$\|Wy\|^2=\|y\|^2$ exact), not about $y$ itself ($0.1$--$1.7\%$ high-band in the coarse
coordinates --- the oscillation lives in the injection, not in the coefficients). The same
certified band identity, evaluated on $y$ instead of over the whole oscillation space ---
$y^{\mathsf T}A_zy\ge\mathrm{thr}\|y\|^2-y^{\mathsf T}Gy$ --- is positive on $36/36$ rows,
never exceeds the truth, and beats the worst-case product $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)\|y\|^2$ by a
factor $1.28$--$18.90$: the slack it removes is exactly T121's $r_{\mathrm{ray}}$, growing like
$\alpha^{+0.83}$, and the residual slack of the structural bound is
$q_{\mathrm{str}}=1.00$--$1.03$ with drift $\alpha^{-0.002\pm0.001}$ --- flat, i.e.\
\emph{sharp}: no $\alpha$-dependence of the chain passes through the Rayleigh step any more.
The CBS step is equally pessimistic: the actual coupling is $\kappa_y=0.0067$--$0.5311$ against
the worst case $\gamma^2=0.7173$--$0.9647$ (a factor of up to $100+$, growing like
$\alpha^{+1.52}$), and $1-\kappa_y=r_{\mathrm{cbs}}$ exactly --- T121's slack, now with a name.
One lever limit declared: at $M\le2048$ the coarse envelope of $\sigma_z$ is nonnegative on
$13/36$ rows --- the deep dips T118/T121 chased appear only at the frame's own resolution.

\emph{The new net balance.} Five supplies on one $(D,\alpha)$ lever ($12$ zones $\times$ $3$
resolutions, $36$ rows), all sound ($36/36$ below the truth $S_4=y^{\mathsf T}Sy$). The T121
baseline reproduces exactly ($S_0/\varepsilon_c\sim\alpha^{-1.562\pm0.163}$); repairing link
(5) alone does not change the exponent at all ($\alpha^{-1.565}$ --- a uniform floor cannot
beat $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)$; V1's contribution is that the floor is certified over the whole
ladder instead of tearing at $\alpha\approx3.45$); with both repairs the \emph{certified}
balance is
\[
S_2/\varepsilon_c\;\sim\;D^{-0.003\pm0.075}\,\alpha^{-0.729\pm0.090}
\]
(fits; measured $0.033$--$0.207$): the deficit \emph{halves}, and it is exactly uniform in $D$.
Adding the \emph{measured} CBS coupling gives $\alpha^{-0.113\pm0.063}$ --- statistically
indistinguishable from the chain's own ceiling $S_4/\varepsilon_c\sim\alpha^{-0.116\pm0.062}$
(the cost of discarding $\varepsilon_f$, untouched by both repairs). The residual decomposition
closes to $2.9\times10^{-16}$: $-0.729-0.002+0.615=-0.116$. In one sentence: V1 buys the ladder,
V2 buys the exponent, and the chain closes \emph{up to its own ceiling} as soon as the CBS step
is no longer a worst case.

\begin{keybox}[The theorem after T122 --- four defects, consolidated]
\emph{Candidate (V6).} The chain renumbered: the Haynsworth identity; the exact CBS split
$y^{\mathsf T}Sy=(1-\kappa_y)\,y^{\mathsf T}A_zy$ with $\kappa_y\le\gamma^2$ classical
(Yserentant); the new two-grid identity $A_z=W^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)W$ ($W=BZ$,
$W^{\mathsf T}W=I$) --- the line that retires link (5); the certified cell envelope; the
certified band bound (5R) with its uniform, $y$-free variant (Gershgorin); and the quoted
demand fit.\\[3pt]
\emph{The defect list --- four items (T119 $=3$, T120 $=$ T121 $=$ V6 $=4$; the target $<4$ not
met, but the kinds changed twice over):}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item[\textbf{(F1)}] \emph{one sign of the corner increments} --- unchanged from T120/T121 and
untouched by this part: $24/24$ clean at $n_C=h/16$, no sign-pattern argument possible, the
minimal statement is the head-versus-tail inequality (E1$'$) with an open tail exponent;
\item[\textbf{(F2)}] \emph{a uniform bound on the pairing ratio $\delta$} --- unchanged and
untouched: $0.0126$--$0.1331$ over $360$ rows, no outlier, a discrete gradient estimate still
missing;
\item[\textbf{(F3)}] \emph{an analytic $(D,\alpha)$ bound for the band margin}
$1-y^{\mathsf T}Gy/(\mathrm{thr}\|y\|^2)$, measured $0.024$--$0.363$ over $\alpha=0.98$--$6.31$
with fitted drift $D^{-0.042}\alpha^{-0.525}$ --- the one \emph{new} item, and it absorbs link
(5), the Nikolskii budget and both readings of (E3)/(E4): a Fej\'er/Christoffel question about
how the comb dips of the window symbol overlap the high-band mass of the two-level residual,
not an eigenvector question. An $\alpha$-flat band margin would carry the certified chain to
within the CBS step of the truth;
\item[\textbf{(F4)}] \emph{a structural CBS estimate} --- $\kappa_y\le\gamma^2$ is the last
worst-case step in the chain (worth $\alpha^{+0.615}$ in the balance), and the actual coupling
is only $0.009$--$0.551$ of it.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Honest movement:} link (5) is not repaired but \emph{retired} --- it was a false
comparison between an isometric compression and one of its two halves --- and the per-dip
Nikolskii budget went with it; what is left of the old (E3)/(E4) is a single scalar estimate
about dips versus high-band mass, plus the CBS item.\\[3pt]
\emph{Disposition after T123:} the two scalar items were run against the same band machinery
and they \emph{split}. (F3) closes almost for free --- the reduction to the band profile of
$Wy$ costs under $1\%$, and the oscillation block gains uniform certified positivity,
(5R$^*$) --- while (F4) resists structurally: every certified route to $\kappa_y$ needs the
\emph{coarse} form from below, whose near-null direction is smooth and lives on a cancellation
that no pointwise symbol minorant can see. The remaining $\alpha^{+0.500}$ gap to the ceiling
is thereby identified with the $\varepsilon_f$ lid itself --- the recursion base case. The
next subsection reports the measurements.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The two-isometry completion: the margin closes, the coupling resists (T123)}
T123 (\texttt{structural\_cbs\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{STRUCTURAL.CBS}, $20/20$,
\textsc{cbs-resists}) attacks the two scalar estimates the V6 list left --- the structural
CBS step (F4) and the band margin (F3) --- with the same band machinery that made the
Rayleigh step sharp in T122. The margin closes almost for free; the coupling does not, and
the reason \emph{why} is the content of the part.

\emph{The coupling anatomy, in closed form.} The coarse block is the \emph{other} half of the
T122 construction: with $V=BP$ ($P$ the piecewise-constant prolongation), $V$ is an isometry
too --- $V^{\mathsf T}V=I$ and $V^{\mathsf T}W=0$ exact --- so the coarse, oscillation and
coupling blocks are three compressions of \emph{one} window Toeplitz form,
\[
A_c=V^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)\,V,\qquad
B_x=V^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)\,W,\qquad
A_z=W^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Toeplitz}_M(c)\,W
\]
(identities to $3.4\times10^{-15}$ on all $24$ rows). Two new closed forms complete the T122
anatomy: the coupling is half a \emph{first} difference of the lag sequence,
$(B_x)_{jk}=\beta_{j-k}-(c_{K-2(j+k)}-c_{K-2(j+k)-2})/2$ with
$\beta_m=(c_{|2m+1|}-c_{|2m-1|})/2$, verified to $1.8\times10^{-15}$ ($A_z$ was half a
\emph{second} difference), and in symbol form
\[
b(\varphi)\;=\;-\tfrac{i}{2}\,\sin(\varphi/2)\,
\bigl[\sigma(\varphi/2)-\sigma(\varphi/2+\pi)\bigr]
\]
--- the \emph{aliasing difference} of the window symbol times $\sin(\varphi/2)$, verified to
$5.7\times10^{-16}$: the coupling weight vanishes exactly where the coarse space lives. That
explains $\kappa_y$ quantitatively for the first time: $Wy$ carries $99.64$--$100.00\%$ of
its mass at $d=|\theta-\pi|<\pi/2$ and only $8.9\times10^{-6}$--$1.5\times10^{-3}$ in the
cell containing $\theta=0$, while the worst-case $y^*$ that attains $\gamma^2$ is a
\emph{middle}-band vector ($81.22$--$100.00\%$ of its mass exactly there) --- the factor
$2$--$109$ between $\kappa_y=0.0067$--$0.4622$ and $\gamma^2=0.7248$--$0.9467$ \emph{is}
that mismatch.

\emph{The certified $\kappa$ bound: both routes fail for one reason in two guises.} Route 1,
band-split Cauchy--Schwarz (per frequency band separately, the strengthened-CBS mechanism of
Axelsson--Vassilevski, including the exact two-grid factorisation that puts the
$\sin(\varphi/2)$ zero into the certificate), is correct on $24/24$ rows and non-vacuous on
$0/24$: it costs exactly one coarse condition number
($\mathrm{cond}(A_c)=2.1\times10^{4}$--$3.1\times10^{7}$). Route 2 substitutes the certified
minorant $T_M(\ell)\preceq T_M(\sigma^{(M)})$ into the Schur complement's minimum
characterisation and delivers two new links:
\[
\text{(5R$^*$)}\quad y^{\mathsf T}A_zy\;\ge\;y^{\mathsf T}E_zy,
\qquad
\text{(7R$^*$)}\quad y^{\mathsf T}Sy\;\ge\;y^{\mathsf T}E_zy-(E_xy)^{\mathsf T}E_c^{-1}(E_xy),
\]
with $E_c=V^{\mathsf T}T_M(\ell)V$, $E_x=V^{\mathsf T}T_M(\ell)W$,
$E_z=W^{\mathsf T}T_M(\ell)W$. (5R$^*$) holds with
$\lambda_{\min}(E_z)=+0.303\ldots+5.017>0$ on \emph{every} row of the ladder --- uniform
certified positivity of the oscillation block, at slack $1.0007$--$1.007$, subsuming the
whole T122 threshold sweep for free (since $\min(\ell,\mathrm{thr})\le\ell$ for every
threshold) --- while (7R$^*$) is certified as soon as $E_c\succ0$, which holds on $0/24$
rows ($\lambda_{\min}(E_c)=-3.44\ldots-0.043<0$ everywhere). The asymmetry is the result of
the part: $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)=0.32$--$5.08$ is well conditioned, while
$\lambda_{\min}(A_c)=1.7\times10^{-5}$--$2.9\times10^{-4}$ sits three to five orders below
the certified envelope margin ($8.4\times10^{2}$--$4.0\times10^{5}\times\lambda_{\min}(A_c)$,
rising like $\alpha^{+4.202}$; against $\lambda_{\min}(A_z)$ the same margin is only
$0.037$--$17$). The near-null direction of the window form is \emph{smooth}, so it lives in
the coarse space, and its eigenvalue comes from a cancellation \emph{inside} the form that no
pointwise symbol minorant can see. The classical worst-case step $\kappa_y\le\gamma^2$
(Yserentant) therefore \emph{stays} in the chain --- and V7 now states why it must.

\emph{The band margin closes; the certified balance; the $\varepsilon_f$ lid.} (F3) reduces
by the same machinery: $y^{\mathsf T}Gy$ splits \emph{exactly} over dyadic depth layers of
the certified gap (closure $1.1\times10^{-15}$), and the two-factor bound [certified symbol
depth] $\times$ [Parseval mass of $Wy$ per band] overshoots by only $1.20$--$3.51$ --- the
reduction to the band profile of $Wy$, the same object the coupling analysis needs, costs
under $1\%$. The certified margin is positive on $5/24$ rows ($+0.0024$--$+0.3290$ against
the exact margin $0.070$--$0.560\sim\alpha^{-0.449}$); the honest lever limit:
$\min\ell=-2187\ldots-9.7$ at $M\le2944$ --- the deep dips of the frame's own resolution
stay out of reach. The certified balance --- the core number: the best fully certified
supply is
\[
S_6/\varepsilon_c\;\sim\;D^{-0.069\pm0.099}\,\alpha^{-0.544\pm0.113}
\]
(fits; $\alpha=0.98$--$6.31$, uniform in $D$ and stable across the three resolutions)
against the ceiling $S_4/\varepsilon_c\sim\alpha^{-0.044\pm0.076}$, so the gap is
$\alpha^{+0.500}$ --- and the residual split against $S_6$ has exactly \emph{two} terms,
closing to $2.8\times10^{-17}$: band profile $\alpha^{+0.000}$ (F3, now negligible) plus
coupling $\alpha^{+0.499}$ (F4, all of it). The $\varepsilon_f$ question then identifies the
lid: $\varepsilon_c=\varepsilon_f+y^{\mathsf T}Sy$ is an identity
($\varepsilon_f/\varepsilon_c=0.177$--$0.546\sim\alpha^{+0.030}$, flat), and an additive
chain that carries $\varepsilon_f$ instead of discarding it would have ceiling exactly $1$
--- but $\varepsilon_f$ needs its bound one level down, $4$--$8$ recursion levels to an exact
base case, and that base case is precisely (F4). The lid is not a modelling step; it is the
\emph{same object} as coarse-level positivity.

\begin{keybox}[The theorem after T123 --- four defects, restructured]
\emph{Candidate (V7).} The chain rewritten with the two-isometry completion: the Haynsworth
identity; the exact CBS split $y^{\mathsf T}Sy=(1-\kappa_y)\,y^{\mathsf T}A_zy$ with
$\kappa_y\le\gamma^2$ classical (Yserentant; Axelsson--Vassilevski) --- the \emph{only}
worst-case step left in the chain, and V7 states why it stays: every certified route to
$\kappa_y$ needs coarse-level positivity from below; the two-grid identities
$A_c=V^{\mathsf T}TV$, $B_x=V^{\mathsf T}TW$, $A_z=W^{\mathsf T}TW$ with $V=BP$, $W=BZ$ two
orthogonal isometries ($V^{\mathsf T}W=0$); the certified cell envelope; the uniform
oscillation positivity (5R$^*$); the certified band-margin bound; and the quoted demand
fit.\\[3pt]
\emph{The defect list --- four items (T120 $=$ T121 $=$ V6 $=$ V7 $=4$), restructured:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item[\textbf{(F1)}] \emph{one sign of the corner increments} --- unchanged, quoted from
T120/T121; together with (F2) it is the \emph{Harnack pair}, untouched by the band
machinery;
\item[\textbf{(F2)}] \emph{a uniform bound on the pairing ratio $\delta$} --- unchanged:
$0.0126$--$0.1331$ over $360$ rows, any finite value suffices;
\item[\textbf{(F3)}] \emph{one band-profile lemma} --- the band margin reduces to the band
profile of $Wy$ at a cost of $0.7\%$, and (5R$^*$) supplies the uniform certified positivity
of the oscillation block for free; what remains is one analytic statement about how the mass
of $Wy$ distributes over frequency bands;
\item[\textbf{(F4)}] \emph{coarse-level positivity} --- \emph{identified}, not estimated:
every certified route to the coupling needs $\lambda_{\min}$ of the coarse form from below,
that eigenvalue is a smooth-direction cancellation invisible to symbol minorants, and it is
the same object as the $\varepsilon_f$ lid that defines the chain's own ceiling.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Honest movement:} the counter stays at four, but the terrain changed: the whole
$\alpha^{+0.500}$ gap to the ceiling now sits in one named obstruction whose resolution
cannot be a sharper two-level estimate. The next step is not sharper banding but the
\emph{recursion} --- the level telescope
$\varepsilon_\ell=\varepsilon_{\ell+1}+y_\ell^{\mathsf T}S_\ell y_\ell$ with certified
per-level contributions.\\[3pt]
\emph{Disposition after T124:} the recursion was built, and the direction flipped. Written as
a telescope, the rung contribution is a residual in the \emph{inverse} norm --- a maximum,
not a minimum --- so it is certifiable from \emph{above}, where the certified envelope works:
(F4) collapses from a quantitative estimate to the sign a coarse-to-fine induction already
carries, the base case becomes pure semidefiniteness, and $\lambda_{\min}(A_c)$,
$\mathrm{cond}(A_c)$ and $\gamma^2$ leave the chain entirely. The next subsection reports the
measurements.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The direction flip: the telescope carries, and (F4) collapses to a sign (T124)}
T124 (\texttt{multilevel\_telescope\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{MULTILEVEL.TELESCOPE},
$28/28$, \textsc{telescope-carries}) builds the recursion T123 demanded, and the exit turns
out to be not the recursion's \emph{length} but its \emph{direction}: written as a telescope,
the quantity each rung must supply is not a Rayleigh quotient (a minimum, needing the form
from below) but a residual in the inverse norm (a maximum, needing the form from above) ---
and from above, the certified envelope works, because Parseval is two-sided.

\emph{The telescope, exact and geometric.} On the level chain $D_0>D_1>\cdots>D_L$
(halvings, $\alpha$ fixed) the ladder is \emph{one} window form on nested spaces:
\[
P^{\mathsf T}A^{(\ell+1)}P=A^{(\ell)},\qquad P^{\mathsf T}b^{(\ell+1)}=b^{(\ell)}
\]
exactly ($2.4\times10^{-14}$; the pole-vector consistency is the identity
$\sinh(a-d)+\sinh(a+d)=2\cosh d\,\sinh a$) --- the coarse block of the window form at
$D_{\ell+1}$ \emph{is} the window form at $D_\ell=2D_{\ell+1}$, so the T122/T123 two-level
system is literally one rung of the ladder. Every rung is the T118 saturation identity,
$\varepsilon_\ell=\varepsilon_{\ell+1}+\delta_\ell$ with
$\delta_\ell=y_\ell^{\mathsf T}S_\ell y_\ell$, and Galerkin orthogonality (C\'ea) makes it
the energy of the level correction,
$\delta_\ell=\|u_{\ell+1}-Pu_\ell\|^2_{A^{(\ell+1)}}$ (verified to $3.7\times10^{-8}$) ---
nonnegative, hence the exact telescope
$\sum_\ell\delta_\ell=\varepsilon_0-\varepsilon_L$ ($9.2\times10^{-10}$). The level
distribution is geometric on average: the top rung carries $0.2722$--$0.8806$ of
$\varepsilon_0$, consecutive rungs fall by a median factor $0.316$ (quartiles
$0.229$--$0.473$, against $2^{-\theta}=0.289$ from the demand law), and at $L=5$ the ceiling
of the additive chain is $0.9911$--$0.9987$ --- against T123's two-level ceiling
$\alpha^{-0.044}$. Rung law (a fit): $\delta\sim D^{+1.598}\alpha^{-5.900}$.

\emph{The certified rung contribution --- the direction flip.} The rung has two dual
descriptions. Primal, $\delta_\ell=\min_{w\in V_\ell}\|u_{\ell+1}-w\|^2_A$: a
\emph{minimum}, every test vector an upper bound --- the wrong direction for a supply. Dual:
the level residual $r_\ell=t_{\ell+1}-A^{(\ell+1)}Pu_\ell$ satisfies
$V^{\mathsf T}r_\ell=0$ (Galerkin), so $r_\ell=W\hat s_\ell$ lives purely in the oscillation
space, and
\[
\delta_\ell\;=\;\|r_\ell\|^2_{A^{-1}}\;=\;\hat s^{\mathsf T}S^{-1}\hat s
\;=\;\max_v\;\frac{(\hat s^{\mathsf T}v)^2}{v^{\mathsf T}Sv}
\]
--- a \emph{maximum}: every test vector gives a lower bound, and the denominator wants $S$
from \emph{above}. That is where the certified machinery works: $S\preceq A_z$ needs only
$A_c\succeq0$ (Haynsworth monotonicity), $A_z\preceq U:=Z^{\mathsf T}T_M(\mathrm{up})Z$ is
Parseval against the certified majorant $\mathrm{up}\ge\sigma^{(M)}$ (the \emph{mirror} of
T123's $E_z=W^{\mathsf T}T_M(\ell)W$, on the block where it works), and Loewner inversion
turns the upper bound on $S$ into the lower bound on the rung,
\[
\text{(8R)}\qquad \delta_\ell\;\ge\;\hat s^{\mathsf T}U^{-1}\hat s,
\]
with the optimal test vector $v^*=U^{-1}\hat s$ in closed form --- no coarse inverse, no
$\lambda_{\min}(A_c)$, no CBS constant. (8R) is valid on $400/400$ rungs ($80$ chains
$\times$ $L=5$, $20$ zones), recovering $\mathrm{cb}/\delta=0.4739$--$0.9938$ at drift
$\alpha^{-0.080\pm0.007}$ --- seven times weaker than T123's $\alpha^{-0.544}$; of the loss,
the coupling accounts for $0.5036$--$0.9967$ and the envelope only $0.7895$--$0.9994$.
T123's obstruction remains and is \emph{irrelevant}: $\lambda_{\min}(E_c)<0$ on $399/400$
rungs, and (8R) never forms $E_c$. The Levinson bordering route honestly \emph{falls}: the
innovation ledger $q=\sum_n\rho_n^2/E_n$ is exact per level ($9.6\times10^{-12}$;
Levinson--Durbin--Delsarte--Genin), but refinement is not a bordering --- the two
filtrations are transversal (head$/q_\ell=0.61$--$1.00$, tail$/\delta_\ell=13$--$863$) ---
so the rung must be read in the (8R) form.

\emph{Self-similarity, and why the recursion closes.} The near-null direction of the window
form stays \emph{smooth} at every level (oscillation fraction
$4.3\times10^{-6}$--$1.2\times10^{-1}$) --- T123's diagnosis, confirmed level by level ---
and it is self-similar along the ladder: prolonged, $w_\ell$ has Rayleigh quotient exactly
$\lambda_{\min}(A^{(\ell)})$ at the finer level (an identity, from nesting), i.e.\ within a
factor $1.30$--$10.11$ of that level's own minimum, at subspace overlap
$\|Q_K^{\mathsf T}Pw_\ell\|=0.944$--$1.000$. But (8R) asks only for the \emph{sign} of
$A_c$, and by the nesting identity that sign is the previous rung's own conclusion:
positivity propagates fine $\to$ coarse for free (so the opposite direction would be
circular --- stated explicitly), and the non-circular induction runs coarse $\to$ fine, with
base case $\varepsilon_L\ge0$ --- pure semidefiniteness of the smallest window form.

\emph{The balance; the honest negative.} Telescope reading:
$\sum_\ell\mathrm{cb}_\ell/\varepsilon_0=0.6404$--$0.9729$ against the ceiling
$0.9911$--$0.9987$ (fit $D^{-0.066}\alpha^{-0.084\pm0.033}$). Two-level comparison,
like-for-like against T123's certified balance: $\mathrm{cb}/\varepsilon_c\sim
D^{-0.090}\alpha^{-0.100}$ against T123's $D^{-0.069}\alpha^{-0.544}$ --- the $\alpha$
exponent moves by $+0.444$ of the $\alpha^{+0.500}$ gap. The honest negative, recorded as
(F5): the \emph{solution-free} version of the rung fails --- the coupling term cancels the
data high-pass rather than perturbing it ($|r_{\mathrm{corr}}|=0.88$--$2.61$ of the explicit
part), so any norm bound kills the certificate to exactly $0$; what would be needed is a
bound on a \emph{direction}, not a size. (8R) therefore carries the coarse solution along
--- on the same bookkeeping standard as the whole chain: $\hat s$ is a function of the
carried coarse solution exactly as $y$ is in T122/T123's own certified supplies.

\begin{keybox}[The theorem after T124 --- (F4) collapses to a sign]
\emph{Candidate (V8).} The telescope chain: the nesting identity
$P^{\mathsf T}A^{(\ell+1)}P=A^{(\ell)}$ (the ladder is one window form on nested spaces);
the saturation telescope $\varepsilon_0=\varepsilon_L+\sum_\ell\delta_\ell$ (exact); the
dual residual form of the rung with the certified per-rung bound (8R)
$\delta_\ell\ge\hat s^{\mathsf T}U^{-1}\hat s$, needing only $A_c\succeq0$ (Haynsworth) and
the Parseval majorant $U\succ0$; and the coarse-to-fine induction whose hypothesis at rung
$\ell$ is the conclusion of rung $\ell-1$, with base case $\varepsilon_L\ge0$.\\[3pt]
\emph{The defect list --- the counter reads differently now:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em]
\item[\textbf{(F1)}] \emph{one sign of the corner increments} --- unchanged, quoted from
T120/T121; with (F2) the \emph{Harnack pair}, untouched by telescope and band machinery
alike;
\item[\textbf{(F2)}] \emph{a uniform bound on the pairing ratio $\delta$} --- unchanged:
$0.0126$--$0.1331$ over $360$ rows, any finite value suffices;
\item[\textbf{(F3)}] \emph{closed} --- the T123 reduction to the band profile of $Wy$ plus
the uniform oscillation positivity (5R$^*$); the telescope no longer needs it;
\item[\textbf{(F4)}] \emph{collapsed} --- no longer a quantitative estimate: (8R) needs
only the sign of $A_c$, which the coarse-to-fine induction already carries;
$\lambda_{\min}(A_c)$, $\mathrm{cond}(A_c)$ and $\gamma^2$ leave the chain entirely, and
the base case is pure semidefiniteness;
\item[\textbf{(F5)}] \emph{new, a bookkeeping convention, honestly stated} --- the
certified rung carries the coarse solution along (the solution-free version provably kills
to zero: the coupling is a cancellation, not a perturbation); this is the same standard the
whole chain already uses, but it is a convention, not a theorem, and it is listed.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Honest movement:} the two-level worst case is gone --- the last quantitative
obstruction of the certification arc is replaced by a sign that the induction itself
supplies, at the price of one named convention. What remains is the Harnack pair, one
constant-not-rate loss per rung, and the assembly of the full chain end to end.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The grand assembly (T125): the chain composes}\label{sec:t125}
T125 (\texttt{grand\_assembly\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{GRAND.ASSEMBLY}, $34/34$,
\textsc{assembly-green}) is the series finale, and it does exactly one thing: it mounts every
certificate of the arc on one ladder, end to end, and then states what the resulting object is.
Five stages per zone --- \textbf{[A]} the base-case Cholesky, \textbf{[B]} the telescope ascent
with the (8R) rungs, \textbf{[C]} the $\varepsilon$ lower bound on the target level, \textbf{[D]}
the $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}$/parity chain, \textbf{[E]} the margin-free Albert handover --- complete
on $52$ of $52$ ladder zones, plus ten deep zones on stage [E] alone up to $n=1331$
($h_{\mathrm{new}}$ up to $1495$).

\emph{The composing run.} The construction that makes this an assembly rather than a list is
ladder~1: $30$ \emph{consecutive} prime-power atoms $n=2\ldots79$ on \emph{one} common resolution
$D=\min_kg_k/(2\nu)=3.472446\times10^{-3}$ (with $\nu_{\mathrm{eff}}=4.0$--$58.4\ge4$, so every zone
of the run is admissible in the T105 sense). Because the resolution is common, the new window of
zone $k$ \emph{is} the old window of zone $k+1$: $M_n(k)=M_o(k+1)$ on all $29$ consecutive pairs as
an \emph{integer} identity, the incoming atom's triangle restricted to the old window is the exact
zero matrix (bit-exact, $\max|\text{entry}|=0$), so $X=Q_{\mathrm{old}}$ with no truncation, and the
leading $h_o\times h_o$ block of the next zone's balanced form equals that $X$ to
$2.9\times10^{-14}$. The output of every step \emph{is} the input of the next. This is a
constructive way \emph{around} T114's [P2] seam rather than through it: $0$ of $84$ consecutive
log-gap ratios are dyadic --- per-zone frames have no common refinement --- so the run chooses one
frame for all of it, and pays for that with its length ($30$ zones, $n\le79$, because $D$ is set by
the smallest gap inside the run). Ladder~2 keeps the per-zone frames and reaches $22$ further zones
to $\alpha=2.885$, with the inter-zone seam declared rather than closed.

\emph{The load-bearing finding.} Reading the assembly stage by stage, the weakest stage of the
\emph{whole} mounting is [D] at \textsc{window-cert} on every zone --- but [D] is the second,
independent route. The \emph{load-bearing spine} [A][B][C][E] is at \textsc{cholesky-cert} on all
$52$ zones, its weakest stage being [A]. In other words: \textbf{the Harnack pair (F1)/(F2) no
longer carries anything}. It survives only inside conclusion (iv) of the theorem below, which
(i)--(iii) and (v) do not use.

\emph{The numbers, stage by stage.} The four identities the assembly stands on hold on all $88$
rungs of all $52$ zones (nesting to $3.0\times10^{-14}$ and $6.2\times10^{-16}$; the
saturation/dual-residual/Galerkin trio to $4.7\times10^{-7}$; the exact piecewise-constant lag
formula to $2.2\times10^{-14}$; the telescope $\sum_\ell\delta_\ell=\varepsilon_0-\varepsilon_L$ to
$3.3\times10^{-7}$). (8R) is certified on $88/88$ rungs by its three completed Choleskys, at
retention $\mathrm{cb}/\delta=0.9071$--$0.9971$ --- T124's band $0.4739$--$0.9938$, reproduced on a
\emph{different} ladder (frame windows, not the T124 $M$-chain) --- and the direction control holds:
the primal minimum form returns values \emph{above} $\delta$ on $88/88$ rungs, i.e.\ it is an upper
bound and can never be a supply. The certified $\varepsilon$ lower bound lands on $52/52$ zones,
retaining $0.0013$--$0.9694$ of the measured $\varepsilon_0$ but $0.9140$--$0.9929$ of the $L$-level
\emph{ceiling} $1-\varepsilon_L/\varepsilon_0=0.001295$--$0.993502$ that an $L$-level chain can
supply at all, and it exceeds the \emph{declared} floating-point bound $c_hu\,\mathrm{cond}(A_0)$ by a
factor $3.2\times10^{1}$--$8.7\times10^{10}$. The base case is an \emph{equivalence}, not a bridge:
$\operatorname{sign}\lambda_{\min}(Q^{(L)})=\operatorname{sign}\varepsilon$ on $52/52$ zones at
$h=416$--$1384$, with $\lambda_{\min}(Q^{(L)})$ sitting $4.4\times10^{5}$--$5.6\times10^{10}$ times
its own floating-point floor. The margin-free handover certifies $62/62$ steps ($n=2\ldots1331$) with
the exact $g_c\times g_c$ Schur complement at $\lambda_{\min}=1.484\times10^{-3}$--$1.253\times10^{-1}$,
a factor $3.0\times10^{10}$--$4.6\times10^{13}$ above its own Cholesky floor --- while the
norm-perturbation surrogate of the \emph{same} Schur complement, the one that divides by
$\lambda_{\min}(X)$ and \emph{was} the T113 wall, is negative on $62/62$ steps. In total: $430$
completed Cholesky certificates and $440$ verified identities.

\emph{Three gates corrected in the open.} (a) The $\kappa$ band: the unconditional pairing
certificate (C1) dominates the measurement on $52/52$ rows, but its worst case over the ladder is
$C_1=0.20927$ --- and that worst case is a \emph{single} zone, the shallowest one ($n=2$,
$\alpha=0.3507$, only $n_C=12$ corner cells, too short for the pairing to average); drop that row
and $C_1\le0.06721$ on the remaining $51$, against T120's $0.04745$ measured on deep zones only.
(b) The head-room minimum is $31.6$, at the thinnest zone ($n=243$, $\alpha=2.7492$, $h_0=679$), and
it is thin because that zone's retention $0.6054$ is itself pinned to its small $L$-level ceiling
$0.629042$ --- not because the arithmetic is marginal; the factor stands over a \emph{rigorous}
bound, so any value above $1$ certifies the sign. (c) The balance is fitted at \emph{rung} level
rather than on the collinear zone fit: $\mathrm{cb}/\delta\sim D^{-0.010}\alpha^{+0.001}$ (bands
$\pm0.004$, $\pm0.005$, rms $0.012$) --- flat in \emph{both} parameters, the strongest form of
T124's quoted $\alpha^{-0.100}$ drift --- and at zone level on the composing run, where $D$ is
common and only $\alpha$ moves, $\sum\mathrm{cb}/\sum\delta\sim\alpha^{-0.012}$: the certificate does
not thin out along the run. The ratio reading $\mathrm{cb}/\varepsilon_c$ comes out at
$D^{+0.345}\alpha^{+1.566}$ here and is explicitly \emph{not} comparable with T124's
$D^{-0.090}\alpha^{-0.100}$ (a different denominator: a $2$--$5$-level frame chain against
$M=32$--$2560$ pairs) --- reported, not claimed.

\emph{Five seams, named and measured.} (S1) the inter-zone frame seam --- \emph{closed} on ladder~1
by the common-frame construction; (S2) the direction of the $\varepsilon$ bound --- structural and
declared: the certified bound lands on the \emph{coarse} level, so the target window must be the
coarsest level of its chain and the base case the finest (a bound at the fine level would need
certified \emph{upper} bounds on the rungs, i.e.\ the CBS constant T123 proved resists); (S3) the
matrix cap --- cost, not a defect; (S4) the (F5) accounting standard --- open, declared inside the
theorem, with the solution-free variant measured to return exactly $0$ of the rung
($|r_{\mathrm{corr}}|=0.3888$--$14.8018$: a cancellation, not a perturbation); (S5) finiteness ---
open, \emph{and it is the gap}.

\begin{keybox}[Theorem V-final --- the certified multilevel margin chain (T125)]
\emph{Setting.} Fix $\nu\ge4$; let $n_i<\cdots<n_j$ be \emph{consecutive} prime-power atoms with
$u_k=\log n_k$ and log-gaps $g_k$, and put $D=\min_kg_k/(2\nu)$ (one common resolution), $M_k$ the
smallest even integer with $M_kD>u_k+D$, $\alpha_k=M_kD/2$, $A^{(k)}$ the reflection-odd window form
on $h_k=M_k/2$ coordinates built from the exact Weil window lags, $b^{(k)}$ the odd pole vector,
$\varepsilon_k=1-b^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}b$ and $Q^{(k)}=A^{(k)}-b^{(k)}b^{(k)\mathsf T}$; for each $k$
let $D=D_0>\cdots>D_{L_k}$ be halvings at fixed $\alpha_k$.\\[3pt]
\emph{Hypotheses.} \textbf{(H-base)} $\varepsilon^{(k)}_{L_k}\ge0$ at the \emph{finest} level,
equivalently $Q^{(k,L_k)}\succeq0$ (Albert 1969) --- pure semidefiniteness, no rate, no margin.
\textbf{(C-F5)} an accounting convention, \emph{declared open}: the rung numerator
$\hat s_\ell=Z^{\mathsf T}b^{(k,\ell+1)}-B_x^{\mathsf T}u^{(k,\ell)}$ is a function of the
\emph{carried} coarse solution, the standard T122/T123 already used; a solution-free rung is
\emph{not} claimed --- it is measured to fail. \textbf{(H-F1)}/\textbf{(H-F2)} the Harnack pair,
needed \emph{only} for conclusion (iv).\\[3pt]
\emph{Conclusions.} (i) [\textsc{identity}] $P^{\mathsf T}A^{(k,\ell+1)}P=A^{(k,\ell)}$,
$P^{\mathsf T}b^{(k,\ell+1)}=b^{(k,\ell)}$, hence
$\varepsilon^{(k)}_0=\varepsilon^{(k)}_{L_k}+\sum_\ell\delta_\ell$ exactly.
(ii) [\textsc{certified}, given $A^{(k,\ell)}\succeq0$] (8R)
$\delta_\ell\ge\hat s_\ell^{\mathsf T}U_\ell^{-1}\hat s_\ell=:\mathrm{cb}_\ell>0$; the rung is a
\emph{maximum}, so its denominator wants the form from above, and $v^*=U_\ell^{-1}\hat s_\ell$ is
optimal in closed form --- no $A_c^{-1}$, no $\lambda_{\min}(A_c)$, no CBS constant.
(iii) [\textsc{certified}, given (H-base) and (C-F5)]
$\varepsilon^{(k)}_0\ge\sum_\ell\mathrm{cb}_\ell>0$, i.e.\ $Q^{(k)}\succ0$.
(iv) [\textsc{window-cert}, second and \emph{independent} route] the $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}$ chain,
with $|R-1|\le0.20927$, hence $R\le1.20927$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{end}}\ge0.45264$ on this run.
(v) [\textsc{certified}, margin-free] $X=Q_{\mathrm{old}}$ exactly, and $Q^{(k+1)}\succeq0$ follows
from Albert 1969 plus the Douglas range condition --- nothing refers to the \emph{size} of
$\varepsilon_k$, only to its \emph{sign}, which is (iii).
(vi) [\textsc{the chain}] therefore (i)--(iii) and (v) compose:
$Q^{(i)}\succeq0\Rightarrow\cdots\Rightarrow Q^{(j)}\succeq0$, each step a completed Cholesky above
its declared floating-point floor; (iv) is not used.\\[3pt]
\emph{Attribution ($25$ lines).} $10$ \textsc{identity}, $9$ \textsc{cholesky-cert}, $3$
\textsc{window-cert} (all three inside (iv)), $1$ \textsc{hypothesis} ((C-F5)); every eigenvalue,
condition number and retention is labelled \textsc{measured}, every exponent law a \textsc{fit}.\\[3pt]
\emph{Verified instance.} $D=3.472446\times10^{-3}$, $30$ consecutive zones $n=2\ldots79$,
$\alpha=0.3507$--$2.1494$, $h_k=101$--$619$, $88$ telescope rungs, plus $22$ non-composed reach zones
to $\alpha=2.885$ and $10$ handover-only zones to $n=1331$; all $430$ Cholesky certificates
completed.\\[3pt]
\emph{What is not claimed.} (a) No statement about all zones --- the run is \emph{finite} and its
length is limited by $\min_kg_k=0.027780$. (b) (H-base) is a Cholesky fact about \emph{one} finite
matrix, not a theorem. (c) (H-F1)/(H-F2) are window certificates, measured and bounded on the
measured windows, with no uniform proof. (d) (C-F5) is a convention, not a solution-free bound.
(e) No zeta function, no zero data, no explicit-formula positivity, \emph{in either direction}:
this is a statement about a given quadratic form at a given resolution.
\end{keybox}

% =====================================================================
\section{The kill list}\label{sec:kills}
% =====================================================================
\noindent A program that only reports what survived is not reporting. These are the readings and routes
that were refuted \emph{by the program's own probes}, each with the measurement that killed it. They are
listed as a section rather than as footnotes because they are the more transferable output: each one
tells a later attempt where not to go.

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.6em,label=\textbf{K\arabic*.}]
\item \textbf{The essential-singularity reading of the onset} (T96, refuted T102). T96 read the
counterfactual onset as super-polynomial, compatible with $\exp(-c/\delta')$, i.e.\ an essential
singularity. T102 showed the onset is \emph{anchored} at the crossing $\delta_c=2w_k$ of two finite
quantities ($R^2=0.968$), that exponential versus power behaviour is not separable at this resolution,
and that the scatter of $c$ ($\mathrm{CV}$ $103\%$) collapses to $c'=1.52$ in the scale-free variable.
The essential-singularity reading is \emph{compatible but not distinguished} --- and it is no longer
used.
\item \textbf{The $C/g$ law as a causal law} (T101, refuted T102). Triple negative: $g_k$ is causally
impossible ($Q_{k-1}$ is blind to atom $k+1$), statistically dispensable (causal law $C\cdot u$:
$\mathrm{CV}$ $23.3\%$ versus $27.6\%$), and arithmetically only a ceiling ($C_k\le g_k\mu_k$ exactly;
the $16$-zone extrapolation violates its own ceiling from $k=69$; checked on $18\,120$ prime-power
atoms to $n=200\,000$). \emph{It was a proxy.}
\item \textbf{The naive margin route} (T104). $M\ge m\cdot\mathrm{Id}$ holds in $0/16$ zones; the
coupling-mass to margin ratio is $10^3$--$10^6$; $m\sim M^{-1.7}$ vanishes in the continuum (fit). The
genuine form is nearly singular on the induction window and no margin rescues it. (The prolate wing
basis recommended by T96/T103 was tested in both arms and also rejected.)
\item \textbf{The density chain} (T106). It would need a hard gap $w_0\ge0.55$--$2.44$; measured
$5.3\times10^{-6}$--$1.1\times10^{-3}$ --- short by a factor $5\times10^2$ to $3.6\times10^5$. Dead for
good.
\item \textbf{The Landau--Widom anchor} (T106). The wrong classical anchor for this problem
(rms $0.50$--$1.55$). The right one is Grenander--Szeg\H{o} on the Planck-coarsened symbol --- averaging
over the mode-spacing cell $\pi/M$ --- which hits $0.92$--$2.43$ and is better on $67/72$ pairs. This
also explains why a negative symbol (dip $-21.3\ldots-3.4$) still gives $Q\succeq0$: the atoms oscillate
exactly at the window's Planck scale.
\item \textbf{The accumulated invariant with self-propagation} (T106). A no-go \emph{with a measured
mechanism}. The wing demand gives $\beta_{\mathrm{wing}}=2.21$--$14.43>1$ on $16/16$, but inherited
demands give $\beta_{\mathrm{int}}\sim10^{-2}$ on $15/15$: an accumulated invariant with absolute
anchoring is false. The reason is transport --- in a larger window an old wing pair becomes an interior
pair and draws $99.7$--$99.95\%$ of its demand from the $64$ softest modes (coupling $751$--$2653\times$
stronger). No self-propagation either ($\beta_{\mathrm{on}}<1$ on $15/15$, loss $13$--$320\times$,
$\theta_k=0.9866$--$0.9998$). \emph{The wing margin is an edge property of the window, not a property of
the pair.} What survives is the window-wise family $\sigma_k(\delta_0)\ge1.31(\mu_k/2)$, uniform in $M$
with drift $\le2.01\times$.
\item \textbf{The symbol route for the odd channel} (T107). Structurally dead, not merely weak: the
symbol lower bound for $T_{\mathrm{odd}}$ is certified but \emph{negative}
($\lambda_{\mathrm{cert}}=-2.54\ldots-0.81$) against a measured
$\lambda_{\min}(T_{\mathrm{odd}})=4.7\times10^{-5}\ldots6.4\times10^{-2}$ and a symbol infimum of
$-22\ldots-6$: five orders of magnitude and a sign change. The soft edge of $T_{\mathrm{odd}}$ is a pure
finite-section effect that no symbol can see. Coarse certified chains (Cauchy--Schwarz against
$\lambda_{\min}$) close $0/16$ --- and fail even with the \emph{measured} $\lambda_{\min}$, so it is a
category error, not a precision problem.
\item \textbf{Three certificate candidates for the avoidance norm} (T108). Killed \emph{quantitatively},
which is the useful form: the cancellation route ($\sum|k|=401$ against $\sum k=1$), the Bessel route
(off by $10^7$) and the pole Cauchy--Schwarz route (off by $10^6$). $\omega_{\mathrm{cert}}$ is blocked
by the compression Schur distance alone ($0.18$--$0.52$). What replaced them is not a fourth bound of
the same kind but a different object class: a boundary-decay statement about one explicit vector.
T109 then killed the natural fourth candidate as well: \textbf{the Combes--Thomas resolvent bound at
the boundary}, evaluated with the \emph{exact} Green row so that no constant can rescue it, closes
$1/16$ --- the mechanism at the edge is cancellation ($44$--$4.6\times10^{4}$ on $15/16$ zones), which
no absolute-value bound survives. What works instead is the residual certificate that carries the
cancellation (Section~\ref{sec:certification}).
\item \textbf{The scalar step law for the margin} (T110). Killed structurally, in three pieces:
bordered Weyl closes $0/15$ handovers; the Schur/Friedrichs scalar cap is vacuous on $15/15$ (its
scalar-only bound degrades back to Weyl); and the strictly scalar graded minorant
($n_{\mathrm{soft}}=\dim$) closes $0/15$, with the strictly scalar \emph{induction} compounding to a
Schur factor of $2.7\times10^{-36}$ by $k=3$. The reason is a boundary layer: the new cells of a
grown window attach at the edge where the pole source sits, so a one-number summary of the bordering
is blind to the deciding direction. What survives is the graded ($n_{\mathrm{soft}}=1$) Loewner
minorant --- one soft direction is exactly the amount of geometry the step needs.
\item \textbf{Two earlier structural kills}, recorded here because they still bound the design space:
the cross-block $\sqrt{\lambda_0}$ law is Douglas range inclusion and therefore circular (T98), and the
cone-library route saturates at $5/24$ coverage and is dead as a covering strategy (T72, promoted into
\vref{v540} as a named limit). Likewise the seam route to the archimedean place (T20--T25) and the
Kohnen-plus route to the central values (T44, promoted into \vref{v537} as a scope fence).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent The finale re-counted this list against the whole certification arc and made it
quantitative: \textbf{$24$ refuted routes across $18$ parts}, every one of them structural --- an
identity (the T123 ceiling, the T124 primal direction), an exact sign ($\lambda_{\min}(E_c)<0$, the
negative norm surrogate, the non-dyadic gap ratios), or a measurement that tuning cannot move (the
vacuous band split, the empty energy route, the cancelling solution-free rung). The counting matters
because \emph{four} of the $24$ died of the \emph{same} cause --- a bound that needs the coarse form
from \emph{below} --- which is why the exit was a direction reversal and not a sharper estimate.
T125 added one entry of its own: a certified $\varepsilon$ bound at the \emph{fine} level of a chain
is the CBS wall again, which is why V-final mounts the target window as the \emph{coarsest} level.

% =====================================================================
\section{The series balance (T102--T125)}\label{sec:balance}
% =====================================================================
\noindent The finale's second half is bookkeeping about the series itself: what the hard core was at
each stage, how much of the final chain is a certificate rather than a hypothesis, and how far that
chain is from any infinite statement. All three are measurements against the same source, and they
are reproduced here because they are the part a later reader can check without re-running anything.

\subsection{The reduction cascade, seven stations}
Each arrow is a strict reduction in dimension or in kind, and every one of them was paid for by a
refutation in the list above:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.9em,label=\textbf{\arabic*.}]
\item \textbf{Matrix wall} (T104--T106) --- a Loewner statement $M\succeq m\,\mathrm{Id}$ on $M/2$
dimensions, killed as a margin route and localised by the parity split to one statement (R) in the
odd channel.
\item \textbf{Scalar} (T107--T108) --- Sherman--Morrison/Woodbury reduce (R) exactly to one scalar
ratio $r=\kappa/\varepsilon\le1$, and $\varepsilon$ itself becomes an identity (the square of the
last Cholesky pivot).
\item \textbf{Ratio} (T108--T109) --- the ratio splits into $\omega<1$, certified unconditionally by
a graded matrix cap, and one statement at an explicit vector.
\item \textbf{Boundary value} (T109) --- what is left is literally one boundary value, carried by a
residue certificate that \emph{keeps} the cancellation instead of estimating it away.
\item \textbf{One inequality} (T110--T117) --- the frame removes the ladder and $\omega$ walls, the
margin wall turns out to measure the discretisation, the margin-free Albert step removes the
division that was the wall; what remains is one lower bound on $\varepsilon$.
\item \textbf{Harnack pair $+$ one sign} (T118--T124) --- the $\varepsilon$ bound becomes an identity
plus a supply, and the supply needs either the corner route ((F1)$+$(F2)) or the telescope route
((8R), whose only requirement is the \emph{sign} of $A_c$).
\item \textbf{One sign, one convention} (T125) --- assembled: the spine [A][B][C][E] needs (H-base),
a sign on \emph{one} finite matrix, plus the (C-F5) accounting convention. \textbf{The Harnack pair
survives as the second, independent route (iv) and is no longer a bottleneck of the spine} --- this
last station is what T125 added.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The certification degree}
Every link of V-final counted once, classified by what actually establishes it:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrr@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Class} & \textbf{Links} & \textbf{\%} & \textbf{Spine} & \textbf{\%}\\
\midrule
\textsc{identity} & $14$ & $45.2$ & $11$ & $42.3$\\
\textsc{cholesky-cert} & $14$ & $45.2$ & $14$ & $53.8$\\
\textsc{window-cert} & $2$ & $6.5$ & $0$ & $0.0$\\
\textsc{hypothesis} & $1$ & $3.2$ & $1$ & $3.8$\\
\midrule
\textbf{Total} & $31$ & $100.0$ & $26$ & $100.0$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent $90.3\%$ of the whole chain is identity or completed Cholesky; on the load-bearing spine it
is $\mathbf{96.2\%}$, with \emph{zero} window certificates and exactly one hypothesis --- the
(C-F5) accounting convention. The three window certificates are confined to conclusion (iv).

\subsection{The honest RH distance, in six checkable statements}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2.4em,label=\textbf{(R\arabic*)}]
\item \textbf{No zeta input.} The chain consumes exactly two arithmetic facts, both classical and
both only for \emph{admissibility} of the frame: $g_k\le\log2$ (Bertrand--Chebyshev 1852) and
$g_k\ge\log(1+1/n)$. No zeta function, no zero data, no zero-derived quantity --- the probe's own AST
firewall rejects the tokens, the imports and write-mode file access in its source.
\item \textbf{RH is not used, in either direction.} ``RH $\Rightarrow$ window Weil positivity''
appears nowhere; (H-base) is a completed Cholesky of one explicit matrix of size $h=416$--$1384$.
\item \textbf{No infinite statement.} V-final quantifies over a \emph{finite} run of consecutive
atoms whose length is bounded by $\min_kg_k=0.027780$: here $30$ zones, $n=2\ldots79$.
\item \textbf{A measurement ladder is not all zones.} $62$ of $85$ admissible handovers in the table,
and $48$ of $34\,034$ prime-power atoms up to $400\,000$ touched at all. Everything outside is
untested, not implied.
\item \textbf{The open items are uniformity, not size.} What is missing for any infinite statement is
a uniform-in-$k$ version of stage [E]'s $g_c\times g_c$ Schur complement, a uniform lower bound on
$\mathrm{cb}_\ell$, a proof rather than a measurement of (H-F1)/(H-F2) --- classical addresses named
(Widom 1974 / Fisher--Hartwig for the corner regularity of the inverse, Ostrowski 1937 / Varga 1962
for the sign) --- and removal of the (C-F5) convention, which T124 measured to be a cancellation.
\item \textbf{What it is.} A machine-certified, finite, multilevel \emph{lower} bound on the Galerkin
prediction defect of a log-singular Toeplitz form with a prime-power comb, together with a
margin-free bordering induction that transports its sign along a nested window chain. That is a
statement in numerical analysis. It is not a statement about zeros.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent The defect counter closes accordingly: (F1)/(F2) window-certified and now \emph{outside}
the spine, (F3) closed in T123, (F4) collapsed to a sign in T124, (F5) declared as (C-F5) --- and one
new entry, \textbf{(F6) uniformity}, the only thing between V-final and an infinite statement.

% =====================================================================
\section{Phase 2: toward uniformity (T126--T155)}\label{sec:phase2}
% =====================================================================
\noindent The series closed at T125 with a mandate fulfilled and a distance named: what separates
V-final from any infinite statement is \emph{uniformity in the zone index}, not size. The first
post-series part --- T126 (\texttt{uniformity\_seams\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{UNIFORMITY.SEAMS},
$31/31$, verdict \textsc{seams-certified}) --- opens a second phase of the diary by attacking exactly
that gap in its three components: the \emph{segment seams} (consecutive runs carry different common
resolutions, and the seam between them is one re-gridding), the \emph{uniformity of the spine
constants} (the (F6) entry of V-final, audited one constant at a time), and the \emph{continuum
argument} (finite-section positivity at every resolution $\Rightarrow$ positivity of the continuum
window form on a test class). All three move; what remains at the end is stated in
Section~\ref{sec:phase2map} and is deliberately short.

\subsection{The seam architecture: the partition question dissolves}
The natural first question is arithmetic: can the prime-power axis be cut into segments such that
each segment carries one common resolution $D_{\mathrm{seg}}\propto\min$-gap and neighbouring
segments have a resolution ratio the certified transport reaches? Since $D_{\mathrm{seg}}$ is set by
the segment's minimum log-gap, this is a question about \emph{minima of windows of the log-gap
sequence} and nothing else --- so it is decided \emph{exactly}, by a dynamic programme over cut
positions on the real gap table ($9\,700$ zones, $n\le10^{5}$; $33$ DP runs in $2.71$\,s). The answer
is a refutation with a replacement:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
\item \textbf{At T115's synthetic threshold $\rho^*=1.83$ no partition exists.} The DP dies at zone
$n=127$, at the local gap pattern $[0.0684,\,0.0325,\,0.0159,\,0.0078,\,0.0232,\,0.0448]$ --- a small
gap directly behind a large one with no intermediate gap in the admissible window: the
twin-after-a-large-gap mechanism, named exactly. The true requirement is
$\boldsymbol{\rho_{\mathrm{crit}}=2.0258}$, identical on $n\le10^{4}$ and $n\le10^{5}$ --- not an
aggregate but essentially \emph{one} quotient of neighbouring gaps, $g(125)/g(127)=2.0238$,
reproducing $\rho_{\mathrm{crit}}$ to $2.0\times10^{-3}$. A partition at $\rho_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is
\emph{constructed} ($306$ segments; the T112 frame lemmas hold on all $9\,700$ zone steps, $\min
\nu_{\mathrm{eff}}=4.00$, zero violations).
\item \textbf{A refinement-only partition exists at no ratio $\le64$}, so the tight-cap partition
forces \emph{coarsening} seams --- and coarsening fails for real: on real pairs the transported
certificate fails at ratios $0.309$ and $0.531$ while the target floor itself stays positive on
$9/9$ (what fails is the certificate, not the positivity), and ladders of intermediate resolutions
make it \emph{worse} ($k=1,2,3$ at $n=47$: $-2.54\times10^{-2}\to-2.52\times10^{-2}\to
-4.63\times10^{-2}$) --- the obstruction is the \emph{destination grid} on which every ladder must
land; a finer subdivision cannot cure a bad target.
\item \textbf{The decisive new measurement:} holding the worst-case ratio $2.0256$ \emph{fixed} and
sweeping the zone, the transported certificate survives on only $12$ of $22$ zones. The seam dies at
the \emph{zone}, not at the ratio --- so no ratio threshold can ever close the programme, and the
lemma the seam architecture needs is not ``a larger $\rho$'' but a \emph{zone-uniform} $\eta/\tau$
bound at ratio $\le2.026$ (U5 below, sharpened).
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textbf{What carries is the free-resolution route.} Releasing the resolution from the
per-segment cap and taking the running minimum $D_k=\min(\mathrm{cap}_k,D_{k-1})$ makes monotonicity
hold \emph{by construction} --- no DP, no gap theorem, not one coarsening seam anywhere. The largest
single refinement drop over $n\le10^{5}$ is $2.0256$ (zone $n=8191$ --- the same binding quotient,
now read in the good direction), and re-gridding happens at only $\mathbf{764}$ of $9\,700$
boundaries ($7.88\%$): a drop needs a \emph{record-small} gap, and every other boundary is a no-op at
ratio $1$. All $12$ affordable actual seams of that route ($n=3\ldots125$, $h\le1222$, largest real
ratio $1.943$) are built and transported: $11$ carry a positive certified floor in \emph{one}
re-gridding, the one at $n=31$ (transported mass $\tau=0.363$, unusually poor) closes through a
three-stage ladder with composed floor $3.56\times10^{-2}>0$; median retention $0.578$, all $12$
brackets valid. The seam device itself is sound throughout: the Haynsworth partial minimisation
reproduces $\lambda_{\min}(S)$ to $2.8\times10^{-14}$ on both grids and the transport bracket
contains the fine value on $30/30$ real pairs; and a \emph{full} seam is certified end to end at the
ratio the arithmetic demands --- spine on grid A, one re-gridding (lower bracket end
$8.671\times10^{-3}>0$, retention $0.103$), spine on grid B --- at $\rho=2.251\ge2.0256$ on $3$ of
$4$ attempts (the failures are the zone dependence above, printed and not hidden). The price of the
route is honest and structural: the resolution overhead $D_{\mathrm{free}}/\mathrm{cap}$ has median
$0.201$, i.e.\ the \emph{running minimum} of the gaps sets the window cost instead of the local gap
--- window size, not an assumption; certified seam by seam the route covers $n\le125$, an evidence
limit rather than a gap in the argument.

\subsection{The uniformity audit (U1--U6)}
Every constant of the load-bearing spine is measured over zones \emph{and} telescope depth ($35$
rungs, $14$ zones, (8R) valid on $35/35$ while audited; every fit jackknifed; the status rule
declared before the numbers, and ``measured-flat'' is never upgraded to ``uniform''):

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}lY l@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Constant} & \textbf{Finding} & \textbf{Status}\\
\midrule
U1 envelope margin & forced by the construction's own doubling loop & self-certifying\\
U2 oversampling depth $L/M$ & needs one $\alpha$-uniform Bernstein/Markov bound & classical in form\\
U3 rung quality $\mathrm{cb}/\delta$ & measured-flat, but the reason is \emph{direction}, not
spectrum: the worst pencil direction of $(S,U)$ does not explain the flatness (alignment
$0.866$--$0.987$, median $0.975$; $\mu_{\min}$ itself falls with the window) & genuinely new\\
U4 base-case head-room & a compute wall over the declared fp floor & no proof obligation\\
U5 Albert--Schur floor & $\lambda_{\min}(S)/\|A\|$ \emph{falls} in the window direction --- the one
load-bearing gap; sharpened by the seam scan to a zone-uniform $\eta/\tau$ bound at ratio
$\le2.026$ & genuinely new\\
U6 resolution monotonicity & the running minimum is monotone by construction & resolved\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\noindent The audit's own finding is that (F6) is not one lemma but three --- U2 (classical in
form), U3 (a direction lemma on $\hat s$), U5 (a zone-uniform Schur floor) --- of which exactly two
are genuinely new mathematics.

\subsection{The continuum step is a fractional Dirichlet energy}
The chain one would write down first --- density of piecewise-constant functions plus a continuity
constant --- is \emph{refuted}: the archimedean kernel is Cauchy-type ${\sim}1/(2|s|)$, not
log-type, hence not locally integrable; its $L^1$ mass over the window and $\lambda_{\max}$ both
diverge like $\log(1/D)$, so neither an $L^2$ nor a sup-norm continuity constant exists (measured).
What makes the divergence harmless is an \emph{identity}: with row sums $w_r$,
\[
v^{\mathsf T}Av \;=\; \sum_r w_r v_r^2 \;+\; \sum_{r<s}(-A_{rs})\,(v_r-v_s)^2 ,
\]
with $86.1\%$ of the off-diagonal entries measurably negative and the row sums bounded --- the
window form \emph{is} a fractional Dirichlet energy, and the divergent diagonal is the
$\tfrac12$-energy of a step function, a property of the test class rather than a defect of the
kernel. The limit argument is therefore quantitative, and it is measured: on the dyadic tower
($5$ levels, $h=63\ldots1008$, a $16\times$ spread in $D$) the Galerkin values converge at order
$D^{1.798\pm0.057}$ for a $C^\infty$ bump and $D^{1.802\pm0.065}$ for a Lipschitz hat ---
superlinear in both cases, exactly as the energy identity predicts --- with every level strictly
positive and Richardson limit $2.1256\times10^{-4}>0$. What is classical here is the consistency
estimate for a Cauchy-kernel Galerkin scheme (Calder\'on--Zygmund; Bramble--Hilbert 1970); what is
measured is its rate on this kernel; the abstract density step is not needed and not used. The
statement that would stand at the end is \textbf{positivity of the Weil window form on Lipschitz
test functions supported in the reached window} ($\alpha=1.5046$ on the tower, $1.6882$ along the
seam chain) --- a finite-window statement whose bridge to RH is Weil's criterion (Weil 1952;
Bombieri 2000), cited here and used in neither direction.

\subsection{The full-proof map: sixteen points, two genuinely new}\label{sec:phase2map}
The definitive fail list after the three attacks has $16$ points: $5$ closed or classical, $9$
conditional (constructed here, classical in form, or a machine limit), and exactly \emph{two}
genuinely new pieces of mathematics:

\begin{keybox}[The two remaining inequalities (T126)]
\textbf{(U3) The direction lemma.} Why the rung's data residual $\hat s$ sits in the good subspace
of the pencil $(S,U)$: the measured flatness of $\mathrm{cb}/\delta$ is provably \emph{not} the
pencil's worst-case behaviour (alignment $0.975$ while $\mu_{\min}$ falls), so a proof must control
the \emph{direction} of $\hat s$, not the spectrum.\\[3pt]
\textbf{(U5) The zone-uniform seam floor.} A zone-uniform $\eta/\tau$ bound for the re-gridding
transport at ratio $\le2.026$ --- the constant every seam consumes. Ladders provably cannot repair a
bad destination grid, so the reach must be won per \emph{zone}, not per ratio.
\end{keybox}

\noindent S4, the coarsening bracket --- which this part opened and which would have been a third
genuinely new statement --- is \emph{dissolved} by the free-resolution route rather than proved.
Still open and unchanged: the (C-F5) accounting convention, and the all-$\alpha$ step --- the one
thing no computation can close (at the tight cap, $6$ boundaries in $9\,700$ would need a double
seam; for all $n$ one needs a gap theorem, or the free-resolution relaxation, which is unconditional
in the arithmetic). T127 (\texttt{two\_inequalities\_probe.py}, contract
\texttt{TWO.INEQUALITIES}, $28/28$, verdict \textsc{both-shaped}) then took both inequalities
head-on. The result --- both come back proof-shaped, and \emph{neither is the statement T126
named} --- is the remainder of this section.

\subsection{The separating variable is geometric, not arithmetic (T127)}
The fixed-ratio sweep reproduces exactly: at the worst-case ratio $2.0256$ the transported
certificate survives on $12$ of $22$ zones, T126's list --- and what dies is the
\emph{certificate}, not the positivity: the true target eigenvalue $\lambda_{\min}(S_f)$ is
positive on all $22$ zones. Of $29$ candidate separating variables, exactly $5$ separate the
surviving from the dying zones perfectly ($22/22$; the rank statistic is a description of a
$22$-point sample and is never called a test), and all five measure the same \emph{geometric}
quantity: the right-hand protrusion $s_{l,r}$ --- the two grids quantise the same window
independently, their border blocks have equal width but sit offset by one rounding, and the
transported mass $\tau_{\mathrm{dn}}$ loses exactly what protrudes past the coarse block's inner
edge (correlation $+0.997$ with $\tau_{\mathrm{dn}}$). Every arithmetic candidate fails (gap,
$\alpha$, atom weight, alias phase; best separation $0.727$) --- the separation is \emph{not} the
atom and \emph{not} the gap. Deep arithmetic ($n\le300\,000$, $1\,082$ record seams): the fine
border block has $4$ cells everywhere, $97.60\%$ of real seams have ratio $\le1.05$ with median
$1.00140$ --- real seams are \emph{near-identity re-griddings} --- and the $11$ seams with ratio
$>1.2$ \emph{all} have next-atom distance $\le2$: Mersenne ($3,7,31,127,8191$), Fermat-like
($4,16,256,65536,131071$), one twin ($101,103$). Mih\u{a}ilescu 2004 (Catalan) is cited as the
classical address of that list and not used.

\subsection{U5 as posed is refuted; the U5 band replaces it}
As a zone-uniform certificate at ratio $\le2.026$, U5 is \emph{dead as posed}. The replacement is
\textbf{U5-band}: all $22$ zones survive for every ratio $\rho\le1.49531$, and at least one dies
for every $\rho\ge1.49766$ (bisected to $2.3\times10^{-3}$; binding zone $n=19$) --- a band that
covers $99.26\%$ of the route's real seams, the remainder being the $8$ enumerated pairs above.
