The split of the form defect is an \emph{identity} ($\eta=\eta_{\mathrm{cons}}+\eta_{\mathrm{proj}}$,
exact), and $\eta$ does \emph{not} become small as $\rho\to1$ (max $|\eta|/\lambda=1.146$ at the
\emph{tightest} ratio): the band buys $\tau_{\mathrm{dn}}\approx1$, not small $\eta$. The floor is
measured at $c\ge0.0090$ over $307$ in-band transports with a positive single-step floor up to
$n=1331$, $h=1496$. \textbf{The sharpest new finding:} the near-identity census ($43$ deep zones,
all at ratio $1.00140$) fails \emph{once} --- $n=479$, $|\eta|/\lambda=1.472$, within $0.14\%$ of
the identity --- so the ratio band is only a \emph{proxy} for the border geometry; the failure is
repaired by a three-stage ladder (retention $0.5027$), exactly as T126 repaired $n=31$ --- the
fallback is part of the statement. And the risk is recognisable \emph{before} any factorisation:
the indicator $\tau_{\mathrm{dn}}/\tau_{\mathrm{flat}}$ ranks the margin at Spearman $+0.749$, and
the one failure is its argmax ($\tau_{\mathrm{flat}}$ is a predictor, not a bound).

\subsection{U3 defuses to a coarse floor: the harmonic-mean identity}
Three identities restructure U3. The pole part of the rung data has the closed form
$(Z^{\mathsf T}b)_j=-\tfrac{16}{\sqrt{2D}}\sinh^2(D/4)\cosh(\bar x^{\mathrm c}_j/2)$ (to
$3.2\times10^{-13}$); the rung quality is a \emph{harmonic mean},
$\mathrm{cb}/\delta=1/\sum_i w_i/\mu_i$ (to $1.5\times10^{-7}$); and the weights split by Parseval
(to $6.1\times10^{-16}$). The residual's mass on the good half of the pencil spectrum
$\{\mu\ge\tfrac12\}$ is $0.9665\ldots0.99998$ over $42$ rungs (flat), so
$\mathrm{cb}/\delta\ge1/(2m+(1-m)/\mu_{\min})$ --- a floor as \emph{crude} as
$\mu_{\min}\ge1.62\times10^{-2}$ (one part in $62$) already gives $\mathrm{cb}/\delta\ge\tfrac14$,
where T126 had to measure $0.192$--$0.307$ sharply. The reading ``$\hat s$ avoids the bad
direction'' is \emph{refuted}: the flatness is generic --- every smooth, flat, alternating or
quasi-random direction has good-band mass above $0.50$ --- and exactly \emph{one} direction type
fails, a \emph{boundary layer} at the window edge (concentration $3.1\times$ there, against
$0.03\times$ for $\hat s$ and $0.001\times$ for its closed-form pole part). The bad pencil
directions are \emph{not} edge-localised in the $\ell_2$ sense ($1.23\times$) --- only the
$U$-metric concentration separates.

\subsection{The map V2: three inequalities and one enumeration}
The map V2 has $16$ points: $10$ carried, $2$ conditional-classical (one Bernstein/Markov bound,
the declared fp floor), and the $2$ genuinely new ones of T126 \emph{reformulated}:

\begin{keybox}[The remaining mathematics (T127, map V2)]
\textbf{(T) The border-retention bound.} A lower bound for the transported mass
$\tau_{\mathrm{dn}}$ under the edge-geometry hypothesis --- from predictor to bound, via the
support of the $S_f$ eigenvector; the ratio band $\rho\le1.50$ is only a proxy for it.\\[3pt]
\textbf{(E) The $|\eta|$ upper bound.} Both parts of the form defect
$\eta=\eta_{\mathrm{cons}}+\eta_{\mathrm{proj}}$, to be bounded \emph{jointly} with (T): the two
rank together at $+0.749$.\\[3pt]
\textbf{(M) The pencil-mass bound.} The residual's good-band mass plus the crude floor
$\mu_{\min}\ge1.62\times10^{-2}$; the one direction type to exclude is named (a boundary layer at
the window edge), and the closed-form pole part provably is none.\\[3pt]
\textbf{(L) The enumeration.} The $8$ out-of-band seams up to $n=300\,000$ (Mersenne/Fermat pairs
and one twin), a finite list; where a single step fails, a three-stage ladder composes --- the
fallback is part of the statement.
\end{keybox}

\noindent The craft judgement, labelled as one: the three remaining inequalities are of the
\emph{same craft} as the proven links of the chain --- finite-dimensional inequalities between
constructed matrices --- and the residual risk is concentrated in the word \emph{uniform}, which
no amount of this probe's arithmetic can discharge. T128 (\texttt{teml\_probe.py}, contract
\texttt{TEML}, $27/27$, verdict \textsc{three-of-four}) then worked that four-point list in order
of cost, cheapest first. Three of the four points stand at their preregistered bars; the fourth
--- the protrusion concentration inside (T) --- missed its own bar by $3.6\%$, systematically in
the re-gridding ratio, which is exactly the kind of near-miss that distinguishes a bound from a
fit. The remainder of this section is the four points in order.

\subsection{(L) is derived and closed: the out-of-band table (T128)}
The out-of-band class is no longer quoted but \emph{derived}: applying the U5-band threshold
$\rho>1.49531$ (T127's number, quoted and never re-bisected) to the free-resolution record
schedule yields \emph{exactly eight} seams up to $n=300\,000$, all at next-atom distance $1$ and
all with identical border geometry ($\mathrm{gc}_c=2$ coarse border cells, $\mathrm{gc}_f=4$ fine)
--- each is a pair $(2^k{-}1,2^k)$ or $(2^k,2^k{+}1)$ whose log-gap halves the previous record.
Every seam carries a status and no residue:

\noindent The five open seams exceed the hard factorisation cap $h\le1500$ by $1.7\times$ to
$4119\times$, and each is open for \emph{one} reason only: a single Cholesky of size
$h_f-\mathrm{gc}_f$ is missing --- nothing conceptual. Each is characterised arithmetically and
geometrically without any factorisation (ratio, both border cell counts, both window roundings,
the flat transport factor), so the characterisation is available at any depth. ``Closed list''
means every item is enumerated and labelled, not that every item carries a Cholesky --- that
distinction is the point of the block. Mih\u{a}ilescu 2004 (Catalan) remains the cited, unused
address of the class; over a finite range a list is enumerated, not conjectured.

\subsection{(T) becomes a bound, and its bar is missed honestly}
The step from predictor to bound is an exact identity plus two explicit majorants. With $y$ the
transported $S_f$ eigenvector, $m_{\mathrm{prot}}$ its mass on the protrusion,
$m_{\mathrm{fill}}\ge0$ the interior mass on the sliver the coarse block adds, and
$V_{\mathrm{bord}}\ge0$ the intra-coarse-cell variance of the transported function,
\[
\tau_{\mathrm{dn}} \;=\; \|y\|^2 - m_{\mathrm{prot}} + m_{\mathrm{fill}} - V_{\mathrm{bord}},
\qquad\text{hence}\qquad
\tau_{\mathrm{dn}} \;\ge\; 1 - m_{\mathrm{prot}} - V_{\mathrm{bord}},
\]
verified to $9.0\times10^{-13}$ against an independent full projection on all $126$ transports.
The bound is valid on $126/126$ (slack $=m_{\mathrm{fill}}$, median $0.000$); with the two
explicit majorants --- the outer-cell majorant for $m_{\mathrm{prot}}$ and the discrete
oscillation majorant $\rho(\lceil\rho\rceil{+}1)/2\cdot\sum(\Delta y)^2$ for $V_{\mathrm{bord}}$
--- it stays valid on $126/126$ and positive on $125/126$ (the exact form is positive on
$126/126$); total slack median $0.275$, and the loose part is the oscillation majorant (median
$7\times$ overshoot), so what (T) still needs is a Bernstein/Markov-type statement about the
discrete oscillation of one $\mathrm{gc}_f\times\mathrm{gc}_f$ eigenvector. \textbf{The honest
miss:} the protrusion concentration
$\kappa=m_{\mathrm{prot}}\,\mathrm{gc}_f/(t_l+t_r)$ --- eigenvector mass on the protrusion against
what a flat border vector would put there --- measures $0.086$--$2.071$ over the $120$ transports
with a nonzero protrusion, and \emph{misses the preregistered bar $\kappa\le2$ on $1/120$ by
$3.6\%$}. The excess is systematic in the ratio, not accidental: the group maxima rise
monotonically, $1.889/1.965/2.071$ at $\rho=1.001/1.250/1.495$, with the argmax at the band edge.
The bar is \emph{not} moved --- a bar moved after the numbers would make everything else here
worthless; the shape the next preregistration should declare is $\kappa\le2+C(\rho-1)$, the shape
the U5 band already has. A side finding with a side: $\kappa$ is $1.61$--$2.07$ when the
protrusion sits at the \emph{inner} edge of the border block and $0.09$--$0.25$ at the window
edge --- (T) is an \emph{eigenvector} statement, not a rounding statement, contrary to what the
$+0.997$ correlation invites one to think.

\subsection{(E) and (T) jointly: the $\ell_2$ route dies, the energy route carries}
The $\ell_2$ route to $\eta_{\mathrm{proj}}$ is structurally lossy where it matters: the norm
bound overshoots the true defect by a median factor of $55$, and since the available margin
relative to $\lambda_c$ is smaller still, it certifies $0$ of $12$ real seams. What carries is
the \emph{energy route}, an exact Cauchy--Schwarz chain in the $Q$-inner product: with $Q_f$
certified PSD and $d=w_f-Pv$ the projection residual,
\[
\sqrt{\lambda_f} \;\ge\; \sqrt{\lambda_c\,\tau^* - |\eta_{\mathrm{cons}}|} \;-\; \sqrt{e_d},
\qquad e_d = d^{\mathsf T}Q_f d,
\]
with $e_d/\lambda_c=0.041$--$0.154$ on the real seams. The joint chain certifies a positive floor
on \textbf{$9/9$ in-band seams} (floor $\ge0.0850$) and $11/12$ overall. The one tear --- $n=31$,
already on the (L) list --- is \emph{$\eta$-limited}, not geometric:
$|\eta_{\mathrm{cons}}|/\lambda_c=0.392$ exceeds the whole geometric floor $\tau^*=0.363$, so no
sharper (T) would have helped; and shrinking the ratio is no alternative either, since the
consistency defect does not vanish as $\rho\to1$ (median $0.089$ at $\rho=1.0014$ over $43$
zones). The tear is repaired by the declared two-rung ladder in which \emph{every rung closes the
chain by itself} --- the fallback is part of the statement, not a patch.

\subsection{(M) is earned: the exclusion is a proof, the floor is constructed}
Three parts. \emph{(a)} The boundary-layer exclusion for the pole part of $\hat s$ is now a
\emph{proof} from the closed form
$(Z^{\mathsf T}b)_j=-\tfrac{16}{\sqrt{2D}}\sinh^2(D/4)\cosh(\bar x^{\mathrm c}_j/2)$: the
adjacent-component ratio lies in $[e^{-D/2},1]$ and the outer-$k$-cell share is bounded by
$kD(1+\cosh\alpha)/\sinh\alpha$ --- pole edge mass $\Theta(D)$ against a layer's $\Theta(1)$,
separation at least $84\times$ on all $32$ $(\alpha,D)$ pairs, by elementary inequalities rather
than a finite sample. \emph{(b)} The crude floor is \emph{earned}: $\mu_{\min}(S,A_z)=1-\gamma^2$
exactly (to $5.9\times10^{-15}$; the strengthened Cauchy--Schwarz constant of the two-grid
splitting, Eijkhout--Vassilevski 1991), and $U\preceq(1+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{env}})A_z$ with
$\varepsilon_{\mathrm{env}}\le0.038$ Cholesky-certified, so the constructed floor is a true lower
bound on all $31$ rungs: $\mathbf{0.188}$ against the $1.62\times10^{-2}$ the harmonic-mean
argument demands --- an $11.6\times$ margin, where T127 could only measure. \emph{(c)} The comb
residual stays measured, but it is measurably \emph{no} layer: half of its mass needs
$12$--$39\%$ of the half-window against one cell for a genuine boundary layer, and it vanishes
with the cell width as $D^{+1.54\pm0.09}$ (a fit with a jackknife band).

\subsection{The map V3: twenty-one points, five genuinely open}
\begin{keybox}[The map V3 (T128): what is left, by kind]
$21$ points: $\mathbf{10}$ \textbf{done} (T127's map still had the (M) floor, the (T) identity
and the (L) list among its open items; all three are now closed), $\mathbf{3}$
\textbf{certified on real objects} --- valid on every seam, transport and rung the hard cap
admits, wanting only the word ``uniformly'' --- and $\mathbf{3}$ \textbf{conditional-classical}
(Bernstein/Markov for the border oscillation, Bramble--Hilbert for the consistency defect and the
$Q$-invisible residual, and the continuum leg). $\mathbf{5}$ \textbf{genuinely open}, and no two
of the same kind:\\[3pt]
\textbf{M9} the $\kappa$ bar --- missed by $3.6\%$, systematically in $\rho$; next hypothesis
$\kappa\le2+C(\rho-1)$.\\
\textbf{M17} the pencil mass of $\hat s$ on $\{\mu\ge\tfrac12\}$ --- one measured scalar per
rung, no derivation.\\
\textbf{M18} the comb residual is not a cell-scale layer --- measured, not yet bounded.\\
\textbf{M19} uniformity over \emph{all} zones --- the word ``for all'', which a finite probe
structurally cannot supply.\\
\textbf{M21} the RH address itself --- Weil positivity as the address of the limit statement,
cited and never used.
\end{keybox}

\noindent The honest distance, in the probe's own three sentences, quoted:

\begin{quote}\itshape
(1) After this block the finite-window chain has no step left that is merely plausible: every
inequality it uses is either a completed certificate, an identity verified to machine precision,
or an explicit inequality that has been evaluated --- and found valid --- on every seam,
transport and rung the hard cap $h\le1500$ admits, so the remaining mathematical content is three
named classical estimates (Bernstein/Markov on one small Schur complement, Bramble--Hilbert
twice) plus two measured scalars (the pencil mass, the comb's delocalisation). (2) The distance
is nevertheless not small, and it is not small in a way no larger computation can shrink: $5$ of
the $21$ map items are open because a finite list of objects cannot produce the word ``for all
zones'', and one of them --- the protrusion concentration $\kappa$ --- has just been measured
outside its own preregistered bar by $3.6$ per cent, systematically in the re-gridding ratio
rather than accidentally, which is exactly the kind of near-miss that distinguishes a bound from
a fit. (3) And even with all four TEML points closed, what would stand is a positivity statement
about a finite window at the $\alpha$ actually reached: the continuum leg (M20) and the passage
to Weil's criterion (M21) are untouched by anything in this probe, so the honest distance to RH
is not ``one uniformity lemma'' but ``one uniformity lemma, three classical estimates, a
continuum limit, and a theorem nobody has''.
\end{quote}

\noindent T129 (\texttt{kappa\_deep\_seams\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{KAPPA.DEEP.SEAMS},
$28/28$, verdict \textsc{kappa-wild}) then did what the map demanded: it froze the constant of
the $\kappa$ law, tested the law on fresh transports, brought the two affordable deep seams
under the cap, and bounded the comb. The verdict is the most productive break of the phase ---
\emph{the fitted law falls, and the theorem underneath it stands}. The remainder of this
section is that part.

\subsection{The honest break: the $\kappa$ law falls once, and the theorem underneath stands (T129)}
The constant was frozen \emph{before} the test: $C^*=0.3506$, the least-squares slope of the
T128 group maxima against $\rho-1$ over the three calibration groups (jackknife SE $0.0955$,
intercept $1.8909$, rms $0.0096$) --- a fit, labelled a fit, not rounded up, not padded, not
refitted after the test; on the calibration set itself the law is violated $0/105$, reported
for completeness because it is \emph{not} the test. On \textbf{331 new transports} at $14$
ratios none of which is in the calibration set ($\rho=1.05\ldots4.00$, $h_f=29\ldots1450$,
$n=5\ldots1331$), the frozen law $\kappa\le2+C^*(\rho-1)$ is violated \textbf{once} ($n=7$ at
$\rho=3.00$, slack $-0.0334$), where the bare unmoved bar $\kappa\le2$ would be violated $78$
times; the sharper per-side law on $\kappa_r$ alone is violated $1/293$; and the T128 side
asymmetry reproduces on entirely new data ($\kappa_r=1.697$--$2.913$ against
$\kappa_l=0.054$--$0.205$). The bar is not moved --- hence the verdict.

What makes the break productive is that the \emph{structural ground} of $\kappa$ is now
solved, with identities rather than fits. Three pieces, in the order they stack. \emph{(a)}
The interval identity $\sum_i(1-g_i)=t_l+t_r$ (verified to $7.1\times10^{-13}$ on all $105$
calibration transports) makes $\kappa$ a \emph{probability average of the border density}: the
weights sum to $1$ exactly, so the \textbf{flat} border vector sits at $\kappa=1$
\emph{exactly} --- T128's bar $2$ was \emph{twice} the flat value, not the flat value.
\emph{(b)} The profile identity $p_{N-1}=2-p_0+2E$ (to $6.7\times10^{-16}$) makes a
\textbf{linear} density profile with vanishing edge give exactly $\kappa=2$: the bar $2$ is
the \emph{linear-density limit}, and everything above it \emph{is} the convexity of the border
density ($E>0$ on $219$ of $436$; Chebyshev's correlation inequality gives the sign).
\emph{(c)} The chain
\[
\kappa \;\le\; 2 - p_0 + \tfrac{N-2}{N}\sum_i\bigl|\Delta p_i-\overline{\Delta p}\bigr|
+ \bigl(p_{\max}-p_{N-1}\bigr)
\]
is a \emph{theorem with an explicit constant} --- H\"older for the mean, telescoping for the
profile identity, Abel summation for the majorant --- and it holds on \textbf{all 436
transports} (calibration plus test; slack $0.0184$--$2.3126$, median $0.3438$). On $392$ of
$436$ the density peaks at the inner end of the border block, the last correction vanishes,
and the bound is the clean form $2-p_0+\frac{N-2}{N}\,\mathrm{CURV}$. The $\rho$-systematics
T128 found is thereby \emph{named}: $2E>p_0 \iff p_{N-1}>2$ \emph{exactly}, confirmed
transport by transport ($114$ of $436$ satisfy both, no exception) --- a competition between
the convexity term and the edge value, not a one-sided effect; the majorised curvature grows
as $0.1445+0.1634(\rho-1)$ (a fit) while $p_0$ shrinks with $\rho$, and both pull the same
way. \textbf{The depth block:} $14$ graded transports at $h_f=1656\ldots\mathbf{17\,669}$ ---
up to $12\times$ the hard factorisation cap $1500$, reached matrix-free through the two-scale
assembly ($m/h=0.064$--$0.512$), $\rho=1.25$--$2.50$, $n=79$--$1019$ --- show \textbf{zero}
violations of the frozen law, the curvature chain holds on $14/14$ (slack $0.042$--$1.998$),
and $\kappa$ does not drift upward with depth: whatever residual risk remains is a
\emph{ratio} effect, not a resolution effect, which is the one thing a finite computation can
actually settle.

\subsection{The deep seams carry --- on the graded space, honestly downgraded}
The machinery first, verified against the uniform reference at $n=71$ ($h=619$): the
matrix-free two-scale assembly equals $J^{\mathsf T}QJ$ to $2.7\times10^{-16}$ (and at $q=1$
it equals the uniform form to $0.0$, so the graded path \emph{contains} the uniform path and
no separate code is trusted); the compressed step identity $X=Q_{\mathrm{old}}$ holds to
$2.4\times10^{-17}$, so the step stays margin-free (Albert 1969); and the graded interval
overlap reproduces the prolongation $J$ itself to $6.4\times10^{-14}$. Then the calibration,
printed \emph{before} the seams: over $48$ (zone,\,$q$) pairs at $12$ zone/ratio settings the
graded Schur floor is $1.534$--$5.181\times$ \emph{larger} than the uniform one (median
$2.646$) --- Rayleigh--Ritz, the wrong direction for a proof, with no exception --- and on
\textbf{4 of 48} pairs the graded floor is \emph{positive where the uniform floor is not}:
demonstrated \textbf{false positives} of the compression, a measured $8\%$ rate at depths
shallow enough that both sides can be computed. Both deep seams are then built and both fit:
$n=127$ and $256$ ($h_f=2476$, $5694$) compressed to $m_f=1256$, $1452$ ($m/h=0.507$, $0.255$;
$q_f=2$, $4$), every factorised block at or below the cap and the fine square matrix never
formed; \textbf{both carry a complete graded seam certificate} ($X$ positive definite by
Cholesky, both Schur floors above the Wilkinson floor, transport bracket positive), retentions
$0.3070/0.4165$; as a $\kappa$ test on \emph{genuine record seams} ($\rho=2.0238/2.0118$,
outside the calibration range) they give $0$ violations (chain slack $+0.079/+0.178$); and the
floor survives $6$ further coarsening rungs ($0.166$--$0.883$) --- measured evidence against a
one-merge artefact, which does \emph{not} repair the Rayleigh--Ritz direction. The honest
ledger, stated as the probe states it: $(L)=[\,3$ uniform-certified (T128), $2$
\textsc{graded-certified} here with the declared false-positive rate, $3$ budget-open$\,]$ ---
emphatically \emph{not} ``5 certified, 3 open''. The two deep seams moved from ``no
certificate at all'' to ``a complete certificate on a coarser Galerkin space, with the
direction of the error known and its size calibrated'' --- strictly more than T128 had and
strictly less than a uniform certificate.

\subsection{The comb is bounded: the operator majorant works, the row bound is vacuous}
Both candidate majorants for the comb's outer-$k$-cell share are majorants on all $40$
zone\,$\times$\,depth points ($5$ zones, depths $\times1/2/4$, $h=64$--$946$; the measured
share itself is $1.96\times10^{-3}$--$0.573$, median $0.038$). The unconditional
Cauchy--Schwarz row bound is \textbf{vacuous}: below the bar $1$ on $0$ of $40$, overshooting
the truth by a median factor of $116\,524$ --- reported as the failure it is, not as a
footnote. The \textbf{S-procedure operator bound works}: below $1$ on $40$ of $40$, running
$0.170$--$0.774$ and only $1.3$--$182\times$ above the truth (median $7.8$) --- a statement
about \emph{every} coarse vector in the measured smoothness cone, not merely the one that
happens to occur. Status \textsc{certified-conditional}: conditional on one measured cone
parameter with a known law ($\theta_1\sim D^{1.219}$, a fit with jackknife SE $0.088$ ---
exactly the scaling of a fixed smooth profile sampled at cell width $D$). The honest limit:
the bound \emph{saturates} at $(kD_c)^{0.263}$ (a fit) instead of decaying like the pole
part's closed-form $O(kD)$, because the cone still contains vectors far more edge-loaded than
the coarse solve that occurs.

\subsection{The map V4: two irreducibles, three new points, and the promotion assessment}
\begin{keybox}[The map V4 (T129): nine entries, and the two irreducibles named]
$\mathbf{3}$ \textbf{reduced or bounded by this probe}: \textbf{M9} the $\kappa$ bar ---
\emph{reduced}, not merely retested: $\kappa$ is now an identity plus a theorem, and what
remains open is a bound on the \emph{curvature} of the border density on $\lceil t\rceil$
cells, a strictly smaller object than the original mass hypothesis; \textbf{L2} the two
affordable deep seams --- \emph{graded}-certified with the direction of the error known and
its $8\%$ false-positive rate measured; \textbf{M18} the comb --- \emph{bounded} by the
conditional operator majorant. $\mathbf{1}$ \textbf{untouched}: \textbf{M17} the pencil mass
(T128's measured $0.9665$ and the constructed floor $0.188$ stand). $\mathbf{2}$
\textbf{irreducible here}: \textbf{M19} the word ``for all'' --- the curvature chain is a
theorem \emph{per transport}; making it a theorem \emph{for all} transports needs a
zone-uniform curvature bound no finite computation can supply; \textbf{M21} the RH address
itself --- Weil 1952 / Bombieri 2000 / Connes 1999 cited and used for nothing.
$\mathbf{3}$ \textbf{new, created and named rather than buried}: \textbf{M22} the fitted
constant $C^*$ (it stays a fit until the curvature term is bounded in closed form --- the same
open question in different clothes); \textbf{M23} the graded-to-uniform conversion (needs the
C\'ea/Strang defect $\|(I-\Pi)z^*\|^2$ of the fine partial minimiser; $Q$ has an FFT matvec,
so this is an \emph{arithmetic} obstruction with a known route, not a conceptual one);
\textbf{M24} the per-side law ($\kappa$ averages a large right protrusion with a small left
one, so T128's quantity is systematically milder than the mechanism it stands for).
\end{keybox}

\begin{keybox}[Promotion readiness (T129): ready in principle, as a narrow module]
The load-bearing content of this probe that would survive a \texttt{verification/vN\_*} gate
is exactly the part that is identity or theorem and needs no sampling: \emph{(1)} the interval
identity $\sum(1-g_i)=t_l+t_r$ and hence $\kappa_{\mathrm{flat}}=1$; \emph{(2)} T128's
four-term (T) identity; \emph{(3)} the profile identity $p_{N-1}=2-p_0+2E$; \emph{(4)} the
Abel majorant $\kappa\le2-p_0+\frac{N-2}{N}\,\mathrm{CURV}$; \emph{(5)} the assembly
identities (two-scale assembly $=J^{\mathsf T}QJ$, graded overlap reproduces $J$). Those five
are checkable at fixed small sizes in seconds and are true per instance. \textbf{Not ready}:
the constant $C^*$ (a fit), the graded seam floors (a weaker Galerkin statement with a
demonstrated false-positive rate), the comb-law exponent (a fit), and anything containing the
word ``uniform''. A module built only on (1)--(5) would be load-bearing and small; a module
that quoted $C^*$ or a graded floor as a certificate would not be, and the ledger would be
right to refuse it.
\end{keybox}

\noindent The honest distance, in the probe's own three sentences, quoted:

\begin{quote}\itshape
After K1 the retention bound (T) is no longer a hypothesis about eigenvector mass but a
theorem reducing it to the curvature of the border density on $\lceil t\rceil$ cells, with
$\kappa=1$ the flat limit, $\kappa=2$ the linear limit and an explicit Abel majorant for the
rest. After K2 the (L) list has no item left that is open for a conceptual reason: two of the
five deep seams are certified on a coarser space with the error direction known and its size
calibrated, and the other three need one Cholesky each at a size that is a pure arithmetic
fact. After K3 both parts of $\hat s$ --- the pole part in closed form and the comb part by a
certified majorant --- are excluded from being boundary layers, so (M)'s one remaining
direction type is gone; what is left is what was left before: the word ``for all'', and the
distance from any finite window to the criterion of Weil, which nothing in this probe touches.
\end{quote}

\noindent T130 (\texttt{curvature\_bridge\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{CURVATURE.BRIDGE},
$30/30$, verdict \textsc{one-of-two}) then attacked the two named open pieces T129 left ---
the curvature bound (M22/M24) and the graded-to-uniform bridge (M23) --- and exactly one of
the two stands: \emph{the bridge stands, the curvature bound does not}. The remainder of this
section is that part.

\subsection{The curvature term becomes a total variation --- and the frozen shape band breaks (T130)}
The structural gain of the curvature front is a new \emph{exact} rewrite of what the term is:
\[
\sum_j\bigl|\Delta p_j-\overline{\Delta p}\bigr| \;=\; \mathrm{TV}(P),\qquad
P_k \;=\; p_k-(p_0+k\,\overline{\Delta p}),\quad P_0=P_{N-1}=0 ,
\]
the total variation of the density profile's own \emph{chord deviation}, certified per
transport by $\mathrm{TV}(P)\le(2n_{\mathrm{run}}-2)\cdot\mathrm{sag}$ with
$\mathrm{sag}=\max_k|P_k|$ --- equality when $P$ has a single monotone hump, in which case the
curvature term is \emph{one} geometric number; on $83$ of the battery's $765$ transports that
is literally the case. The border eigenvector never changes sign inside the border block on
$765/765$ transports (the T109 cancellation geometry lives outside the block), and the shape
family is decided: the power profile $|w_i|\sim(i+1)^a$ wins on $635/635$ transports against
linear, exponential and reciprocal-pole candidates ($a=0.703$--$0.999$, median $0.856$ ---
T109's linear border profile, independently recovered). The Levinson coupling supplies the
\emph{structure}, not the bound: the T108 corner identity
$u_0=-\sqrt2\,\rho_{M-1}/E_{M-1}$ is independently reproduced ($24$ zones up to $M=1236$, to
$4.2\times10^{-9}$, exactly one negative pivot per zone), but the pole solve as a profile
model is only a \emph{proxy} (direction cosine $0.992$--$0.996$, curvature ratio
$1.5$--$5.3$) --- no majorant deployment. And the honest core: the shape band frozen on the
calibration half ($a\in[0.734,0.897]$, $S^*=1.1926$) \textbf{breaks on 13 of 545 disjoint
test transports} --- the bar is not moved; with the per-transport measured exponent the same
$S^*$ holds on $520/545$. \textbf{M22 is thereby reduced to a zone-uniform bound on
\emph{one} exponent --- reduced, named, and not closed.}

\subsection{The bridge stands as an identity: the matrix-form C\'ea/Strang defect}
The graded-to-uniform bridge --- the object M23 named --- is implemented and it is an
\emph{identity}:
\[
S_{\mathrm{graded}}-S_{\mathrm{uniform}} \;=\; R^{\mathsf T}X^{-1}R ,
\]
verified to $1.3\times10^{-13}$ and evaluated entirely matrix-free (an FFT matvec for the
fine form, a BPX-preconditioned CG, one graded Cholesky at or below the cap --- the fine
square matrix is never formed). The decisive distinction is scalar against \emph{matrix}
form: the scalar bound $\lambda_{\min}(S_{\mathrm{graded}})-\lambda_{\max}(G)$ is vacuous
(it loses a factor $3$--$28$), while the matrix form reproduces the \emph{directly computed}
uniform Schur floor on \textbf{84 compression pairs at 21 zones to $2.5\times10^{-11}$
relative, without a single overshoot} --- so \textsc{graded-certified} becomes a one-sided
statement about the \emph{fine} space. T129's measured $8\%$ false positives are thereby
explained \emph{completely}: $16/84$ reproduce at the bracket level but \textbf{0/84 at the
floor level} --- they sat in the transport bracket (graded test vectors), an object the
bridge makes unnecessary, not in the Schur floor. \textbf{Both deep seams carry}: $n=127$
($h_f=2476$) $\to\lambda_f=0.2255$ and $n=256$ ($h_f=5694$) $\to\lambda_f=0.2614$, with
positive bridged transport brackets, at $1.7\times$ and $3.8\times$ the factorisation cap,
reached through FFT matvec and CG only. The status is honest:
\textsc{bridged-certified-conditional} --- exactly \emph{one} measured number per grid
enters, a positive lower bound for $\lambda_{\min}(X_{\mathrm{fine}})$, currently supplied
by Lanczos (which bounds $\lambda_{\min}$ from \emph{above}: a measurement, never a
certificate); the Grenander--Szeg\H{o} symbol route for that number is \emph{dead} (the Weil
lag symbol is indefinite at these windows, rigorous symbol floor $-76\ldots-27$); and the
robustness is benign: the bridge conclusion does not move when the assumed floor is shrunk
by $100\times$ or $10\,000\times$.

\subsection{The theorem battery, the map V5, and the promotion check list}
The $\kappa$ battery against the \emph{theorem} chain rather than the fitted law:
\textbf{zero violations of the exact chain on all 765 transports} of the new battery --- the
chain is proved per transport, so this is implementation verification, not a test. The
fully explicit chain (the D1 candidate substituted for the curvature term) closes
$635/635$ of the shape transports --- but under the bare bar $2$ it closes \emph{none}:
measured $\kappa>2$ on $130/765$. Read honestly: the chain's own slack survives the $13$
shape-band misses --- that is robustness, not repair; the bound that would close M22 still
does not exist.

\begin{keybox}[The map V5 (T130): twelve items, one new gap, and the self-supply candidate]
\textbf{Certified}: \textbf{I1} the interval identity $\sum(1-g_i)=t_l+t_r$ ($\omega$ a
probability measure, $\kappa_{\mathrm{flat}}=1$); \textbf{I2} $\kappa$ as a weighted mean of
the border density (H\"older); \textbf{I3} the profile identity with
$2E>p_0\iff p_{N-1}>2$; \textbf{I4} the Abel chain; \textbf{I5 new: the TV rewrite} with the
one-hump collapse and the $(2n_{\mathrm{run}}-2)\cdot\mathrm{sag}$ bound.
\textbf{Certified-conditional}: \textbf{I6} the C\'ea/Strang bridge and \textbf{I7} the two
deep seams (one measured number per grid). \textbf{M22} reduced to a zone-uniform bound on
one exponent; \textbf{M23} \emph{conditionally closed} (the bracket-level false positives
remain as a test-vector artefact; the bridge replaces the bracket rather than repairing it);
\textbf{M25 new: a certified positive lower bound for
$\lambda_{\min}(X_{\mathrm{fine}})$} --- the only input I6 lacks, and it is small (the
$10^4$ robustness above); its elegant candidate is the \emph{self-supply}: the chain's own
telescope $\varepsilon$ floor could deliver it, which is exactly what T131 tests.
\textbf{M19} (``for all'') and \textbf{M21} (the RH address) are untouched.
\end{keybox}

\begin{keybox}[The promotion check list (T130): what a narrow vN module would carry]
Exactly the certified block: \emph{(1)}--\emph{(5)} T129's five identity/theorem pieces
(interval identity with $\kappa_{\mathrm{flat}}=1$; the mean representation with the
H\"older step; the profile identity and its equivalence; the Abel chain; the TV rewrite with
the sag bound), plus new from this probe \emph{(6)} the defect identity
$S_{\mathrm{graded}}-S_{\mathrm{uniform}}=R^{\mathsf T}X^{-1}R$ with its matrix bridge ---
pure Schur/Galerkin algebra, checkable on a random symmetric pair in three lines.
\textbf{Not in}: the frozen shape band and $S^*$ (a calibrated hypothesis, M22), the Lanczos
inner floor (M25), and both deep-seam certificates (they inherit M25). The three remaining
(L) seams are pure scale questions: only $n=8191$ ($h_f=295\,253$) admits a graded frame
under the cap at all ($q=256$, $m/h=0.0042$ --- far below the calibrated range), the other
two at no merge factor $q\le2048$.
\end{keybox}

\noindent T131 (\texttt{self\_supply\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{SELF.SUPPLY}, $25/25$,
verdict \textsc{supply-partial}) then tested exactly that candidate --- the self-supply of
$\lambda_{\min}(X_{\mathrm{fine}})$ through the chain's own telescope $\varepsilon$ floor,
the hardening of the four M22 properties, the M17 composition, and a map V6 with the final
promotion list --- and the answer is: \emph{the self-supply loop is built, one number short
of closed}. The remainder of this section is that part.

\subsection{The secular sandwich: the self-supply becomes a theorem (T131)}
The $\varepsilon\to\lambda_{\min}$ chain is no longer a candidate but a \emph{theorem},
derived from the secular equation of the rank-one downdate (Sherman--Morrison):
\[
\frac{\varepsilon}{\|u\|^{2}+\varepsilon/\mu_{1}}
\;\le\; \lambda_{\min}(Q) \;\le\; \frac{\varepsilon}{\|u\|^{2}},
\qquad
\varepsilon>0 \ \wedge\ A\succ0 \iff \lambda_{\min}(Q)>0 ,
\]
with $\mu_{1}=\lambda_{\min}(A)$ --- the upper half is convexity of the secular function,
the lower half the Sherman--Morrison denominator, and the sign half is an
\emph{equivalence} (Albert 1969). On $10$ dense windows ($h=26$--$434$, $n=3$--$31$):
\textbf{zero violations}, predicted relative width $1+\varepsilon/(\mu_{1}\|u\|^{2})
= 1.2663$--$1.3894$, and the true value sits at $1.06$--$1.31\times$ the lower bound
(median $1.22$) --- the bound is not merely valid, it is \emph{sharp}. The $\varepsilon$
side is supplied \emph{without} a fine factorisation: the same C\'ea/Strang defect that
carried the bridge carries $\varepsilon$ (a CG-tightened certified lower bound), valid on
$10/10$ and retaining $1.0000$ of the true $\varepsilon$. Two side findings, both named:
\emph{(a)} the \textbf{direction obstruction} --- the mirror rung lacks a Loewner
\emph{minorant}, $T(\ell)$ is indefinite on $4/4$ windows ($\min\ell<0$ wherever the lag
symbol dips: the same indefiniteness that killed the Szeg\H{o} route), so the telescope
runs coarse-from-fine only; \emph{(b)} a \textbf{correction to T130}: the Lanczos Ritz
value overestimates $\lambda_{\min}(X)$ by a factor of up to $\mathbf{7.9}$ --- it is an
upper bound by construction (Rayleigh--Ritz), so T130's conditional hung on a number that
is systematically \emph{too large}; a chain floor below the Lanczos value is not a loss but
the difference between an estimate and a bound. The bridge test: on \textbf{84 bridge pairs
the chain floor replaces the Lanczos floor with zero brackets lost} (bar $0$, declared),
retaining $0.766$--$1.059$ (median $0.875$) against the genuine certified Cholesky floor;
\textbf{both deep seams are chain-positive} ($n=127$: $\varepsilon\ge2.63\times10^{-7}\to$
floor $1.08\times10^{-6}$, $\lambda_f=+0.2255$; $n=256$: $3.83\times10^{-8}\to
2.52\times10^{-7}$, $\lambda_f=+0.2614$); and the residual input is robust --- $\mu_{1}$
may be underestimated by \textbf{nine decades} before the floor dies, because $\mu_{1}$
enters only as a correction. \textbf{M25 is thereby reduced-to-pole-free}: what was
``certify $\lambda_{\min}$ of the near-singular inner block at $h=5694$'' is now ``certify
positivity of the \emph{pole-free} odd Toeplitz--Hankel section, with any floor within nine
decades of the measured value''.

\subsection{The Perron sign theorem, the honest one-hump break, and the M17 vacuity (T131)}
On $575$ transports, one of the four M22 properties becomes a theorem and one honestly
breaks. \emph{(i)} \textbf{Sign constancy is a theorem} --- not via Metzler (the border
block has all off-diagonals nonpositive on only $11/575$, so that route fails as a
hypothesis) but via \textbf{Perron--Frobenius on $S^{-1}$}, which is entrywise positive on
$575/575$ transports (Perron root $=1/\lambda_{\min}(S)$); the eigenvector is strictly
sign-constant \emph{and} $\lambda_{\min}$ is simple --- exactly what T130's $765/765$ could
only record. \emph{(ii)} The \textbf{one-hump property breaks at depth}, revising T130: on
the deeper sweep $P$ has more than two monotone runs on $530/575$ transports, so the
$\mathrm{curv}=2\cdot\mathrm{sag}$ identity is not generally available; what carries
instead is the certified $(2n_{\mathrm{run}}-2)\cdot\mathrm{sag}$ majorant, valid on
$575/575$ with no hump hypothesis at all, plus the run-count chain
$n_{\mathrm{run}}\le s_{2}+2$. \emph{(iii)} The exponent band stays a \emph{measurement}
($a=0.727$--$0.999$ over $492$ transports, $55/492$ outside the old calibration band; the
drifts are fits, not bounds). \emph{(iv)} $S^{*}$ stays a measurement --- and the deeper
sweep \emph{raises} it: the frozen $1.1926$ is exceeded $16$ times, and the constant this
sweep would need is $\mathbf{1.8472}$. M22 thereby collapses onto \emph{one} object --- the
border profile exponent --- but by a different route than expected: (i) is proved, (ii) is
dropped rather than reduced. And M17 is \textbf{assembled --- and vacuous, with the loss
attributed}: the mass bound is mounted from its two parts (the pole part in closed form to
$1.8\times10^{-13}$, the harmonic-mean identity to $2.0\times10^{-11}$), majorises the true
bad mass on $27/27$ rungs --- and reaches the bar on $0/27$. The dominant loss is the
\textbf{U-metric mismatch} $2.18$ (the assembly measures in the $U$-metric but bounds the
comb in the Euclidean one); the named fix is M18 run in the whitened basis --- not more
measurement. Status: \textsc{assembled-vacuous}.

\begin{keybox}[The map V6 (T131): thirteen items, and the nine-item promotion list]
\textbf{Theorem/identity} ($3$): the $\varepsilon\to\lambda_{\min}$ sandwich (M25a), the
Perron sign theorem (M22a), the C\'ea/Strang bridge identity (M23).
\textbf{Certified(-conditional)} ($2$): $\varepsilon$ at a deep grid without a fine
factorisation (M25b), the chain floors on the $84$ bridge pairs and both deep seams (M25c).
\textbf{Measurement/hypothesis/fit} ($4$): $\lambda_{\min}(A)>0$ for the pole-free section
(M25d --- the one number the whole loop now rests on), the exponent $a$ (M22c), the
constant $S^{*}$ (M22d), the quantifier (M19). \textbf{Dead/broken} ($3$): the mirror rung
(M25e), the one hump (M22b), the RH address (M21 --- cited, never used). The
\textbf{promotion list grows from six to nine items}, each a per-instance certificate, none
zone-uniform --- new here: \emph{(7)} the $\varepsilon\to\lambda_{\min}$ sandwich,
\emph{(8)} sign constancy from $S^{-1}>0$ (Perron--Frobenius), \emph{(9)} the run-count
chain $n_{\mathrm{run}}\le s_{2}+2$. The shortest honest remaining list has four points:
\emph{(1)} $\lambda_{\min}(A)>0$ pole-free (nine decades of slack), \emph{(2)} a
zone-uniform bound on the exponent and the run count, \emph{(3)} $S^{-1}>0$ structurally,
\emph{(4)} the quantifier.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The reverse flow: the toolkit pays the theory side back (T132/T133)}
For the first time the direction of the program inverts. Two \emph{reverse-flow} probes
carry the certification toolkit built for the prime front back to the theory side of the
compiler, and both land.

\noindent T132 (\texttt{bd\_seam\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{BD.SEAM}, $21/21$, verdict
\textsc{spectrum-only}) reads the seam Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator through the
Beurling--Deny triad (jump kernel, killing measure, diffusion part) and uses it as an
\emph{operator} discriminator where the spectrum alone is blind. The Beurling--Deny identity
is exact to $2.4\times10^{-16}$ at every $N$; the triad reconstructs the operator, not just
its form ($1.4\times10^{-14}$ at $d=256$); the diffusion part vanishes identically (a finite
atomic state space carries no strongly local part, and the continuum DtN is the pure-jump
$\tfrac12$-Laplacian, Caffarelli--Silvestre 2007, fitted decay exponent $1.980$); and the
killing measure \emph{is} the $\mu_4$ mark-sum curvature profile pointwise
($1.3\times10^{-14}$) --- the marks enter the operator exclusively through the killing term.
The Markov remainder is not small but \emph{exactly zero}, with a closed jump-weight form
$J_{rs}=1/(d\sin^2(\pi(r-s)/d))$ for $r-s$ odd and exactly $0$ for $r-s$ even, which is what
explains T126's $86.1\%$ count fraction (the even lags are the zeros, not defects). The
discriminator then separates: the model DtN and the $\mu_4$-graded free contraction share
their spectrum to $7.5\times10^{-13}$ but are \emph{not} the same operator, and the gap sits
in the killing measure at $0.1746$ at every $N$ (spread $1.9\times10^{-14}$ relative) while
the jump gap shrinks monotonically ($7.7\times10^{-3}\to3.5\times10^{-4}$) --- so the
difference is not a truncation artefact. The mark-preserving lattice symmetries (the clock
rotation and the sheet reflection, generating $D_4$) leave the triad exactly invariant
($1.6\times10^{-14}$), while a random orthogonal or a generic $\mu_4$-equivariant
conjugation destroys it: the triad is canonical relative to the \emph{marked boundary
coordinate}, not to the marks alone --- which is a coupling statement,
\texttt{QGEO.MARKS.01} $\leftrightarrow$ \texttt{QGEO.KERNEL.01}. \textbf{Ceiling, stated:}
this is the \emph{model} DtN, not the raw RP seam, so \texttt{QGEO.KERNEL.01} is
\emph{not} closed.

\noindent T133 (\texttt{cert\_floor\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{CERT.FLOOR}, $23/23$,
verdict \textsc{mixed}, $840$ control validations on the certificate machinery itself) turns
the toolkit on the load-bearing suite's own PSD rows and produces one finding that changed a
module in this same round. The $10\times10$ Hankel matrix \vref{v379} builds is, \emph{as a
matrix of doubles}, certifiably \emph{not} positive semidefinite --- a certified negative
direction $x^{\mathsf T}\widehat Mx=-7.75\times10^{-18}$ at $60$ digits --- and the module's
own tolerance ($\lambda_{\min}>-10^{-10}$) passes it. The reason is priced, not guessed:
$\mathrm{cond}\approx1.8\times10^{18}$ against a certifiability ceiling of
$1.0\times10^{13}$ at $n=10$, and the entry bar $7.6\times10^{-12}$ from evaluating a
transcendental kernel in binary64 dominates every floor the floating-point route could
produce; the blind band is exhibited explicitly (a matrix with a certified negative
direction passes the test). The \emph{mathematical} matrix is fine --- at $40$ digits it
carries a certified floor $2.7\times10^{-25}$, while rounding its entries to doubles moves
it by $3.5\times10^{-17}$, eight orders more --- and the exact repair needs no precision at
all: the same object is a \emph{positive-mixture Gram}
$M=V^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{diag}(1/1600)V$ with $V_{ki}=e^{-\varepsilon_k\tau_i}$, so
$x^{\mathsf T}Mx=\sum_kw_k(v_k\!\cdot\!x)^2$ is a sum of squares and $M\succeq0$ holds in
exact arithmetic with no bar. The probe's recommendation table asks for method and wording,
explicitly \emph{not} a marker move (\vref{v171}'s Vandermonde Gram is exactly certified
already; \vref{v527}/\vref{v519} certify at the $40$ digits they already run).
\textbf{That repair is executed in this same change:} \vref{v379} now asserts the exact
mixture identity ($1.1\times10^{-15}$, at the rounding level) plus a sum-of-squares
certificate, the eigenvalue check is retained and relabelled a diagnostic
\emph{measurement}, and the ledger row \texttt{SEAM.S3.RP.01} is reworded accordingly ---
marker \mE\ unchanged.

\noindent The narrow identity module of this phase is promoted: \vref{v542},
Section~\ref{sec:v542}.

\subsection{The pole-free floor: existence closes, and every cheap route fails by sign (T134)}
T134 (\texttt{pole\_free\_floor\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{POLE.FREE.FLOOR}, $21/21$,
verdict \textsc{partial}; sub-verdicts \textsc{floor-certified-per-window} /
\textsc{class-identified} / \textsc{mass-vacuous-metric-fixed}) attacks the three items of
T131's rest list, and nothing else. The first answer closes the existence question: the
floor is \emph{never} an existence problem. Every Cholesky pivot of the \emph{pole-free}
form is positive on $79/79$ windows --- T119's famous negative pivot belonged to the form
\emph{with} the pole --- and the per-window Cholesky certificate carries R4 on $79/79$ and
R2b on $68/68$. But the route table is unanimous: every cheap lower-bound route fails by
\emph{sign}, not size, so the nine decades of slack T131 measured are worthless to them ---
they die at a sign that no margin can heal.

\begin{center}
{\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Cheap route} & \textbf{Certifies} & \textbf{Failure mode}\\
\midrule
Dirichlet $+$ Gershgorin & $0/68$ & sign\\
global Gershgorin & $0/68$ & sign\\
Jacobi dominance & $0/68$ & sign\\
Ostrowski (1937) & $0/68$ & sign\\
path comparison & infeasible & congestion up to $4.7\times10^{5}$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\end{center}

\noindent The anatomy names the culprit. The good half $\mathrm{diag}(w)+L_N$ of the T126
fractional Dirichlet split is positive definite on $68/68$ windows, with
$\lambda_{\min}=0.13$--$3.06$ --- that is $8$--$85{,}000\times$ \emph{above} the target;
the \emph{entire} loss is the positive-off-diagonal Laplacian $L_P$ at the long lags:
$\lambda_{\max}(L_P)/\mu_{1}$ up to $4.7\times10^{5}$ at effective rank
$k_{\mathrm{eff}}=24$--$397$, so $L_P$ is neither small nor low-rank, and T131's rank-one
trick does not generalise. R5 is measured rather than hoped: the compression \emph{and}
the Schur floor both sit \emph{above} $\lambda_{\min}$ on $56/56$ pairs --- no coarse
recursion can ever supply a lower bound here --- and the certified floor degrades like
$D^{2.26}$ (a fit, labelled a fit). The second answer gives the inverse-positivity its
\emph{class}: the naive split stays definite on only $659/900$ blocks, but the
\textbf{lumped Stieltjes comparison matrix} $S_B=S+L_\Delta$ (the positive off-diagonals
lumped onto the diagonal) is Stieltjes \emph{and} positive definite by construction, with
Jacobi spectral radius $\rho=0.709$--$0.990<1$ on $900/900$ blocks --- so $S_B^{-1}$ is a
convergent nonnegative Neumann series, a discrete Green function: the M-matrix ground of
the sign constancy that T131 could only record via Perron. The naive killing reading is
refuted (row sums negative on $900/900$), the Neumann margin is a valid majorant on
$558/558$ (a-priori coverage $26.9\%$), entrywise positivity of $S^{-1}$ is now
\emph{certified} a posteriori on $900/900$ (residual $\le2.6\times10^{-11}$ against
smallest entries $\sim10^{-2}$), and the weakening ``reflection positivity on the floor
eigenspace only'' is \emph{refuted} ($535/900$): the full statement is needed, the weaker
one is false. The third answer honestly corrects T131's diagnosis: the $2.18$ U-metric
mismatch was \emph{not} the dominant loss --- in the one honest norm the \emph{comb
dominates the pole} by $4.7$--$81\times$, invisible to T131 because its comparison mixed
two metrics. With the exact normaliser the whitened mass bound gets a \emph{finite} number
for the first time: $2.81$--$5.04$ (sharp $2.52$--$4.26$) against the bar $0.50$ --- a
factor $5.6$--$10$, no longer orders of magnitude --- and $\sigma_b=0.77$--$1.00$ exhausts
the budget alone, so what is missing is a \emph{localisation} statement about the bad
pencil subspace, not more measurement.

\begin{keybox}[After T134: fifteen promotion items, five remaining points]
The promotion list grows from nine to \textbf{fifteen} per-instance items (new: the
per-window Cholesky certificate of the pole-free form, the lumped Stieltjes construction,
the $\rho(\mathrm{Jacobi})<1$ series, the a-posteriori $S^{-1}>0$ certification, the
row-sum refutation of the killing reading, the whitened mass identity). The remaining list
has \textbf{five} points: \emph{(1)} $D$-uniformity of the floor via the M-matrix question
(under attack in T136), \emph{(2)} a zone-uniform bound on the exponent and the run count,
\emph{(3)} $\rho(K)$ a priori rather than measured, \emph{(4)} the M17 localisation,
\emph{(5)} the quantifier.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The reverse flow, continued: a bounded seam state where the Weil window has none (T135)}
T135 (\texttt{comb\_compressibility\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{COMB.COMPRESS}, $13/13$,
verdict \textsc{bounded-state (matched)}) is the third reverse-flow trace: does the T116
boundary-state machinery, which \emph{failed} at the Weil window because the off-band mass
sits in the prime comb, \emph{succeed} on the seam DtN of T132/\vref{v210}, whose comb is
an arithmetic progression? The port is verbatim --- the T116 Riccati recursion runs
unchanged on $\Lambda_\Sigma$ (bordered pole identity $1.5\times10^{-16}$, the banded
$m$-march reproducing the dense state to $8.2\times10^{-16}$), with the pole candidate the
rank-$8$ Calder\'on polarisation of \vref{v113} on the $16$ residue classes, documented as
a \emph{model} (v210's DtN carries no rank-one defect of its own). The census is not a
kill: $100.00\%$ of the off-band mass sits on the mod-$4$ stripes (the complement is
machine zero), and the stripe count grows exactly like $\lfloor W/4\rfloor=\Theta(W)$
(fitted exponent $1.0000\pm0.0000$) where the true prime comb grows like $\pi(W)$
($0.726\pm0.018$). The core number: $\mathbf{m_{\mathrm{cert}}=12}$ --- the recursion
error is $3.3\times10^{-16}$ of the margin exactly ($h=64$--$1500$) and
$2.1\times10^{-17}$ certified on the deep march ($h=1000$--$100{,}000$, \emph{falling} in
$h$) --- against T116's $1.3\times10^{2}$--$1.1\times10^{6}$ \emph{above} the margin at
the Weil window; growing the state $m=4\to48$ divides the error by $4.7\times10^{8}$,
where T116 saw no decay at all. The match is honestly labelled: $12$ lies in the
pre-declared look-elsewhere set $\{8,12,16,24,32\}$ (the search ladder contains five
non-member values), so the verdict is \textsc{matched}, not ``derived'' --- and the value
is bar-fragile, with only the band $8\le m_{\mathrm{cert}}\le16$ defensible. The controls
name the driver: an artificial mod-$3$ seam \emph{also} closes ($m=8$), so the causal
driver is \textbf{weight summability}, not the comb period as such; and the \emph{true}
prime comb \emph{fails} under identical machinery (no $h$-uniform floor, error/margin
$0.16$--$0.27$ and rising, $12\times$ more state buying only $1.69\times$) ---
non-vacuity established: the same recursion separates the two combs. \textbf{Sober, three
times over:} \texttt{QEC.SEAM.01} is \emph{not} advanced (the driver is not the period),
$m_{\mathrm{cert}}$ is partly circular (the $16$ channels are set by hand), and this is
the model DtN, not the raw reflection-positive seam. What is new and non-circular: the
finite-dimensionality of the seam is visible in a \emph{recursion} for the first time, not
only in a spectrum, with the Weil window as the proved contrast.

\subsection{The $M$-matrix pair: one item closes, and the bookkeeping is exact (T136)}
T136 (\texttt{m\_matrix\_pair\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{M.MATRIX.PAIR}, $30/30$, verdict
\textsc{one-carries}) attacks the $M$-matrix question T134 named, and one of the three goals carries
cleanly. The classical row-sum criterion --- the thing one would normally invoke to get
$\rho(\mathrm{Jacobi})<1$ --- is \emph{vacuous} at the border block (the row sums are negative on
$900/900$, the same sign failure that killed T134's cheap routes). It is not needed: for the lumped
Stieltjes comparison, \textbf{Varga's regular splitting turns convergence into an identity},
$\rho(J)=\tau/(1+\tau)$ with $\tau=\rho(A_B^{-1}N_0)$, and the Collatz--Wielandt bound at the
$M$-matrix anchor vector reproduces the measured $\rho=0.709$--$0.990$ with a gap shortfall of only
$1.00$--$1.03$, \emph{flat} in $D$ and in the zone on $900/900$ blocks (a priori
$\rho\le0.7160$--$0.9903$ against the declared bar $10$). Two solves replace a spectrum, and rest item
\emph{(3)} --- ``$\rho(K)$ a priori rather than measured'' --- is \textbf{closed}.

The other goal resists, but its anatomy is now exact. The pair is the right tool and runs the right
way; the anchor half is certifiable and loses at most $1.51$. What kills it is the margin: $1-\rho(E)$
is only $2.5\times10^{-6}$--$2.5\times10^{-2}$ wide while \emph{every} cheap upper bound on $\rho(E)$
sits at $1.82$--$3.11$. The shifted ladder is empty \emph{by monotonicity of $M$-matrix inverses}, not
by measurement. And the three-way bookkeeping splits the degradation exactly:
\[
\lambda_{\min}(A)\ \sim\ D^{2.27}\ =\ \underbrace{D^{-0.56}}_{\text{anchor}}\times
\underbrace{D^{2.72}}_{\text{margin gap}}\times\underbrace{D^{0.12}}_{\text{slack}}
\qquad\text{(exact split; the exponents are fits).}
\]
The anchor \emph{improves} with depth; the slack is flat; the entire loss is the gap. Finally M17
closes \emph{negatively}: the bad pencil subspace is \textbf{delocalised} (participation ratio
$0.54$--$0.98$, $91\%$ of the cells carrying $90\%$ of the mass, flatness $0.98$ on every rung),
neither a border layer nor the comb stripes, with a dimension tracking the \emph{atom count} rather
than the grid ($n_{\mathrm{bad}}/n_{\mathrm{at}}=1.64$ median). Hence $\sigma_b(k)\ge0.646$ on the
whole ladder and no choice of cut brings M18 under its $0.50$ bar (best $2.76$--$7.00$): what M18 needs
is a localisation \emph{statement}, and there is none. The identity block of this part is promoted:
\vref{v543}, Section~\ref{sec:v543}.

\subsection{The long lags: the support is arithmetic, and the absolute value is the culprit (T137)}
T137 (\texttt{long\_lag\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{LONG.LAG}, $22/22$, verdict
\textsc{both-resist}) names the perturbation, and the name is arithmetic. The archimedean half of the
kernel is nondecreasing outside its near field and its odd section carries \emph{no} positive
off-diagonal ($0$ of $40$ windows), so the whole bad half $L_\Delta$ is \textbf{comb-generated},
supported on the anti-diagonal stripes $r+s=M-1-\mathrm{round}(u_j/D)$ addressed by the prime-power
atoms of the window ($1.00$--$1.00$ of the positive entries within one grid cell of an atom, $4$--$192$
stripes on $3$--$99$ atoms, $1.33$--$1.97$ stripes per atom), with the certifiable amplitude law
$\Delta\le c_{\mathrm{comb}}(\mathrm{anti})$ --- while the single-atom form $\mu_j/2$ is only
\emph{typical} (worst ratio $2.268$). Each stripe is a perfect matching, so $L_\Delta$ is explicit.

Half the question then closes, and it closes as a \emph{certificate of death}. Gershgorin bounds,
row-sum norms and Collatz--Wielandt quotients at positive weights are all upper bounds for
$\rho(|E|)$, so a single \emph{lower} bound decides them all: the Collatz--Wielandt lower end gives
$\rho(|E|)\ge1.3218$--$2.2963$, above $1$ on $35/35$ windows. \textbf{No member of the
absolute-value envelope family can ever produce a floor} --- not ``has not yet''. The second family
(edge/Gram bounds built on the \emph{second differences} of the Green function) is alive but does not
beat the margin: $1.1530$--$2.3502$ against a true $\rho$ of $0.9752$--$1.0000$ and a target of
$0.998879$--$0.999999$, clearing it on $0$ of $35$ windows (overshoot $24$--$7.1\times10^{5}$ gaps).
The coarsening ladder says why it fails structurally --- the bound drops below $1$ only when the entire
Gram is a single block, so \emph{the inter-stripe coupling is the object} --- and the measured decay of
those second differences is algebraic ($\mathrm{dist}^{-0.63}\pm0.02$, a fit) where a
Demko--Moss--Smith argument would need a geometric one, $A_B$ being dense so that theorem does not
apply at all.

Rest item \emph{(2)} is delivered without being closed. The Neumann series of the regular splitting
gives $(S_B^{-1})_{rs}\ge\max_{k\le6}(J^k)_{rs}/d_s$ \emph{with no error term} --- only a discarded
nonnegative tail --- the partial sum recovering $0.011$--$0.406$ of the true minimum entry, and the
anchor-weighted ceiling overshooting the maximum by only $2.49$--$12.24$: the Green function is
two-sidedly bracketed by path quantities. But the certificate for $S^{-1}>0$ still stops at $563/900$
blocks ($716/900$ with the sign-aware $k$-exact split, $66/900$ from the structural bracket alone)
because $\rho(|F|)\ge1$ on $77$ blocks --- the same wall. \textbf{The diagnosis is one sentence:} the
absolute value destroys the cancellation that the seam lives on, and what is missing on both sides is
a \emph{sign-preserving} bound. The structure block of this part is promoted: \vref{v544},
Section~\ref{sec:v544}.

\begin{keybox}[After T137: thirty promotion items, and a rest list of three]
The promotion list grows from fifteen to \textbf{thirty} per-instance items ($7$ new in T136, $8$ in
T137); thirteen of them are now load-bearing as \vref{v543} and \vref{v544}. Of T134's five remaining
points, \emph{(3)} $\rho(K)$ a priori is \textbf{closed} (Varga plus Collatz--Wielandt) and
\emph{(4)} the M17 localisation is \textbf{closed negatively} (the bad subspace is delocalised, so no
cut works); the Green lower bound asked for under \emph{(1)} is delivered two-sidedly. What is left is
\emph{(1)} $D$-uniformity of the floor --- now sharpened to a single named requirement, a
\emph{sign-preserving} bound on $\rho(E)$, every absolute-value route being certifiably dead ---
together with \emph{(2)} the zone-uniform exponent and \emph{(5)} the quantifier.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The sign law is interval geometry, and the $m$-paired certificate removes the arithmetic wall (T138)}
T138 (\texttt{sign\_compensation\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{SIGN.COMPENSATION}, $26/26$, verdict
\textsc{pair-exact}) keeps the cancellation instead of majorising it, and finds the mechanism. The
sign of an inter-stripe coupling is decided by the \textbf{interval geometry} of its two edges ---
nested pairs positive on $0.76$--$0.94$ of the area, crossing pairs on $0.59$--$0.63$, disjoint pairs
on only $0.06$--$0.36$ (i.e.\ mostly \emph{negative}) --- and \emph{not} by the lag distance (the
alternation reading is false, flip rate near $0.5$). The couplings cancel globally to
$|\Sigma|/\Sigma|\cdot|=0.29$--$0.70$: that one number is the reason for every absolute-value
overshoot of T134--T137. The honest negatives stand beside it: Karlin total positivity \emph{fails}
(only $0.54$--$0.65$ of the TP$_2$ minors nonnegative), and the Green function is rank-one dominated
but \emph{not} semiseparable --- the Gantmacher--Krein one-pair form stays a candidate, not a theorem.

The best \emph{certified} signed bound (band-exact by completed Cholesky, tail by block row sums)
reaches $0.9758$--$1.1020$ against a target of $0.998556$--$0.999979$ --- clearing $1$ of $16$
windows, overshoot $0.02$--$565$ gaps against T137's $27$--$8\times10^{5}$: three orders of magnitude
better, and still no floor. The clamp is measured, not conjectured: the band part alone crosses the
target at bandwidth $b=2$--$8$ while the certified tail there is still $0.46$--$0.70$ --- the two
terms are never small together; the far tail is \emph{net negative} (truncation \emph{raises} $\rho$
on $59/142$ rungs, so the tail is no small remainder --- it carries compensation); the Leibniz
pairing buys nothing; and the exact two-stripe blocks are thin ($\le0.6\%$ gain, three-body terms up
to $2.5\times$): \textbf{the compensation is a many-body effect}.

The gain of the part is the \textbf{$m$-paired Neumann certificate}. From the identity
$(I-F)^{-1}=\bigl(\sum_{j<m}F^{j}\bigr)(I-F^{m})^{-1}$ only the \emph{outer} factor is majorised, so
the condition is $\rho(|F^{m}|)<1$ --- genuinely weaker than $\rho(|F|)<1$, because the signs inside
the $m$-fold product are kept ($m=1$ is verbatim T137's certificate). It removes the arithmetic wall
on \emph{all} $77$ dead blocks and certifies $52$ of them; the pool rises from $563$ to $875/900$
(mechanism: $\max|F^{m}|/|F|^{m}$ falls to median $0.295$). And M17 gets its characterisation:
whitened, $\mathrm{St}=I-W_S$ with $W_S\succeq0$, so $\lambda(\mathrm{St})\le1$ structurally, and the
\emph{direct} Kantorovich harmonic-mean route needs no bad subspace anywhere, relaxing the required
margin from $0.5$ to $0.8284$ ($854/900$) --- \textsc{harmonic-partial}. The honest conclusion: the
signed route does \emph{not} inherit the absolute-value wall --- the $m$-ladder is a real structural
gain --- but the margin question returns one level down, as an upper bound on $\rho(W_S)$.

\subsection{The decay lemma is refuted at its hypothesis, and the sign law is derived (T139)}
T139 (\texttt{green\_decay\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{GREEN.DECAY}, $30/30$, verdict
\textsc{dense-resists}, bars declared in the docstring before any number) asks whether the classical
dense-matrix literature supplies the decay lemma T138 named as the one missing input --- and the
answer is an \emph{arithmetic} refusal. The lemma fails \textbf{at the hypothesis}: the certified
kernel envelope of the lumped pair decays only like $d^{-0.15}$--$d^{-0.04}$ where Jaffard 1990 needs
$p>1$ --- the archimedean lags sit \emph{on} the borderline ($\sim1/d$) and the prime-power atoms lay
$O(1)$ spikes across the whole window, so no entrywise envelope can see the thinness of the comb. The
constant chain is built constructively rather than asserted: Demko--Moss--Smith is \emph{verified}
where it applies ($75/75$ banded rungs --- the failure on the dense object is a statement about the
object), the operator gate reads $0.97$--$1.83$ ($1/75$ below $1$), the algebra gate
$3.4$--$1.6\times10^{3}$ ($0/75$); and the exponent is \emph{not} inherited (kernel
$d^{-1.5\ldots-1.1}$ against inverse $d^{-0.52\ldots-0.37}$). The positive replacement object is an
identity, not a fit: the exact double telescoping
$b_e^{\mathsf T}Gb_{e'}=\sum_{\mathrm{box}}H$ ($4\times10^{-15}$), with the mixed second-difference
kernel $H$ negative on $0.71$--$0.87$ of the off-diagonal and positive on $100\%$ of the diagonal ---
\textbf{T138's entire sign law follows from one kernel plus box geometry} (disjoint pairs have boxes
missing the diagonal), derived instead of measured; the Gantmacher--Krein one-pair form is
quantitatively dead.

The never-truncating layer series is then killed \emph{from below}. It delivers $3.388$--$6.171$
against a target of $0.999182$--$0.999972$ ($0/15$, overshoot $681$--$46900\times$), and triple
direction pedantry pays: no layer may be dropped (zero trace forces $\lambda_{\max}>0$, and a
negative \emph{mean} is not a Loewner sign), $\mathrm{Gram}\preceq\mathrm{Band}_b$ holds \emph{never}
($85/85$), and by Weyl every layer series is
$\ge\max_b\mathrm{Rayleigh}(\mathrm{Band}_b)=1.0303$--$1.1116$, above the target on $15/15$ windows
--- \textbf{no decay lemma of any strength can rescue the family}, and the overshoot \emph{is} the
effective layer count, growing with the window. The clean-up localises the rest: on a rebuilt pool of
$620$ border blocks the $m$-paired ladder out to $m=48$ converges on all $620$ and certifies
$\mathbf{619/620}$ --- the one open block (need $2.15$) has its tightness at index distance $15$,
far-carried $1.000$: the single item for which an $H$-decay statement would be the right tool. One
level down, $\rho(W_S)=0.667$--$0.939$ inherits the structure verbatim; the three-body cumulant is
negative on $0.93$--$1.00$ of $4200$ stripe triples --- certified \emph{benign} (it pushes upper
bounds down). The three-sentence verdict, quotable: the lemma does not exist classically for this
object, for an arithmetic reason; the right object is now identified exactly (the sign law is
derived), but every absolute-value envelope built from it is certified dead; \textbf{the measured
core has shrunk from ``a decay law is missing'' to ``one signed inequality at stripe distance
$b\le16$ is missing''}.

\begin{keybox}[After T139: forty-seven promotion items, and a rest list led by one signed inequality]
The promotion list grows from thirty to \textbf{forty-seven} per-instance items ($7$ new in T138,
$10$ in T139). The sign-preserving bound T137 asked for is now \emph{located} rather than merely
named: everything that fails, fails at stripe distance $b\le16$ --- the far layers are certifiably
harmless (the tail is net negative, the three-body remainder certified benign, the border pool at
$619/620$) --- so \emph{(R1)} is \textbf{one signed small-bandwidth inequality} that keeps the
cancellation among the first few stripe layers (a signed block-Rayleigh or interlacing step, not a
norm). Behind it: \emph{(R2)} a replacement for the Weyl step (any $O(nb)$ triangle-inequality route
is dead by construction), \emph{(R3)} an optional certified decay statement for $H$ (which by
construction cannot close R1 --- every entrywise envelope is an absolute-value route), the same
margin question one level down ($\rho(W_S)$), and, unchanged, the zone-uniform exponent and the
quantifier.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The signed inequality acquires an exact finite core, and the $D$-dependence is located (T140)}
T140 (\texttt{signed\_band\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{SIGNED.BAND}, $31/31$, verdict
\textsc{finite-core}, bars declared in the docstring before any number) attacks exactly the one
signed inequality at stripe distance $b\le16$ that T139 left --- and nothing else. The central move
lifts the $H$-telescope from the \emph{entries} of the Gram to its \emph{form}:
$\mathrm{Gram}=CHC^{\mathsf T}$ exactly ($3\times10^{-15}$), with $C=\mathrm{diag}(\sqrt\Delta)M$
and $M$ the edge-interval incidence matrix, so
$x^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Gram}\,x=u^{\mathsf T}Hu$ with $u=C^{\mathsf T}x$ the occupation function of
the edge system --- Abel summation over the box structure, with no absolute value and no
factorisation. Three exact consequences follow. First, $\mathrm{rank}(\mathrm{Gram})\le h-1$
(measured $44$--$202$ against $128$--$1499$ edges, a $2$--$10\times$ reduction): the whole spectral
question lives on the index grid. Second, \textbf{the exact finite-core reduction}
$\rho(W)=\lambda_{\max}(K^{1/2}HK^{1/2})$ ($6\times10^{-14}$ on every window), with
$K=M^{\mathsf T}\Delta M$ the \emph{covering kernel} in closed geometric form
$K_{rs}=W([r\wedge s,\,r\vee s])$ --- entrywise nonnegative, monotone by inclusion, Green-like but
\emph{not} one-pair (the off-tridiagonal defect of $K^{-1}$ is $0.08$--$0.43$). The signed
inequality is thereby a statement about two matrices of size $h-1$ per prime-power zone, reached
with no truncation, no envelope and no series anywhere. Third, the energy reordering
$H=\mathrm{diag}(s)+L_N$, exact to $5\times10^{-16}$: T139's sign law read as a mass term plus a
long-range \emph{Dirichlet form} with positive weights on $0.71$--$0.87$ of the off-diagonal. The
direct certified chain (every Loewner step a completed Cholesky) then locates the failure exactly:
it \textbf{tears at step (i)} --- dropping the $13$--$29\%$ \emph{positive} part of $H$'s
off-diagonal alone costs the whole margin ($6.4$--$239\times\rho(W)$), because that positive part
is precisely the compensation the sign law lives on; the discrete Hardy step adds only
$6.4$--$9.0\times$ on top, with the first-moment input $\max_kQ_k=18.5$--$339$; below the target on
$0/16$ windows for all four routes.

The checkerboard split solves R2. With stripe groups of length $b$ the band condition forces
adjacent groups, the adjacent pairs two-colour into two families of disjoint supports, and
$\mathrm{Band}_b=D+A_{\mathrm{even}}+A_{\mathrm{odd}}$ holds \emph{entrywise exactly} --- so
\textbf{three $D$-independent Weyl steps replace the $O(nb)$ ones} whose count T139 identified as
the overshoot itself (value $0.76$--$1.79\times\rho(W)$). And the zone question is answered by
measurement: the stripe block norms are \emph{zone-uniform} (group diagonal
$\sim D^{0.13\pm0.01}$, cross blocks $\sim D^{0.02\pm0.01}$, fits) while
$\lambda_{\max}(K)\sim D^{-2.99}$, $\lambda_{\max}(H)\sim D^{+0.33}$ and
$\max_kQ_k\sim D^{-2.20}$ against a flat $\rho(W)=0.9962$--$0.9999$ --- \textbf{the entire
$D$-dependence of the core sits in the geometry, not in the stripe blocks}, so only a \emph{joint}
bound on the pair can be $D$-uniform. Floquet is not applicable (the stripes sit at atom
positions; group spread $0.15$--$0.69$, quasi-periodic); Bendixson certifies
$1.07$--$1.08\times\rho(W)$ without any Gram (the spectrum of $W$ is real), below the target on
$0/16$. Two hard negatives close the additive readings for good: the band \emph{from below}
exceeds the target on $16/16$ windows (Rayleigh $0.3894$--$1.1116$ on $229$ (window,\,$b$) pairs
--- the mechanism is \emph{rank inflation}: truncation inflates the rank by $1.2$--$10.1\times$,
cutting exactly the directions in which the boxes compensate), and the new index-grid band family
--- the only one in which a truncation could have been legitimate --- fails the Loewner test too:
the discarded far part is certified Loewner-\emph{positive}
($\lambda_{\max}\ge1.14\times10^{-1}$) on $256/256$ pairs. The clean-up inherits and localises: of
$420$ rebuilt border blocks the $m$-paired ladder to $m=24$ certifies $402$; the $18$ open ones
carry need $1.01$--$10.99$ with argmax at index distance $11$--$15$ and \emph{far mass fraction}
$0.635$--$0.912$ --- genuinely far-carried, so a decay statement is the right tool \emph{there},
unlike R1; and the finite core is inherited one level down
($\rho(W_S)=\lambda_{\max}(K_S^{1/2}H_SK_S^{1/2})$ to $4\times10^{-15}$;
$\rho(W_S)=0.541$--$0.925$).

\begin{keybox}[After T140: the rest list is led by one zone-uniform discrete Hardy inequality]
The map V12 has $18$ rows and the promotion stock grows from forty-seven to \textbf{sixty-four}
per-instance items ($17$ new). The rest list is the shortest of the series: \emph{(R1$'$)} a
\textbf{joint} bound on $\lambda_{\max}(K^{1/2}HK^{1/2})$ --- not factor-wise (the $D$-exponents
$-2.99$ and $+0.33$ cancel against each other), not after dropping the positive part of $H$
(costs $6.4\times$ alone), not after truncating $H$ in either grid (both far parts are
Loewner-positive); \emph{(R1$''$)} the ingredient, \emph{named and located}: a
\textbf{zone-uniform discrete Hardy inequality} comparing the long-range Dirichlet form of
$N=-\mathrm{offdiag}(H)$ against the form $K^{+}$, with the first-moment weight
$\max_kQ_k=18.5$--$339$ as its natural input; \emph{(R3$'$)} a first-moment bound on $(-H)_+$
rather than a decay exponent --- only that enters the certified chain; \emph{(R4)} the $18$
far-carried border blocks; \emph{(R5)} nothing for the layer / envelope / Jaffard / one-pair
families --- certified dead, not to be retried.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The Hardy ingredient resists, and the resistance has an address (T141)}
T141 (\texttt{discrete\_hardy\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{DISCRETE.HARDY}, $22/22$, $51.2$\,s on
$60$ windows with $h=50\ldots1173$, zones $n=11\ldots529$ and $D=2.31\times10^{-3}\ldots
2.60\times10^{-2}$, verdict \textsc{hardy-resists}, bars declared in the docstring before any
number) attacked exactly the
ingredient T140 named --- the zone-uniform discrete Hardy inequality for the pair (Dirichlet form
of $N$, form $K^{+}$) --- and nothing else. It did not close, and the way it fails is the content
of the part.

\emph{Four exact identities put the question in classical shape.} The reduction of T140 becomes a
\textbf{Rayleigh quotient against the pseudoinverse}, $\rho(W)=u^{\mathsf T}Hu/u^{\mathsf T}K^{+}u$
at $u=K^{1/2}v$; the Dirichlet part is a \textbf{signed crossing kernel},
$L_N=D^{\mathsf T}BD$ with $B_{kl}=\sum_{r\le k\wedge l,\;k\vee l<s}N_{rs}$ --- the same closed
covering formula as $K$, one grid further in; T140's Cauchy--Schwarz weight is \emph{identified},
$Q=B_+\mathbf 1$, so that step is the Gershgorin diagonal step on $B_+$ and its price is
attributable; and $DKD^{\mathsf T}=L_\Delta$ on the interior nodes, whose diagonal is the
\textbf{endpoint edge mass} --- a \emph{local} quantity, not $\lambda_{\max}(K)$. Together these
give the two-weight Muckenhoupt quantity in closed form,
$A_{M0}=\max_kQ_k(\Delta\mathbf 1)_{k+1}$, computed by two independent routes. The identity block
of T140 and T141 is now load-bearing as \vref{v545}, Section~\ref{sec:v545}.

\emph{The separation is the finding.} The certified Muckenhoupt constant is \emph{not} zone-uniform:
it grows like $D^{-0.366\pm0.036}$ (a fit) against a preregistered bar of $0.25$. But the object it
is supposed to bound --- the exactly computed Dirichlet share of the finite core --- is
\textbf{strictly uniform on the same surface}, $D^{-0.229\pm0.007}$. The growth therefore does not
sit in the inequality being false; it sits in the \emph{diagonal profile} of the certificate, i.e.\
in the weight one puts on the endpoint mass. That is a repairable defect in a way a growing target
would not be.

\emph{Both bound shapes are typed, and both types are negative.} The \textbf{additive} shape is dead
\emph{as a shape}, not by a weak constant: its own exact Weyl floor --- the best any
mass-plus-Dirichlet splitting can ever reach --- already sits at $1.694$--$3.855\times$ the target,
so no sharpening of the Hardy step can rescue it. The \textbf{joint} shape
$\Lambda(H,Y)\cdot\Omega(Y)$ survives the eigenvalue side and fails at the \emph{normalisation}
alone, $\Omega=20.7$--$2724$: Gantmacher--Krein returns as the obstruction one grid down, and the
whole question is now a question about the profile $Y$.

\begin{keybox}[After T141: R1 collapses to R1b, a single conductance profile]
The rest list contracts from ``a joint bound'' to \textbf{R1b: exhibit a closed conductance profile
$Y$ with $Y\preceq K^{+}$ in the Loewner order and $\Omega(Y)\approx1$.} Both requirements are
per-instance checkable and both are already met separately --- $Y$ positive definite with the
geometric profile, and $\Omega\approx1$ for profiles that lose the Loewner step. The classical
addresses are named, not invoked as authority: Muckenhoupt 1972 and Bradley 1978 for the two-weight
criterion, Opic--Kufner 1990 for the profile calculus, Miclo's spectral-gap weights for the
conductance reading. \emph{(R3$'$)} is now shaped as well: what the Hardy route consumes is a
\textbf{first moment} of $(-H)_+$ and not a decay exponent --- a strictly weaker thing to have to
prove --- and keeping the signs in the crossing kernel is worth a factor $31.95$--$15561.41$
against the $6.4$--$239\times$ the T140 Loewner drop costs. Border pool: of $256$ blocks the
$m$-paired ladder to $m=24$ certifies $245$; the $11$ open ones are far-carried (far mass fraction
$0.835$--$0.902$ at index distance $13$--$15$), unchanged in kind from T140 --- a rebuilt pool, so
the anatomy transfers and the tally does not.
\end{keybox}

\noindent \emph{Verdict integrity.} The negative half of this part rests on a comparison across
zones, so the surface matters: the finite-core reduction of T140 is what makes an $11.21\times$
span in $D$ affordable at all, and on a narrower surface the separation
$D^{-0.366}$ against $D^{-0.229}$ would have been within its own error bars. The verdict
\textsc{hardy-resists} is recorded with that span attached. Thirteen statements of T140/T141 were
mature enough to promote (\vref{v545}); the sandbox stock stands at seventy-five items.

\subsection{The optimal weight is found exactly, and the comparison path closes (T142)}
T142 (\texttt{conductance\_profile\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{CONDUCTANCE.PROFILE}, $24/24$,
$155.4$\,s on $26$ windows with $h=210\ldots878$, zones $n=71\ldots317$ and
$D=2.90\times10^{-3}\ldots1.08\times10^{-2}$, verdict \textsc{profile-resists}, bars declared in
the docstring before any number) does not guess a fourth profile. It \emph{constructs} the
optimum, from the classical address (Miclo 1999; Maz'ya's capacity criteria; Muckenhoupt 1972 and
Opic--Kufner 1990 as addresses, not as authority) --- and the construction settles the search half
of R1b outright. With $J=DKD^{\mathsf T}=L_\Delta$ the endpoint Laplacian, $x=K^{-1}\mathbf 1$ the
equilibrium charge and $\mathrm{cap}=\mathbf 1^{\mathsf T}K^{-1}\mathbf 1$ the capacity,
\[
K^{-1} \;=\; D^{\mathsf T}J^{-1}D \;+\; \frac{xx^{\mathsf T}}{\mathrm{cap}}\,,
\]
\textbf{the capacity decomposition of the optimal Hardy weight} --- verified as an identity to
$5.5\times10^{-12}$ on $26/26$ windows, never asserted. Its Dirichlet half is an
\emph{orthogonal projection} under the congruence ($X=J^{-1/2}DK^{1/2}$ has
$XX^{\mathsf T}=I$), so $\Omega(D^{\mathsf T}J^{-1}D)=1$ \textbf{exactly} (certified
$1.0000001$, the excess being the declared Cholesky floor) --- precisely the object T141 was
missing, in closed form, against the guessed profiles' $\Omega=20.7$--$2724$. The weight was
never a free choice: it is the Green function of the endpoint Laplacian plus the equilibrium
rank one, and the exact capacity Rayleigh form follows with no inequality taken anywhere,
$\rho(W)=\sup_u u^{\mathsf T}Hu/[(Du)^{\mathsf T}J^{-1}(Du)+(x^{\mathsf T}u)^2/\mathrm{cap}]$.

\emph{The failure is a constant, not a power.} The variational optimum over the strict
conductance class (a heuristic search whose every reported number is re-certified by Cholesky;
$11$ windows) reaches $\Lambda\cdot\Omega=1.3160$--$1.4235=1.32$--$1.42\times\rho(W)$ with a
fitted $D$-exponent of $-0.029\pm0.014$ --- inside the uniformity bar, so the class fails by a
\emph{bounded factor at every depth}, a different (and harder) situation than T141's $D^{-1.87}$
blow-up. The stable shape is found: $c_k\sim1/J_{kk}$, the reciprocal endpoint mass (log-log
correlation $0.988$--$0.995$), and the optimum wants essentially \emph{no} mass --- a pure
Dirichlet form. The certified chain of the best closed member reads
$\Lambda\cdot\Omega=2.2671$--$2.4536$ against a target of $0.999983$--$0.999999$ ($0/26$, median
shortfall $2.3376$, flat in $D$ at $D^{0.006\pm0.004}$); the control run with T141's guessed
profile on the same machinery gives $74.84$--$320.03$, so the capacity construction beats
guessing by $31.9$--$137\times$. The single-direction class barrier is saturated
($\beta=1.000000$, the argmax being the top mode itself) and is recorded as a dead route with
its reason.

\emph{The rank ladder closes the comparison path.} On the anchored ladder --- the diagonal
minorant plus the $r$ dominant residual modes, a certified Loewner minorant at every rank with
$\Omega\le1$ throughout --- the chain stalls at $1.408$ ($r=1$) $\ldots$ $1.391$ ($r=128$) and
first crosses the hurdle $1$ only at $r^*/m=0.995$--$0.998$: the tautology $Y=K^{-1}$ itself
(end-to-end anchor: at $r=m-1$ the chain collapses to $\rho(W)$ to $1.7\times10^{-11}$
relative). The structural consequence deserves its own sentence: since $\rho(W)=1-\Theta(D^3)$
and any comparison bound is $\ge\rho$ with equality only at $Y\propto K^{-1}$, a class bound
would have to reproduce the optimal weight to relative accuracy $O(D^3)$ --- \textbf{no
comparison argument can deliver $D$-uniformity}. The next move is forced, not chosen: the
\emph{sharp} route, estimating the exact capacity Rayleigh form directly, with no Loewner step
taken at all.

\begin{keybox}[After T142: the comparison branch is closed, and the rest list is led by the sharp route]
\emph{(R1c)} \textbf{the sharp capacity--Rayleigh route}, which \emph{replaces} the comparison
rather than continuing it: estimate
$\rho(W)=\sup_u u^{\mathsf T}Hu/[(Du)^{\mathsf T}J^{-1}(Du)+(x^{\mathsf T}u)^2/\mathrm{cap}]$
