directly (Miclo 1999, Maz'ya capacity --- a Hardy inequality for the Green form of the endpoint
Laplacian, no profile choice left). What sends the next probe there is measured here: the best
closed minorant is short by $2.27$--$2.45$, the whole conductance class by $1.32$--$1.42$, and
no truncation below $r=m/2$ ever helps. \emph{(R1d)} a multi-direction dual certificate for the
class (LP/SDP over the conductance cone), without which every negative statement about the class
stays \emph{measured}. \emph{(R3$''$)} the corrected R3$'$: T141's line was backwards --- only
$0.03\%$ of the first moment of $(-H)_+$ sits \emph{inside} index distance $8$ (support radius
$204$--$873$), so R3$'$ survives as a \emph{long-range} inequality (the Loewner step
$L_{N_-}\preceq T_{Q^-}$ is certified with the full weight on every window) whose constant
$\max_kQ^-_k=3.20\times10^{2}$--$3.46\times10^{3}\sim D^{-1.73}$ is not $D$-uniform. \emph{(R4)}
border pool rebuilt: $72$ blocks, $68$ certified, $4$ open and far-carried --- the required far
shrink factor, $0.656$--$1.052$, is the one number a decay statement has to beat. Map V14; nine
statements ripe (stock $75\to84$), $0$ promoted here. The verdict, stated precisely: a
resistance of the comparison \emph{method} at a known optimum, not a failure to identify the
weight. T143 (\texttt{sharp\_capacity\_probe.py}) is running at exactly R1c.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The sharp route carries: $D$-uniformity is reduced to one named inequality (T143)}
T143 (\texttt{sharp\_capacity\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{SHARP.CAPACITY}, $24/24$, $18.5$\,s on
$26$ windows with $h=142\ldots1173$ (core $m=141\ldots1172$), zones $n=31\ldots529$ and
$D=2.54\times10^{-3}\ldots1.28\times10^{-2}$, verdict \textsc{sharp-carries}, bars declared in
the docstring before any number) estimates the exact form instead of comparing it --- exactly
R1c, with no Loewner step taken anywhere. The T140/T142 identities assemble into one quotient,
verified as an \emph{identity} on all $26$ windows (numerator to $1\times10^{-11}$, denominator
to $5\times10^{-12}$, eigenvalue reproduction $3\times10^{-6}$ relative under the explicitly
declared $1/\mathrm{gap}$ conditioning factor):
\[
1-\rho(W) \;=\; \inf_v\;
\frac{d^{\mathsf T}\!\left(J^{-1}-B\right)d \;+\; (x^{\mathsf T}v)^2/\mathrm{cap}
      \;-\; \sum_k s_k v_k^2}
     {d^{\mathsf T}J^{-1}d \;+\; (x^{\mathsf T}v)^2/\mathrm{cap}}\,,
\qquad d = Dv\,.
\]
The ingredient bookkeeping at the exact minimiser is structurally new: the minimiser is
\emph{orthogonal} to the equilibrium charge (to $10^{-5}$ --- the capacity rank one carries
\emph{nothing}); the mass share is \emph{negative} ($-0.0698$ to $-0.0029$ --- the site masses
\emph{help} the gap); and the gap itself is the cancellation between the Green share (exactly
$1$) and the crossing share ($1.0029$--$1.0698$). The naive split
$\rho\le\rho_{\mathrm{mass}}+\rho_{\mathrm{long}}$ is certified dead at $6.19$--$66.63$, i.e.\
$6$--$67\times\rho$ itself: any capacity criterion must be applied to the \emph{gap} form, where
its factor of four costs the coefficient and not the scale.

\emph{The core number: Maz'ya's criterion lands inside its window.} Brought to the gap form by a
congruence (the capacities in closed form,
$\mathrm{cap}_E(A)=\mathbf 1^{\mathsf T}[(E^{-1})_{AA}]^{-1}\mathbf 1$, verified against the
constrained minimisation itself), Maz'ya's ratio gives
$\Phi_{\sup}\lambda=0.5438$--$0.6457$ on $26/26$ windows --- inside the window $[1/4,1]$ the
criterion forces for a Dirichlet form --- although this form is \emph{not} one ($72$--$84\%$
positive off-diagonals). The lower bound $\lambda\ge1/(4\Phi_{\sup})$ captures
$0.3872$--$0.4597$ of the true gap, and the route's \textbf{loss factor is zone-uniform}:
$D^{-0.048\pm0.010}$ against the preregistered $0.25$ bar (the gap's own scatter,
$D^{0.000\pm0.079}$, belongs to the arithmetic and not to the criterion); as a coefficient of
$D^{2.829}$ the bound reads $c=4.95$--$67.70$ against the envelope constant $12.04$. The
supremum is not an oracle: the closed families (Green diagonal, transported potentials, index
intervals) recover $0.9606$--$1.0000$ of the best value found, a full add/remove local search
improves them by at most $1.0410\times$, and in the \emph{node} coordinates the near-optimal
sets are \textbf{intervals} of window lags, dominating the extremal level sets by a factor
$8.3$--$129.5$ --- the one-dimensional structure Muckenhoupt's 1972 closed form needs. The
honest half: Miclo's constructive capacitary \emph{chain} loses a factor $46$--$2561$
($\kappa=19.9$--$1144.9$, drifting like $D^{-2.13}$), because the top dyadic level sets are
small sets of $O(1)$ capacity against an eigenvalue of $10^{-5}$ --- the criterion's
\emph{conclusion} holds while its classical \emph{proof mechanism} does not, and that is
precisely where the remaining work sits.

\emph{The rest, typed.} R1d is negative with a reason: the LP dual over the conductance cone
($39$ $\Lambda$ and $35$ $\Omega$ directions, a hand-verified dual point on $4/4$ windows)
certifies a class floor of $\rho(W)$ itself to a factor $1.000000$ --- saturated at the trivial
floor, exactly as T142's single-direction barrier was; a finite direction sample of $\Omega$
cannot bind the cone, so R1d needs the full SDP constraint
$K^{1/2}YK^{1/2}\preceq I$ (recorded so the sampling version is never retried --- and after the
core number above, no longer on the critical path). R4: $36$ border blocks rebuilt, $32$
certified by the ladder, $4$ open; on the $6$ largest blocks \emph{all} $255$--$16383$ subsets
were enumerated, so $\Phi_{\sup}$ is \emph{exact} there ---
$\Phi_{\sup}\lambda=0.6087$--$0.9016$, again inside the window, the only
non-family-restricted supremum of the probe --- and the capacity argmax set is \emph{entirely}
the far tail ($1.000$ of its indices at block index $\ge8$), the exact object a decay statement
has to act on, with T142's far shrink $0.656$--$1.052$ still the number to beat.

\begin{keybox}[After T143: what is missing is a proof, not a number --- one named inequality]
\emph{(S1)} \textbf{the one named inequality}:
$\mathrm{cap}_E(A)\ge|A|\,\lambda_0/c_0$ for \emph{all} sets $A$ with an absolute $c_0$ ---
equivalently $\Phi_{\sup}\lambda\ge1/c_0$. Measured here at $0.5438$--$0.6457$ on $26$ windows
and \emph{certified by enumeration} on the small border blocks; for a Dirichlet form this is
Maz'ya's theorem with $c_0=4$, and what is needed is the criterion for a \emph{non-Markovian}
form ($\sim80\%$ positive off-diagonals). \emph{(S2)} \textbf{the Muckenhoupt route to S1}: in
the node coordinates the near-optimal sets are intervals, so a comparison of the gap form to a
one-dimensional path form \emph{for the purpose of the capacity only} reduces the sup over all
sets to a closed two-weight sup (Muckenhoupt 1972) --- and T142's obstruction does \emph{not}
apply, because the capacity only has to be right to a bounded factor, not to $O(D^3)$.
\emph{(S3)} a closed formula for $\Phi_{\sup}$ or a closed lower bound on
$\mathrm{cap}_E(A)$ (the families already recover $0.9606$--$1.0000$: bookkeeping, not a
search). \emph{(R1d$'$)} the class dual as an SDP, no longer on the critical path. \emph{(R4)}
the $4$ open border blocks, their capacity argmax entirely far. Map V15; ten statements ripe
(stock $84\to94$), $0$ promoted here. The honest state: $D$-uniformity is reduced to a capacity
verification, and the verification is a Muckenhoupt-type statement about \emph{intervals}, not
another profile. T144 (\texttt{capacity\_inequality\_probe.py}) is running at exactly S1/S2.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The interval route carries: the chain has exactly one unproven input (T144)}
T144 (\texttt{capacity\_inequality\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{CAPACITY.INEQUALITY}, $31/31$,
$91.9$\,s on $34$ windows with core $m=142\ldots1393$, verdict \textsc{interval-carries}, bars
declared in the docstring before any number) attacks exactly the first three items of T143's
rest list --- S1, the interval route S2, and the closed capacity bound S3. The probe's single
comparison licence is the \emph{Jacobi whitening} --- a denominator comparison, which T142's
obstruction does not touch --- certified at $\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}=1.1195$--$1.3162$ per window
(Gershgorin would have allowed $\le1.983$); it puts the criterion in coordinates where the
measure is the counting measure. Two different interval-reduction statements then separate, and
only one survives. The \emph{pointwise hull comparison is false}:
$\sup_A\Phi(A)/\Phi(I(A))$ reaches $425$ and drifts like $D^{-1.137}$, with T143's scattered
level sets as the offenders --- so S2 cannot be proved by transporting each set to its convex
hull; it must be a \emph{testing condition}. The \emph{best-interval comparison holds}:
$c_{\mathrm{glob}}=\Phi_{\sup}/\Phi_{\mathrm{int}}=1.0000$--$1.6876$, zone-uniform at
$D^{-0.068\pm0.024}$ against the preregistered $0.25$ bar. The exhaustion behind these numbers
is honest: $136$ blocks of size $g=14$ with \emph{all} $16383$ subsets each, $2.24$ million
sets in total; and capacity \emph{monotonicity} holds on all of them (measured), although the
form is not Dirichlet.

\emph{The two-weight sup, exact rather than sampled.} The interval class is exhausted
\emph{exactly} on every window --- $11\,390\,676$ intervals via a Cholesky nestedness,
$\mathrm{cap}_E([a,b))$ a prefix sum of squared triangular-solve entries, verified to
$10^{-11}$ against an independent solve. The core number of the part:
$B_{\mathrm{res}}\hat\lambda=0.6694$--$0.7813$ inside the Maz'ya window $[1/4,1]$ on the whole
surface, fitted $D^{0.013\pm0.005}$ --- zone-uniform and coordinate-robust (T143 measured
$0.5438$--$0.6457$ in the unwhitened coordinates). The free half passes its falsification test,
$\Phi_{\sup}\hat\lambda\le1$ on all $2.24$ million sets, as the theorem requires. The chain
then closes modulo one constant,
\[
\lambda \;\ge\; \frac{1}{c_0\,\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\,c_{\mathrm{glob}}\,B_{\mathrm{res}}}\,,
\]
certified per window at $0.1002$--$0.2653$ of the exact gap --- with the remaining
$D$-dependence sitting in the Jacobi \emph{transfer} ($D^{-0.178}$), not in the capacity
calculus. Dead with a reason: the naive Muckenhoupt \emph{transcription} through trial-function
energies misses the interval supremum by $89$--$6995$ ($D^{-1.885}$ --- the equilibrium
potential spreads over the whole window, and no local trial function sees it); closed rankings
bound the value but do not point at the argmax. And the honest counter-side, computed exactly:
$\Phi_{\mathrm{int}}\hat\lambda=0.0928$--$0.3578$ drifts like $D^{0.654\pm0.016}$ --- the
intervals saturate S1 only from \emph{above}, no interval-only argument will produce the
constant; what carries is the closed resistance sup above them, a two-weight statement about
the Green function and not about a family of sets.

\emph{The rest, typed.} (i) The Markov perturbation route is \emph{certified dead}:
$\lambda_{\min}(E-P)=-0.340\ldots-0.274<0$ on every window, and the largest certified $t$ with
$E-tP\succ0$ shrinks like $t^*\sim D^{3.96}$; only $3$--$12\%$ of the off-diagonal
\emph{entries} are positive, but they carry $36.7$--$64.5\%$ of the \emph{mass} --- the
positive couplings are load-bearing, so S1 must be proved without the Markov hypothesis.
(ii) S3 returns \emph{stronger than asked}: the Cauchy--Schwarz floor
$\mathrm{cap}_E(A)\ge|A|^2/(\mathbf 1^{\mathsf T}R_{AA}\mathbf 1)$ makes $\Phi_{\sup}$ a
\emph{maximum density subgraph} value for the Green kernel $R=E^{-1}$ (Charikar 2000; Goldberg
1984), so the sup over \emph{all} $2^m$ sets is bounded with a cited absolute constant:
$\Psi_{\mathrm{all}}\hat\lambda=0.7399$--$0.8515$ at $D^{0.015\pm0.005}$ --- the flattest
number of the probe, with no family restriction, no interval reduction and no Markov hypothesis
anywhere; and the argmax density set lives on \emph{intervals} too, an independent confirmation
of the reduction, with $B_{\mathrm{res}}$ only a factor $1.078$--$1.129$ below.
(iii) R4: $22$ border blocks (a new count), $19$ certified, $3$ open; on $5$ blocks \emph{all}
subsets were enumerated, so $c_{\mathrm{glob}}=1.0000$ \emph{exactly} there.

\begin{keybox}[After T144: one unproven input --- the absolute Maz'ya constant]
\emph{(S1$'$)} \textbf{the leading object, the sharpest available shape of S1}: it suffices to
prove the capacitary strong-type inequality for forms whose Green function satisfies a
\emph{mean-density bound} ($\Psi_{\mathrm{all}}\hat\lambda\le0.8515$ measured here) --- a
hypothesis of \emph{Muckenhoupt} type rather than of Markov type. \emph{(S1)} unchanged in
statement, reduced in scope: the family restriction is gone (the density bound covers all
$2^m$ sets), the Markov hypothesis is unrestorable (the positive couplings are load-bearing),
and the coordinates are fixed at a certified $O(1)$ cost. \emph{(S2$'$)} the best-interval
comparison $c_{\mathrm{glob}}\le1.6876$ is measured, not proved; a proof would be a testing
condition, and the exhaustive R4 blocks show it is not vacuous. \emph{(R4)} the $3$ open border
blocks. \emph{(R5)} the whitening sandwich $\lambda/\hat\lambda=1.213$--$2.175$, the last
$O(1)$ factor of the chain, removable by a two-sided estimate of $\kappa$. Map V16; nine
statements ripe (stock $93$), $0$ promoted here. The honest state, in one sentence: the
\emph{only} unproven input of the certified chain is the absolute constant $c_0$. T145
(\texttt{mazya\_proof\_probe.py}) is running at exactly S1$'$.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The proof attempt: one step missing, and it has a name (T145)}
T145 (\texttt{mazya\_proof\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{MAZYA.PROOF}, $33/33$, $8.0$\,s on $64$
windows $m=26\ldots1173$, verdict \textsc{one-step-missing}, the verdict rule hard-wired and
checked \emph{before} the verdict is printed) attacks exactly S1$'$: it does not look for a
better chain, it takes the classical \emph{proof} of the capacitary strong-type inequality
apart, transcribes it onto the non-Markovian form line by line, and reads off what survives.
The step list, declared before it is measured:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}l Y l l@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Step} & \textbf{Content} & \textbf{Status here} & \textbf{Factor}\\
\midrule
M1 & dyadic level decomposition of the minimiser & theorem, verified & $1.649$--$2.670$ ($\le3$)\\
M2 & $|A|\le\Phi_{\sup}\,\mathrm{cap}(A)\le\Psi\,\mathrm{cap}(A)$ (Green floor) & theorem (Cauchy--Schwarz) & $\times1$\\
M3 & $\mathrm{cap}(A_{k+1})\le4^{-k}E(f_k)$, truncation admissible & theorem, verified & $\times4$, slack $\le1.036$\\
M4 & $\sum_kE(f_k)\le E(f)$ --- \emph{the} Markov line & \textbf{void} on this form & measured\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\noindent M1--M3 are theorems for \emph{any} positive definite form (verified here in the
direction they are used, on $168$ levels of $24$ windows for M3). M4 is the only step that uses
the Markov property, and it uses it twice --- the sign of the conductances and the
sign-coherence of the truncation increments. On this form it is \emph{void}, not merely
unproved: on all $64$ windows $3.2$--$14.9\%$ of the off-diagonal entries of $E$ are positive
and carry $18.3$--$58.7\%$ of the off-diagonal mass, the conductance part of the minimiser's
energy is itself \emph{negative} on $61$ of $64$ windows ($-0.380\ldots0.593$ of $E(\psi)$) ---
and the same code on the Stieltjes surrogate $E-P$ reproduces the classical step, so the death
belongs to the form and not to the measurement. T143's Miclo chain loss of $46$--$2561$ is the
same death, now localised in \emph{one line} of the proof instead of in a construction.

\emph{But M4 splits, and only one half is Markovian.} By the pair (Beurling--Deny)
decomposition the step is the sum of a \emph{conductance} half, which needs a nonnegative
weight and fails above, and a \emph{mass} half, which rests on the pointwise theorem
$\sum_kf_{k}^2\le\psi^2$ for the dyadic truncations (measured ratio $0.2639$--$0.2995\le1$) ---
and the mass half \emph{dominates} ($0.407$--$1.380$ of $E(\psi)$), so the full step
$\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}=\sum_kE(f_k)/E(\psi)=0.2145$--$0.4425$ is below $1$ on every window
although its Markov half is dead. That one exact number per window is all the classical proof
ever needed. Two routes then make the constant \emph{explicit}: the \textbf{energy route}
(the classical proof with M4 replaced by its exact value at the minimiser) gives
$c_0=12\,\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}=2.5734$--$5.3099$; the \textbf{sign-free Green route}
(entrywise domination $x^{\mathsf T}Rx\le|x|^{\mathsf T}|R||x|$ for the first use of Markov,
nested-set domination of a nonnegative matrix for the second --- no sign hypothesis anywhere)
gives $c_0=G_{\mathrm{dy}}=2.1559$--$6.3937$, the geometric level constant of the minimiser's
own layer cake. The better route:
\[
c_0 \;=\; 2.2480\text{--}4.2265,\qquad \text{fit } D^{0.028\pm0.017}\ \text{(zone-uniform)},
\]
against Maz'ya's classical Dirichlet value $4$ and the minimum $1.067$--$1.352$ the measured
density sup allows. And S1$'$ stands as a \emph{certified window inequality} on all $64$
windows: $\mathrm{cert\_lam\_max}(R)\le c_0\,\Psi$, with the certified side independent of
whether the computed vector is the minimiser. The price of dropping the sign, as a number: the
density hypothesis must be stated for $|R|$ rather than $R^{+}$, a factor $1.12$--$1.41$ ---
and the direction error this fences ($x^{\mathsf T}Rx\le|x|^{\mathsf T}R^{+}|x|$ is \emph{false})
carries its own built-in witness,
$R=\bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}1&-1/2\\-1/2&1\end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$ at $x=(1,-1)$, where
$3>2$.

\emph{The no-go, and why the hypothesis list is tight.} The ``direct spectral argument'' is not
an independent bypass: $\Psi(A)$ is a Rayleigh quotient of $R$ at $\mathbf 1_A$, so
$\Psi_{\mathrm{all}}\hat\lambda\le1$ is a theorem with \emph{no content} for S1$'$ --- the
load-bearing direction is the lower one, a statement about the minimiser's \emph{profile}
(participation ratio $0.3026$--$0.4588$, $K=35$ levels, delocalised and sign-structured). And
the explicit form $R=aa^{\mathsf T}+\varepsilon I$ with $a_i=i^{-1/2}$ --- positive definite,
entrywise \emph{nonnegative}, with the \emph{proved} density ceiling $4+\varepsilon$ (prefixes
by rearrangement) --- has $\lambda_{\max}\sim\log m$: $\Psi_{\mathrm{all}}\hat\lambda$ decays
$0.7023\to0.4882$ along $m=64\to1500$ while $G_{\mathrm{dy}}$ grows $7.6\to36.8$ (flat
$1.69$--$4.16$ on the Markovian control, $2.25$--$4.23$ on the TFPT windows). So positivity, a
nonnegative Green function and a bounded density sup do \emph{not} imply S1$'$ with any
absolute constant: a hypothesis on the minimiser's \emph{level profile} is necessary, and the
step list is not merely sufficient but tight.

\emph{The verdict discipline, as its own statement.} The verdict rule is checked before the
verdict is printed: \textsc{proof-shaped} requires every step factor to be a theorem or a
certified bound, the replacement of M4 is a \emph{measured} constant at the minimiser, no
a-priori bound for it is available here --- so exactly one step of the transcribed proof is
open and the verdict is \textsc{one-step-missing}. Boundedness (max $4.2265\le16$, the
preregistered ceiling) and flatness ($D^{0.028\pm0.017}$ against the $0.25$ bar) of that
constant are \emph{evidence} for the missing lemma and are reported as such, never as the
lemma. The rest, typed: S2$'$ is \emph{retired as a requirement} (the chain never mentions an
interval, so $c_{\mathrm{glob}}$ is not an input; the finite test family brackets the all-sets
sup with $c_{\mathrm{test}}=1.0782$--$1.1327$ against Charikar's cited $2$); R5 is certified on
\emph{both} sides per window but the sandwich width
$\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}/\kappa_{\mathrm{lo}}=2.668$--$28.532$ drifts like $D^{0.543}$ ---
downgraded, not retired, the used side $\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}=1.1491$--$1.4230$ staying flat
($D^{0.065}$); R4: all $24$ border blocks of this pool close directly and inherit exactly the
one missing input. Map V17, $11$ new statements (stock $104$). The identity spine of the whole
cycle T142--T145 --- the capacity decomposition, the exact capacity--Rayleigh identity, the
Cholesky nestedness, the whitening certificate and the M4 split with the certified chain --- is
load-bearing as \vref{v546} \veri{v546\_capacity\_chain\_identities.py}
(Section~\ref{sec:v546}).

\begin{keybox}[After T145: one named lemma --- L1, the level lemma]
\emph{(L1)} \textbf{the only mathematical item left in the S1$'$ branch}: an a-priori bound,
uniform in $D$, for \emph{one} constant of the minimiser's own dyadic layer cake --- either
$\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}=\sum_kE(f_k)/E(\psi)\le C$ on the energy route (measured
$0.2145$--$0.4425$) or $G_{\mathrm{dy}}\le C$ on the sign-free route (measured
$2.1559$--$6.3937$). Either one alone closes S1$'$ with an explicit $c_0$; the no-go proves
some such bound is \emph{necessary}; and both are delocalisation statements about the
minimiser (participation ratio $0.303$--$0.459$). \emph{(L2)} the visible route: the T143
profile (orthogonal to the charge, masses help) plus a perturbation estimate, with the level
count $K=35$ and the sign share $0.348$--$0.672$ as the profile's address. \emph{(R4)}
bookkeeping only. Retired with reasons: S2$'$ (the chain has no interval step), the Markov
perturbation search (T144 certified it dead, and the proof needs Markov in exactly one line
with two replacements), and the reading of $\Psi_{\mathrm{all}}\hat\lambda\le1$ as evidence (a
content-free theorem). The honest state, in one sentence: T144's unproven input was an absolute
constant with no address --- after T145 it is a bounded, flat, exactly computable quantity
attached to a named object with a necessity proof behind it, which is a strictly better place
to stand and still not a theorem. T146 (\texttt{level\_lemma\_probe.py}) is running at exactly
L1.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The level lemma stands (T146)}
T146 (\texttt{level\_lemma\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{LEVEL.LEMMA}, $30/30$, $7.6$\,s on $64$
windows $m=26\ldots1173$, verdict \textsc{level-carries}, the verdict rule hard-wired on $9$
preregistered conditions: a constant \emph{measured at the minimiser} counts as \emph{not} a
priori, and the rule cannot be argued out of that) attacks exactly L1 --- and closes it on the
measurement surface as a chain of theorems and certified window inequalities, with no step
reading the minimiser.

\emph{The profile defeat is the point.} Four hand-written closed profiles --- the endpoint
sine mode, the linear ramp, the frame's own pole vector, the equilibrium potential of the
lumped partner --- lose against the true minimiser by \emph{five to nine orders of magnitude}
in the Rayleigh quotient: T143's cancellation anatomy seen from the other side, since every
guessed profile sees $O(1)$ while the gap is $O(D^3)$. What survives is the \emph{Green
column} itself --- one solve of the closed endpoint-Laplacian geometry, a legal test vector
(Rayleigh--Ritz) with $\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}/\hat\lambda=1.0497$--$1.3764$, tens of percent
and not orders. The transfer step one would write next is instrumented and then
\emph{discarded}: Davis--Kahan is informative on $0$ of $64$ windows (bound $1.28$--$87.4$),
because the bottom of the whitened spectrum is a near-degenerate \emph{block}
($\lambda_2/\hat\lambda=1.2542$--$3.4471$ --- the separation sits in the denominator); and
even where informative it would not help, since transferring a level-set functional needs an
anti-concentration hypothesis, a second unproved input. L1 bypasses the transfer entirely
because $\psi=\lambda R\psi$ is an \emph{identity}. The step classification, declared before
it is run and then earned: S1 a certified window object, S2 a theorem (Rayleigh--Ritz), S3 a
theorem (the counting lemma, form-independent), S4 a theorem (the resolvent identity) plus
certified column norms with the solve residual paid for --- and S5, the transfer, \emph{not
needed}.

\emph{Delocalisation, read off the form.} The lever of the whole part is one display:
\[
\|\psi\|_\infty\;\le\;\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}\,\max_j\|R e_j\|\,,
\]
verified on every window within a factor $1.055$--$1.426$ of the truth --- the $\Theta(D^3)$
smallness of the gap, everywhere else in this line the obstacle, is here the \emph{tool}: the
minimiser is $\lambda$ times the Green function applied to itself, so it cannot concentrate
where the Green columns do not. Made quantitative: the trivial ceiling
$\max_j\|Re_j\|\le1/\lambda_{\min}$ would give only $\Gamma=\sqrt m\,\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}
\max_j\|Re_j\|\le\sqrt m=5.1$--$34.2$ (useless); measured, $\Gamma=1.8356$--$2.2797$ --- the
column norms sit a factor $2.7$--$16.0$ \emph{below} the trivial ceiling, and that factor
\emph{is} the delocalisation, a functional of the form and never of the minimiser. The
localisation contradiction (restricted Gershgorin over all $\binom{m}{l}$ sets, $\ell^*=2$--$5$)
stays a theorem with no quantitative content (leak tolerance $\le2.38\times10^{-3}$ --- it
forbids only exact localisation), and Perron--Frobenius applies only to the lumped partner
(sign purity $1.0000$), not to $E$ ($0.348$--$0.672$ positive entries): delocalisation does
not come from positivity on this surface.

\emph{The composite constant, and the two structural findings.} The a-priori level constant
\[
c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}\;=\;2b^2\,\Gamma\,\min(1,\Gamma_1)+\varepsilon\;=\;3.9042\text{--}4.8488
\qquad(b=1.031,\ 64/64\text{ windows})
\]
dominates T145's \emph{measured} $2.156$--$6.394$, and the chain
$\lambda\ge1/(\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\,c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}\,\Psi_{\mathrm{abs}})$ holds with loss
factor $0.0422$--$0.1586$ of the exact gap --- every factor a functional of the form alone.
Finding (a): \emph{the cake base is free}. The chain consumes only the lower domination
$|\psi|\le\psi_t$, so Maz'ya's classical dyadic $8$ was never a price of the theorem, only of
the convention: $2b^2\to2$ as $b\to1$, a gain of $3.76$ at zero cost, because the union
identity makes the level constant an $O(m)$ closed form and the levels are never materialised.
Finding (b): the spectrum bottom is a \emph{block}, which kills every perturbation route and
costs the identity route nothing --- the resolvent identity holds for every eigenvector at the
bottom. Remarkably, $c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}$ lands at the size of Maz'ya's classical Dirichlet
value $4$. The stress tests discriminate in both directions: on T145's no-go form $\Gamma$
grows $3.676\to13.800$ ($m^{0.420}$, \emph{unbounded} --- the preregistered criterion) while
the theorem half survives there (slack $1.087$--$1.242$), and on the Dirichlet path-Laplacian
control $\Gamma$ stays flat ($1.7183$--$1.7315$); $\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}$ counts as a certified
building block with its qualification stated (per-window completed Cholesky, maximum $1.4230$;
the $D$-flatness is a fit, so what is certified is a finite list --- the same status the level
lemma has, and the chain is homogeneous). The border pool of this part: $24$ blocks, the
direct Schur route closes $24/24$ and the a-priori chain is positive on all $24$
($c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}=2.855$--$3.176$); T145's $3$ open blocks are not reproducible from a
rebuilt pool --- the anatomy transfers, the count does not. The four a-priori pieces --- the
union identity, the counting lemma at a general base, the resolvent delocalisation bound and
the composite level lemma with its no-go discriminator --- are load-bearing as \vref{v547}
\veri{v547\_level\_lemma\_identities.py} (Section~\ref{sec:v547}).

\begin{keybox}[After T146: the level lemma stands --- one remainder, all-$D$]
\emph{(G1)} \textbf{the one genuine remainder}: $D$-uniformity for \emph{all} $D$ rather than
on a finite surface --- $\Gamma$ is a certified per-window number (fit $D^{-0.016\pm0.005}$, a
fit and never load-bearing), and turning the finite list into a theorem needs an asymptotic
bound on $\max_j\|Re_j\|\,\lambda_{\min}$, i.e.\ the delocalisation of the \emph{Green
columns} themselves. \emph{(G2)} $\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}$ on the energy route stays measured
--- \emph{not} a hole (either constant alone closes S1$'$, and the Green route is the
certified one), recorded so nobody mistakes the asymmetry for a gap. \emph{(G3)} the
\emph{actual} $3$ open R4 border blocks from the T134 assembly, rather than a proxy pool ---
bookkeeping. The honest state, in one sentence: T145's missing lemma is now a theorem chain on
the measurement surface with an explicit a-priori constant of classical size, and what
separates it from a statement for all $D$ is exactly one asymptotic delocalisation bound.
T147 (\texttt{green\_asymptotic\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that asymptotics.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The identity and the named mechanism (T147)}
T147 (\texttt{green\_asymptotic\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{GREEN.ASYMPTOTIC}, $39/39$,
$27.2$\,s on $85$ windows $m=26\ldots1491$ spanning a factor $15$ in $D$, verdict
\textsc{asymptotic-shaped}, the verdict rule wired constructively: ``proved'' cannot be returned
in any branch, because a finite surface proves no statement for all $D$) attacks exactly the one
remainder of T146 --- and the last open object becomes an \emph{exact identity},
\[
\Gamma\;=\;\sqrt{Q^\star}\,\cdot\,S_w\,,\qquad
S_w=\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}\|R\|_F\,,\qquad
Q^\star=m\,\max_j (R^2)_{jj}/\operatorname{tr}(R^2)\,,
\]
verified to $2.3\times10^{-16}$ on all $85$ windows and on both stress forms: the $D$-uniformity
of the level constant is \emph{equivalent} to the boundedness of two named scalars and to nothing
else --- $S_w$ purely spectral (the effective bottom multiplicity
$n_{\mathrm{eff}}=\sum_k(\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}/\lambda_k)^2=1.251$--$2.831$, certified
$S_w\le4.6438$ at a certified cut of a preregistered ladder, the count confirmed by a completed
LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ inertia and no eigenvector read), and $Q^\star$ purely geometric and scale-free
(one for a perfectly flat Green diagonal, $m$ for a localised column; certified $\le2.8634$
against the localised extreme $m=1491$).

\emph{The classical route dies first, by computation.} Demko--Moss--Smith 1984 gives the decay rate
$q=0.994815$--$0.999999$ on this surface, so the envelope $q^{m/2}=0.9346$--$0.9995$ sits above
the $0.05$ bar on \emph{every} window --- the culprit is the condition number $1/\Theta(D^3)$,
not the form ($E$ itself decays off-diagonally with Jaffard exponent $0.06$--$0.82$) --- and the
Green columns are \emph{delocalised} (half-mass radius $0.048$--$0.105$ of the window width), so
no decay statement can be the mechanism: the delocalisation itself \emph{is} the bound. The norm
anatomy locates it --- $0.9864$--$0.9996$ of $\|Re_j\|^2$ at the largest column comes from the
bottom block alone ($|B|=3$--$6$, certified by inertia) --- and the equidistribution constant
$Q_B=m\,\max_j(\Pi_B)_{jj}$, whose exact floor is $|B|$, measures $Q_B/|B|=1.375$--$1.839$: the
naive alternative $Q_B\sim m$ is wrong by a factor $5$--$258$, and T146's unexplained factor
$2.7$--$16.0$ is \emph{identified} as $\sqrt{m/Q_B}$ up to $\lambda_{\mathrm{up}}/
\lambda_{\mathrm{lo}}$ --- growing like $\sqrt m$, exactly the rate that makes $\Gamma$ bounded.
The honest negative is reported with it: the crude free-tail split (drop the mid-spectrum with
$\|\Pi^\perp e_j\|\le1$) is valid but its tail piece grows like $m^{0.538\pm0.020}$ --- on record,
\emph{not} used.

\emph{The mechanism, named and made quantitative.} The form is a \emph{Toeplitz-minus-Hankel
section} ($A_{rs}=c_{|r-s|}-c_{M-1-r-s}$), whose bottom eigenvectors are the Fourier modes at the
symbol's near-zero by Szeg\H{o}/Widom theory (Kac--Murdock--Szeg\H{o} 1953; Widom 1958;
Basor--Ehrhardt 2009) --- and a Fourier mode is \emph{exactly} equidistributed. The sharp
prediction $Q_B\le2|B|(1+o(1))$ \emph{holds} ($1.375$--$1.839\times|B|$), and the chain down to it
is pure arithmetic: $Q_B\le\sum_{k\in B}m\|\psi_k\|_\infty^2\le2\sum_B\|a_k\|_1^2$, with sparsity
$\le8.773$ per bottom mode and $0.999{+}$ of each mode's energy in the lowest $4|B|$ Fourier
modes. The surface is stratified in four $D$-strata with their own certified maxima; six trend
exponents are all flat against the preregistered $0.25$ bar and all labelled fits, never
load-bearing. Discarded with a reason: the congruence structure of the zones does not carry
(scatter $0.465$ --- the equidistribution is a property of the frame, not of the zone
arithmetic).

\emph{Clean-up, in both directions.} $\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}$ on the energy route does \emph{not}
fall, but now has an address: truncation is a nonlinear operation and leaves the invariant
subspace (block leakage up to $0.9627$ against the $2.7\times10^{-7}$ that boundedness through
the split would need) --- not a hole, the Green route is the certified one. \textbf{The $3$ open
R4 border blocks close}: rebuilt in this probe's coordinates, $8/8$ border windows carry a valid
positive lower bound through the T146 chain plus the new spectral bound
($c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}=2.9135$--$3.0383$) --- the same mechanism, no border-specific argument; what
stays open there is the same all-$D$ step, not a border gap. The mandatory stress separates the
forms: on T145's no-go form both constants \emph{diverge} as required ($Q_B=13.49\to190.10$ over
$m=64\to1500$) and hit the closed-form prediction $Q_B=m/H_m$ to $1.0000$, while on the Dirichlet
path-Laplacian control --- whose bottom modes are half-sines, exactly the Fourier modes the
mechanism predicts --- both stay flat ($Q_B/|B|=1.4142$--$1.4376$ against the closed sine value
$2$). A bound that stayed finite on the no-go would be too strong and therefore wrong.

\begin{keybox}[After T147: an exact identity, two certified factors --- one lifting statement]
\emph{(H1)} \textbf{the one lifting statement}: a Szeg\H{o}-type theorem for the \emph{diagonally
reweighted} Toeplitz-minus-Hankel section --- that the bottom block of
$\Lambda^{-1/2}A\Lambda^{-1/2}$ is Fourier-sparse with an $m$-independent $\ell^1$ norm. That
single statement lifts the whole chain from the stratified surface to all $D$ (address: Widom
1958; Basor--Ehrhardt 2009; the measured obstacle is that the mechanism's hypothesis is
translation covariance, which the Jacobi whitening breaks --- Toeplitz defect $0.21$--$0.54$).
\emph{(H2)} an a-priori bound for the spectral half $S_w$, i.e.\ for the eigenvalue counting
function of the form near zero: the factorisation pays $S_w\ge1$ whatever $Q^\star$ does, so a
bottom block whose width grows with $D$ would break the bound even at perfect equidistribution ---
a separate question from the shape of the eigenvectors. \emph{(H3)} the $\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}$
classification --- either an energy-side analogue of the split or the explicit statement that the
Green route is the only certified one. The honest state, in one sentence: what stands after T147
is a theorem-shaped task with two addresses --- the all-$D$ claim remains an extrapolation over
$85$ stratified windows and a factor $15$ in $D$ until the two statements are proved.
T148 (\texttt{szego\_bottom\_probe.py}) is running at exactly the Szeg\H{o} statement.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The lifting statement dissected (T148)}
T148 (\texttt{szego\_bottom\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{SZEGO.BOTTOM}, $31/31$, $13.0$\,s on
$48$ windows $m=96\ldots1077$ in four $D$-strata, stress ladders to $m=1500$, verdict
\textsc{one-input-missing}, the verdict rule wired constructively: ``proved'' cannot be returned
in any branch) attacks exactly T147's two rests --- and closes one of them, at the price of an
honest negative, while the other resists with its hypothesis reduced to a single named scalar.

\emph{The step classification, every sub-statement typed.} The passage from the pure
Toeplitz-minus-Hankel section to the reweighted one $E=\Lambda^{-1/2}A\Lambda^{-1/2}$ runs in
seven steps: \emph{(1)} the order-2 model ladder $4k^2/\pi^2\le\lambda_k/\hat\lambda\le k^2$ is
a \textsc{theorem} (Kac--Murdock--Szeg\H{o} 1953), verified exactly with $C\le\pi^2/4$;
\emph{(2)} the Fourier sparsity of the \emph{pure} section's bottom mode is a \textsc{theorem}
(Widom 1958; Basor--Ehrhardt 2009), measured flat on the model ladder ($\nu=4.236$--$4.257$);
\emph{(3)} the $\ell^1$ \emph{ceiling} --- the lever --- is \textsc{certified}: from the
identity $\mu_k^pa_k=\langle s_k,L_0^p\psi\rangle$ the coefficient bound
$|a_k|\le\min(1,\|s_k\|_\infty\|L_0^p\psi\|_1/\mu_k^p)$ and, with $\mu_k\ge4k^2/(m+1)^2$, the
$m$-\emph{free} closed form $\|a\|_1\le2\sqrt\nu+1$,
$\nu=(m+1)^{3/2}\|L_0\psi\|_1/(2\sqrt2)$ --- every power of $m$ cancelled, $\nu$ alone carries
the $m$-dependence (largest excess $-2.6\times10^{-6}$, i.e.\ slack); \emph{(4)} the chain
$Q^\star\le2\langle\|a\|_1^2\rangle_w\le2\langle\mathrm{ceil}^2\rangle_w$ is \textsc{certified}
on $48/48$; \emph{(5)} the commutator/Davis--Kahan route is \textsc{dead by computation}
(perturbation norm from \emph{below}, the correct direction for a vacuity claim: factor
$5.4\times10^{2}$--$5.3\times10^{5}$, vacuous by $2.7$--$5.7$ orders --- the reweighting is not
a perturbation); \emph{(6)} the live route is weighted Szeg\H{o} via the \emph{Liouville
transform} --- $E\psi=\lambda\psi\Leftrightarrow A\varphi=\lambda\Lambda\varphi$ with
$\varphi=\Lambda^{-1/2}\psi$, an exact change of variables ---
$m\|\psi\|_\infty^2\le2\kappa_\Lambda\,\mathrm{ceil}(\hat\varphi)^2$ with
$\kappa_\Lambda=\max\Lambda/\min\Lambda=1.733$--$3.933$ certified (a ratio of two diagonal
entries of the form, a priori), buying a factor $50.6$ in the smoothness functional of the
Green-weighted modes; \emph{(7)} $\nu$ bounded by a functional of the form: \textsc{the open
input}. \emph{The hypothesis isolated}: a controlled weight-class experiment on a model section
with a genuine order-2 zero shows both bounded-variation weight classes flat ($\nu$ trends
$x^{0.000\pm0.001}$ and $x^{-0.007\pm0.002}$) while the rough class ($\mathrm{TV}\sim m$) grows
as $x^{1.994\pm0.002}$ --- at \emph{identical} $\kappa_\Lambda=4$: it is the roughness, not the
conditioning, and $\mathbf{TV(\log\Lambda)}$ is the named scalar. On the real surface the
a-priori $Q^\star$ ceiling measures $81.4$--$469.0$ and grows $m^{0.615\pm0.016}$; $\nu_L$
would need $\lesssim m/(8\kappa_\Lambda)\approx34$ at the largest window, measured $282$
(trend $x^{0.166\pm0.025}$); $\mathrm{TV}(\log\Lambda)=3.63$--$11.93$, trend
$x^{0.444\pm0.006}$, \emph{not} flat --- the real whitening is rough in the experiment's sense.

\emph{$S_w$ closes --- and the symbol falls.} A layer cake over LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ inertia counts
at a preregistered threshold ladder (no sorted eigenvalue list; Sylvester 1852) certifies
$\mathbf{S_w\le1.3905}$--$\mathbf{1.9587}$ against the true $1.2820$--$1.6825$, trend
$x^{0.007\pm0.007}$ \emph{flat}, certified per $D$-stratum ($4\times12$);
$n_{\mathrm{eff}}=S_w^2=1.644$--$2.831$. The honest negative, asserted rather than hidden: the
Toeplitz symbol of the arithmetic lag sequence has $\mathbf{f(0)<0}$ \textbf{on 48/48 windows}
($-87.3\ldots-22.5$, curvature $1.9\times10^{5}$--$9.7\times10^{7}$) --- the positive
definiteness of the section is a \emph{finite-section effect} of the minus-Hankel part and the
Stieltjes lumping, not a symbol property; the KMS order-2 hypothesis is not available at the
symbol level, and the bottom scaling survives only as a \emph{measurement}
($\alpha=1.643$--$1.989$ inside the preregistered band $[1.5,2.5]$). The mandatory stress
separates exactly where it must: the no-go violates precisely the smoothness hypothesis
($\nu=48.7$--$4.28\times10^{3}$, divergent; its spectral half is innocent,
$n_{\mathrm{eff}}=1.0000$), and the Dirichlet control is exact ($\nu\to\pi$,
$m\|\psi\|_\infty^2\to2$).

\emph{The whole chain, end to end, with the big number.} The chain
$\lambda\ge1/(\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}\Psi)$ with $\Gamma=\sqrt{Q^\star}S_w$
(T147's identity, re-verified in this file's code path to $4.4\times10^{-16}$) is a certified
\emph{lower} bound on all $48$ windows and delivers $\mathbf{8.36}$--$\mathbf{15.86\%}$
\textbf{of the true gap} (median $11.74\%$, no window losing more than a factor $12$); the same
chain with the a-priori-shaped factors substituted is still a lower bound everywhere at a
factor $10.5$ (fraction $0.72$--$1.56\%$) --- the price of a-priority is a number, and the loss
sits entirely in the looseness of the $\ell^1$ ceiling, not in the architecture. The uniformity
balance over all ten factors: six \textsc{certified}, two \textsc{measured} ($\nu_L$,
$\mathrm{TV}$), two \textsc{fits} --- and the fits enter no inequality; the load-bearing
distinction is now certified-per-window versus certified-\emph{and}-$m$-free, and exactly one
factor sits on the wrong side. $\sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}$: \textsc{retired as a route} --- the
Green route is the only certified one, no compiler statement waits on it, and later passes
should not re-open it as a gate. The identity/certificate core of T147+T148 --- the
factorisation identity, the $Q_B$ floor, the $\ell^1$ ceiling, the layer-cake $S_w$ certificate
and the Liouville transform with its weight-class discriminator --- is load-bearing as
\vref{v548} \veri{v548\_green\_szego\_identities.py} (Section~\ref{sec:v548}).

\begin{keybox}[After T148: $S_w$ closes on the surface --- one scalar input remains]
\emph{(I1)} \textbf{the load-bearing rest}: a bound $\nu_L\le F(\text{form})$ with $F$
$m$-free for the bottom block of the \emph{reweighted} section --- equivalently bounded
$\mathrm{TV}(\log\Lambda)$, or any weaker smoothness of the multiplier that still controls
$\|L_0\hat\varphi\|_1$; the target is $\nu_L\lesssim34$ at the largest window, measured $282$.
That single scalar statement is the whole rest of the lifting statement Z1. \emph{(I2)}
cosmetic: a theorem behind the order-2 bottom ladder directly from the minus-Hankel structure
(Basor--Ehrhardt 2009 is the address) --- the $S_w$ certificate does not need it (it is an
inertia count), but without it the $m$-freeness of the KMS constant rests on a measurement.
The honest state, in one sentence: what stands after T148 is a certified end-to-end chain that
delivers $8.4$--$15.9\%$ of the true gap on a stratified surface of $48$ windows, with exactly
one scalar --- $\nu_L$, equivalently the roughness of the whitening weight --- between the
finite list and a statement for all $D$.
T149 (\texttt{weight\_smoothing\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that scalar: whether a
smoothed whitening $\tilde\Lambda$ with a certified sandwich removes the TV roughness.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The smoothing freedom (T149)}
T149 (\texttt{weight\_smoothing\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{WEIGHT.SMOOTHING}, $30/30$,
$61.4$\,s on $44$ prime-power windows $m=277\ldots1393$ in four $D$-strata, $9$ preregistered
gauges, verdict \textsc{partial-smoothing}, the verdict rule wired constructively: ``proved''
cannot be returned in any branch) attacks exactly T148's one open input, through the
observation that the whitening diagonal is a \emph{free gauge}: for any positive diagonal
$\tilde\Lambda$ the chain runs unchanged --- an identity plus one Rayleigh step, licensed and
verified before use --- so every gauge gives a valid lower bound and the maximum over the
family is valid too.

\emph{The three numbers, under the preregistered winner rule} ($8$ admissible of $9$
candidates; the winner is \texttt{const}, the geometric mean of the Jacobi diagonal): the
sandwich is \textsc{certified} at $\sigma\le5.5789$ (trend $x^{0.154\pm0.022}$, bar $8.0$
held); $\mathbf{TV(\log\tilde\Lambda)}$ \textbf{is exactly} $\mathbf{0.0000}$ --- T148's
blocking number, $11.93$ growing as $x^{0.444}$, is not reduced but \emph{eliminated}; and
$\nu_{\tilde L}=222.70$ ($x^{0.170\pm0.047}$) meets the target $m/(8\kappa)$ only on
$\mathbf{21/44}$ windows ($\rho=1.347$ at $m=277$ falling to $0.485$ at $m=1393$) ---
\textsc{partial}: the small-$m$ end is missed. The price of the gauge:
$\tilde\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\le2.3146$ against Jacobi's $1.2647$ (a factor $1.83$, paid once
and linearly, certified). \emph{The real finding is the two-regime decomposition, and it
refutes the hypothesis}: the flutter-smoothing gauges (flutter removed, macro profile kept)
are \emph{cheap} (sandwich $1.129$--$1.572$, $\mathrm{TV}\to1.27$--$1.73$) yet move
$\nu_{\tilde L}$ by at most $\mathbf{0.9\%}$; only the macro-removing gauges improve
$\nu_{\tilde L}$ at all (factor $1.27$) --- the roughness the smoothness functional responds
to was never in the multiplier: $\mathrm{TV}(\log\Lambda)$ \emph{was the mislocated
hypothesis}, withdrawn and relocated --- the roughness sits in the \emph{form} itself.

\emph{The lifted chain.} All $178$ assembled chains are valid lower bounds;
$\Gamma=\sqrt{Q^\star}S_w$ holds gauge-invariantly at $4.4\times10^{-16}$ over $89$
assemblies. Certified, the family-maximum chain delivers $\mathbf{9.52}$--$\mathbf{18.45\%}$
\textbf{of the true gap} (median $12.68\%$; T148's $8.36$--$15.86\%$ reproduced on a longer
surface), a priori $0.800$--$1.566\%$ (an improvement of up to $1.284\times$); on the
certified side the Jacobi gauge still wins ($44/44$ --- its sharper
$\tilde\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}$ outweighs the smoother $Q^\star$ ceiling: the gauge freedom is a
tool for the a-priori side, not a free win). The $Q^\star$ ceiling falls $697.9\to149.1$ with
its exponent falling $0.551\to0.330$ --- bounded yes, \emph{flat no}, and the driver is named:
$\nu_{\tilde L}=265.2$ ($x^{0.772\pm0.048}$) on the Green-weighted modes \emph{above} the
bottom block.

\emph{Stress, and the corrected question.} The mandatory no-go's own multiplier is already
smooth (best sandwich $1.2629$ over the whole family), and its $\nu$ diverges as
$x^{1.420\pm0.004}$ under \emph{every} gauge --- the break stays visible in the form,
smoothing rescues nothing and cannot manufacture equidistribution (passed, preregistered).
The Dirichlet control has a constant diagonal, all $9$ candidates are the identity on it
($5.9\times10^{-14}$), $\nu\to\pi$ exactly, $m\|\psi\|_\infty^2\to2$ --- and the control
\emph{corrects the question}: classically $\nu_k=\pi k^2$ \emph{exactly}, so the right form is
the ladder $\mathbf{\nu_k\le Ck^2}$ \textbf{with} $m$\textbf{-free} $C$ (measured in the
\texttt{const} gauge: $C=18.66$--$44.61$, $x^{0.272\pm0.021}$, attained at $k=1$). The TV
anatomy of the Jacobi diagonal locates the cheapness: the macro part is $0.78$--$1.52$
($8.5$--$14.2\%$ of the total), the flutter part $6.42$--$13.02$ --- many \emph{small} steps
(amplitude $0.064$--$0.182$, \emph{flat} at $x^{-0.007\pm0.028}$, largest single log-step
$\le0.193$) spread over the whole window, with the frame edges not the source (enrichment
$0.75$--$1.20$): TV grows through the step count while the sandwich pays only the amplitude.
The order-2 bottom ladder from the minus-Hankel structure stays \textsc{named open}, cosmetic
($C_{\mathrm{bot}}=5.6$--$14.1$; Basor--Ehrhardt 2009 does not cover a sign-changing symbol;
the chain does not wait on it --- $S_w$ is certified by an inertia count). Seven promotion
candidates are named and stay unpromoted.

\begin{keybox}[After T149: TV eliminated, the missing input relocated --- a ladder remains]
\emph{(J1)} \textbf{the load-bearing rest}: an $m$-free constant $C$ in the ladder
$\nu_k\le Ck^2$ for the modes of the smoothed section (measured $C=18.66$--$44.61$,
$x^{0.272\pm0.021}$) --- with the constant gauge this is \emph{exactly} a question about the
deep modes of the pure Toeplitz-minus-Hankel section, no multiplier, no whitening, no
arithmetic weight; it makes the $Q^\star$ ceiling $m$-free and the whole measurement-surface
chain a priori. \emph{(J2)} \textbf{the second lever, which would close (J1) outright}: an
a-priori bound on the flutter \emph{amplitude} of $\log\operatorname{diag}(A_B)$ --- measured
$0.064$--$0.182$ and \emph{flat} ($x^{-0.007\pm0.028}$); it would make
$\kappa_{\tilde\Lambda}=1$ and $\tilde\kappa_{\mathrm{up}}\le2.3146$ a priori rather than
certified per window. \emph{(J3)} cosmetic: the order-2 ladder theorem from the minus-Hankel
structure (unchanged from T148). The honest state, in one sentence: after T149 the smoothing
freedom has retired $\mathrm{TV}(\log\Lambda)$ as a hypothesis --- eliminated exactly, at a
certified price --- and the one input between the finite list and a statement for all $D$ is
now the smoothness of the deep modes of the pure section, in ladder form.
T150 (\texttt{mode\_ladder\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that ladder, through the flat
flutter amplitude.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The mode ladder and the parity mechanism (T150)}
T150 (\texttt{mode\_ladder\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{MODE.LADDER}, $36/36$, $48.0$\,s on
$72$ prime-power windows $m=50\ldots1393$, verdict \textsc{one-term-missing}, the verdict rule
wired constructively: ``proved'' cannot be returned in any branch) attacks the ladder
$\nu_k\le Ck^2$ through the flat flutter amplitude --- T149's rest (1) via rest (2) --- and
comes back with the mechanism's \emph{name}.

\emph{The parity compression.} The reflection-odd form that every probe since T106 works with
is \emph{exactly} the compression of the full symmetric Toeplitz section onto its
antisymmetric parity sector:
\[
U_-^{\mathsf T}\,T_M(c)\,U_-\;=\;T-H\,,\qquad
U_+^{\mathsf T}\,T_M(c)\,U_+\;=\;T+H\,,\qquad
U_+^{\mathsf T}\,T_M(c)\,U_-\;\le\;4.0\times10^{-16}
\]
also on real windows (Basor--Ehrhardt 2009: the parity sectors are the natural objects of the
Toeplitz$+$Hankel algebra) --- and the inertia \emph{localises}: \textbf{the entire negative
inertia of the sign-changing symbol sits in the even sector} (odd $0$, even $1$ on $72/72$,
LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ certified). T148's honest negative $f(0)<0$ is thereby \emph{explained
rather than worked around}: it is a statement about the \emph{other} parity sector. The
matched parity sines $t_k(r)=\tfrac{2}{\sqrt N}\sin(2\pi k(r{+}1)/N)$, $N=2m{+}1$ (exact
eigenpairs of the parity Laplacian, corner $3$ forced by the antisymmetry; control model exact,
$\nu_k=\pi k^2$ to $0.0002$) give a third valid ceiling, improving the ladder constant by
$1.000$--$1.130$ over the Dirichlet$+$Neumann minimum of T148/T149.

\emph{The zone diagonal, and who carries the positivity.} The whitening diagonal is an
\emph{explicit zone function},
$\Lambda_r=c_0-c_{M-1-2r}+\sum_{s\ne r}\max(c_{|r-s|}-c_{M-1-r-s},0)$ (residual $0.0$ on
$72/72$), and the lag vector splits exactly as $c=c^{\mathrm{arch}}+c^{\mathrm{atom}}$ with
$c^{\mathrm{atom}}\le0$. The flutter amplitude becomes a certified \emph{form} functional,
no fit anywhere: $\mathrm{amp}_{\mathrm{loc}}=0.0606$--$0.1993$ at trend $x^{-0.165\pm0.015}$
(T149's fit-based $0.064$--$0.182$ reproduced fit-free), with the certified chain
$\mathrm{amp}_{\mathrm{loc}}\le\mathrm{half}\cdot d_1^{\log}\le
\mathrm{half}\cdot d_1^\lambda/\min\Lambda$ ($1.345$--$3.954$) and the closed atom budget
$\|c^{\mathrm{atom}}\|_1\le4B\sqrt N$. The honest negative beside it: the archimedean
diagonal is \emph{not} the macro profile --- the level shift
$\|\log(\Lambda/\Lambda^{\mathrm{arch}})\|_\infty=0.734$--$2.647$ grows as $x^{0.244}$;
the flutter is small and a priori, the \emph{level} is not. And the perturbation step is
precisely dead, for two independent reasons: the multiplicative (Loewner) step transfers
scale-free with $\exp(\mathrm{amp})\le14.11$ --- the gauge step \emph{closes} --- while the
additive step (Weyl 1912 / Bauer--Fike 1960 / Davis--Kahan 1970) compares
$\|A^{\mathrm{atom}}\|=4.3$--$12.3\%$ of the section against a $\Theta(D^3)$ bottom and
misses by $2.4$--$5.8$ \emph{orders of magnitude}; moreover the archimedean-only section is
itself \emph{indefinite} ($2$--$7$ negative eigenvalues, LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ certified) while
the full section has none --- \textbf{the atoms are co-responsible for the positivity}, not a
perturbation of it.

\emph{The one gate, with its number.} The chain delivers $0.0680$--$0.1845$ of the true gap
certified (the best window reproduces T149's $0.1845$ exactly), a priori
$8.16\times10^{-3}$--$1.74\times10^{-2}$; the ladder-shaped $Q^\star$ ceiling is valid by
monotonicity ($38.76$--$261.3$ against the certified minimum $36.98$--$144.6$, price
$\le2.555\times$ for the reduction to \emph{one} constant). But
$C=14.24$--$43.39\;(=13.81\pi)$ with trend $x^{0.258\pm0.009}$: the gate ``ladder constant
non-growing'' falls --- the only one of all. The maximum sits at $k=1$ ($67/72$), the growth is
carried by the deeper modes, and the gap $\pi\to13.81\pi$ is exactly the \emph{arithmetic
leakage} of the bottom mode ($\ell^1$ mass $1.41$--$1.79$,
$m\|\psi\|_\infty^2=2.45$--$3.41$ against the model's $2$). The stress is now localised:
because the amplitude and the ladder are separate statements, the T145 no-go can be asked
\emph{which} it violates --- the answer is the ladder alone (its multiplier is smooth,
$\mathrm{TV}\le0.2334$; its ladder constant grows $x^{1.840\pm0.130}$), while the Dirichlet
and parity controls reproduce $C=\pi$ to $0.0004/0.0002$. The identity/certificate core of
T149+T150 --- the gauge freedom with the const gauge and its certified sandwich, the two-regime
discriminator, the explicit zone diagonal with the closed atom budget, the parity compression
with the inertia localisation, and the parity-sine calibration with the per-window ladder
certificate --- is load-bearing as \vref{v549} \veri{v549\_gauge\_parity\_identities.py}
(Section~\ref{sec:v549}).

\begin{keybox}[After T150: the mechanism is named --- one inequality remains]
\emph{(K1)} \textbf{the load-bearing rest}: $\nu_k\le Ck^2$ with an $m$-free $C$ for the
\emph{odd parity sector} of a sign-changing-symbol Toeplitz section --- \emph{the} one
inequality; measured $C\le43.391=13.81\pi$ certified per stratum, trend $x^{0.258\pm0.009}$
against the flatness bar $0.25$. \emph{(K2)} a closed lower bound on
$\min\Lambda^{\mathrm{arch}}$ from the smooth kernel --- it would make the flutter-amplitude
ceiling closed rather than per-window certified. \emph{(K3)} the extension of Basor--Ehrhardt
2009 to a symbol with $f(0)<0$, or an explicit local model at the symbol minimum inside the odd
sector. The honest state, in one sentence: after T150 the mechanism has a name --- parity ---
T148's honest negative is explained (the negative inertia lives in the even sector), the
flutter amplitude and the zone diagonal are certified form objects with the atoms
co-responsible for positivity, and the one input between the finite list and a statement for
all $D$ is a single named inequality about the low modes of one explicitly described finite
matrix.
T151 (\texttt{odd\_ladder\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that odd-sector ladder.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The odd ladder and the Sobolev reroute (T151)}
T151 (\texttt{odd\_ladder\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{ODD.LADDER}, $27/27$, $43.4$\,s on
$72$ prime-power windows $m=96\ldots1491$, verdict \textsc{odd-carries}, the verdict rule
wired constructively: ``proved'' cannot be returned in any branch) attacks the one remaining
inequality --- and closes it by \emph{rerouting off the ladder}.

\emph{The grid step-over, and the matrix-versus-symbol lesson.} The positivity of the odd
sector is a \emph{grid} fact: the parity sines live on the shifted grid $\theta_k=2\pi k/N$
with $\mu^P_k=2-2\cos\theta_k$ exactly, and the symbol's negative window (width
$\theta_c=7.06\times10^{-4}$--$1.32\times10^{-2}$) lies \emph{below} the first odd grid point
$\theta_1$ on every window ($\theta_c/\theta_1=0.328$--$0.407$, trend $x^{-0.057}$): the
grid steps over it on $72/72$ --- stepped over rather than cancelled. But the pointwise
symbol \emph{language} fails at exactly this resolution: $f''(0)=3.8\times10^{5}$--$6.8\times
10^{8}$, $f$ moves by a relative $0.47$--$0.85$ across \emph{one} mode spacing, the section
form averages the symbol against a Fej\'er kernel of exactly that width instead of sampling
it (mode-by-mode deviation $1.39$--$1.83$ at $k=1$), and the one-parameter symbol margin is
vacuous ($\rho\ge1$) on $72/72$. \textbf{T150's rest item 3 --- extending Basor--Ehrhardt
2009 to $f(0)<0$ --- is thereby answered in the negative}: the local model at the symbol
minimum exists, but as a \emph{matrix} statement, not a symbol one ---
$\lambda_k(A)\le S\mu^P_k$ for $k\le8$ certified by completed LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ counts
(Sylvester 1852) with $S=1.1019$--$2.3870$: the odd effective minimum sits at $\theta_1$,
order $2$, $O(1)$ prefactor, $f(0)<0$ \emph{absorbed} by the Rayleigh floor
$L_P\ge\mu^P_1I$. The leakage T150 left as a bare number is anatomised: a \emph{low-$k$
rotation} of the bottom mode away from $t_1$ ($\sin\angle(\psi,t_1)=0.8156$--$0.8943$,
band shares $0.355$ at $k=1$, $0.687$ at $k=2$--$4$, $0.005$ beyond $k=64$; sideband peak at
$l^\ast=2$) --- not an arithmetic sideband.

\emph{The Sobolev step, and the linear bound.} The multiplicative mode comparison T150 asked
for \emph{is} the discrete Sobolev step: an odd-sector vector vanishes at the virtual node,
so $\|\psi\|_\infty^2\le2\|\psi\|_2\|\nabla\psi\|_2$; the certified pencil floor
$\kappa=0.2273$--$0.4502$ (\emph{one} completed Cholesky per window, floor carried, trend
$x^{0.047\pm0.009}$) bounds the gradient energy by $\lambda_k/\kappa$, and
\[
b_k\;:=\;m\|\psi_k\|_\infty^2\;\le\;2m\sqrt{\frac{K_{\mathrm{bot}}\,\mu^P_k}{\kappa}}
\;\le\;C_S\,k
\]
with $C_S=11.5137$--$19.5731$ (median $13.82$, trend $x^{0.020\pm0.007}$):
\textbf{linear in $k$ and non-growing} where T150's quadratic ladder grew ($C=13.81\pi$ at
$x^{0.258}$) --- nothing additive anywhere, so the $\Theta(D^3)$ bottom that killed Weyl /
Bauer--Fike / Davis--Kahan never enters, and the $\sqrt m$ that kills every rotation route
(the $\nu$ conversion costs $4\sqrt m$ and overshoots $\pi$ by $4.2\times10^{3}$--$1.0\times
10^{6}$) is never paid; at the bottom the gain over the $\nu$ route is a factor
$7.5$--$34.9$. The layer cake buys only $1.00$--$2.37$ and does not change the trend. The
honest negative beside it: the \emph{global} pencil ratio grows $x^{2.695}$ (useless ---
$L_P$ saturates at $4$ while the section's top does not); everything is carried by the
\emph{bottom} ratio $R=K_{\mathrm{bot}}/\kappa=3.3634$--$9.7108$ at $x^{0.037\pm0.015}$ ---
the \emph{one} remaining fit in the chain.

\emph{The large numbers, and the localised stress.} T150's rest item 2 is closed \emph{and
attained}: $\min_r\Lambda^{\mathrm{arch}}_r=c^{\mathrm{arch}}_0-c^{\mathrm{arch}}_{M-1}
=2.5928$--$4.8981$ at relative deviation $0.0$ (two per-window-checked shape properties).
$Q^\star_{\mathrm{sob}}=12.39$--$23.94$ ($x^{0.029\pm0.008}$) beats the $\ell^1$ ceiling
$43.66$--$158$ by $2.69$--$10.13$; end to end moves from
$7.88\times10^{-3}$--$1.74\times10^{-2}$ to
$2.01\times10^{-2}$--$3.52\times10^{-2}$ of the true gap (gain $1.64$--$3.18\times$), and the
dominant loss is now $\Psi=4.7\times10^{3}$--$6.1\times10^{6}$ --- never before the
bottleneck. The stress localises exactly where it must: the T145 no-go breaks \emph{in the
pencil and only there} ($R$ grows $x^{1.986}$ to $3.0\times10^{7}$; its localised bottom mode
has parity Dirichlet energy $\sim1/\log m$ instead of $\mu^P_1$), while the controls are
exact (parity pencil $(1,1)$ to $3.73\times10^{-12}$, $C/\pi-1\le2.5\times10^{-4}$; the
model Sobolev price is \emph{exactly} $\pi$ --- the honest, $m$-free cost of leaving the
$\ell^2$ world), and the Dirichlet-versus-parity pencil is $1.985$--$1.999$: one rank-one
corner mismatch, bounded. Verdict \textsc{odd-carries}: all six preregistered gates met,
every layer typed --- \emph{theorem} (parity compression, Weyl minimax, Sylvester, exact
$\mu^P$), \emph{certified} ($\kappa$, $K_{\mathrm{bot}}$), \emph{elementary-verified} (the
Sobolev step) --- and the one fit named. The identity/certificate core of T151 --- the grid
step-over with the certified symbol vacuity, the certified bottom ladder, the Sobolev step
with the $\pi$-price calibration, the closed-and-attained archimedean minimum, and the linear
per-mode bound with the no-go discriminator --- is load-bearing as \vref{v550}
\veri{v550\_odd\_sector\_identities.py} (Section~\ref{sec:v550}).

\begin{keybox}[After T151: the bound is linear --- one scalar remains]
\emph{(L1)} \textbf{the load-bearing rest}: the $m$-freeness of the bottom pencil ratio
$R=K_{\mathrm{bot}}/\kappa$ --- \emph{one} scalar, no eigenvector, the only non-certified
link left in the chain; measured $3.3634$--$9.7108$, trend $x^{0.037\pm0.015}$, and the T145
no-go breaks exactly there ($x^{1.986}$). \emph{(L2)} a closed lower bound on
$\kappa=\lambda_{\min}(A,L_P)$ from the lag vector --- it would make the Sobolev route closed
rather than per-window certified; the symbol route to it is \emph{vacuous} and the reason is
now known, so this needs the arithmetic of the atoms, not an asymptotic. \emph{(L3)} $\Psi$,
the new dominant end-to-end loss now that $Q^\star$ has been improved. The honest state, in
one sentence: after T151 the one inequality closes by rerouting off the ladder --- the odd
grid steps over the symbol's negative window, the bottom spectrum is certified against the
parity Laplacian, the Sobolev step makes the per-mode bound linear with a non-growing
constant --- and the whole distance between the finite list and a statement for all $D$ is
the $m$-freeness of a single printed scalar.
T152 (\texttt{pencil\_ratio\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that scalar.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The pencil ratio and the cancellation (T152)}
T152 (\texttt{pencil\_ratio\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{PENCIL.RATIO}, $37/37$, $33.2$\,s on
$60$ prime-power windows $m=149\ldots1445$, verdict \textsc{one-term-missing}, the verdict
rule wired constructively: ``proved'' cannot be returned in any branch) attacks the one
scalar T151 left as a fit --- the $m$-freeness of the bottom pencil ratio
$R=K_{\mathrm{bot}}/\kappa$ --- and returns the sharpest structural fact of the phase.

\emph{Both hoped-for gifts refuted: positivity is a cancellation.} $A^{\mathrm{arch}}$ is
\emph{not} positive in the odd sector on any of the $60$ windows
($\lambda_{\min}=-2.8132$--$-1.8396$, certified negative by a completed Cholesky of
$A^{\mathrm{arch}}-tI$ with the floor carried): the odd sector removes the constant mode but
not the negative archimedean mass near the origin. In pencil normalisation the failure is
catastrophic, not marginal --- $\kappa_{\mathrm{ar}}=\nu_{\min}(A^{\mathrm{arch}},L_P)
=-5.28\times10^{5}$--$-4.00\times10^{3}=O(-m^{2})$, because the lowest parity sine has
$L_P$-energy $\mu^P_1=O(m^{-2})$ while it sees an $O(1)$-negative form. And the atom part is
negative too ($\nu_{\min}(A^{\mathrm{atom}},L_P)=-1408$--$-25.6$ on $60/60$): \emph{neither}
summand of the exact split is positive, and the sum is --- \textbf{the positivity of the odd
section is a cancellation between the archimedean kernel and the prime-power atoms, not a
property of either half} (exhibited exactly at $t_1$: an archimedean part
$-5.26\times10^{5}$--$-3.98\times10^{3}$ plus an atom part
$8.19\times10^{3}$--$2.88\times10^{6}$ add to a near-$O(1)$ quotient --- the atoms even
\emph{overshoot} the archimedean hole). The additive Weyl route misses $\kappa$ by a measured
$1.5\times10^{4}$--$1.8\times10^{6}$ and the closed $\ell^1$ budget route by a further
$2.3\times10^{5}$--$9.9\times10^{7}$ --- the $O(m^{2})$ conversion $1/\mu^P_1$ kills both.
But the archimedean diagonal is negative on only an $m$-\emph{independent} number of parity
modes ($k\le4$--$7$ of up to $1445$), and above them its pencil quotient is $\ge0.989$ on
$60/60$: \textbf{the Schur two-block criterion (Schur 1917 --- an equivalence, with both
Cholesky floors subtracted) with a fixed $16$-mode low block certifies
$\kappa\ge t=0.2250$--$0.2500$ on every window}, at $m^{0.006\pm0.036}$ with identical
quartile medians --- the floor is no longer a fit. It consumes \emph{one} unproven
inequality --- $B_{HH}\succeq tI$ on the bulk block --- and entrywise routes are provably
insufficient for it: Gershgorin returns $-8.99\times10^{5}$--$-1.3\times10^{3}$ on the full
whitened parity block and still $-7189$--$-58$ after cutting at the last arch-negative mode,
the median row coupling being $3.9$--$19.6$ against a diagonal of $0.36$--$0.89$. What is
needed is a \emph{block} statement, not a bound on individual prime-power sums.

\emph{The ratio, certified on both ends.} The archimedean part does not carry the
\emph{ceiling} either: $K^{\mathrm{arch}}_{\mathrm{bot}}=24.6$--$1326$ at $m^{1.519}$, one
to two orders of magnitude above the true $K_{\mathrm{bot}}=1.102$--$2.118$ and growing
where the true one is flat --- the atoms do not perturb an archimedean alignment, they
\emph{create} it. The new number: \textbf{$R\le K_{\mathrm{bot}}/t=4.408$--$8.471$ at
$m^{0.099\pm0.167}$, certified on both ends} (inertia ladder above, Schur floor below), with
a quartile walk of $1.269$ over a $4.54\times$ $m$-range. Two structural $m$-free routes to
the ceiling lose four to six orders of magnitude: Courant--Fischer on the parity basis (the
atom overshoot is $O(m^{2})$), and the two-sided bulk block --- whose KMS ratio step \emph{is}
a theorem ($\sin a/\sin b\le a/b$, hence $\mu^P_{k+16}/\mu^P_k\le289$, verified
$285.95$--$288.97\le289$ on the table) but whose ingredient $T_{HH}=26.8$--$3116$ grows
$m^{2.044}$: the section's low \emph{eigenvalues} track the model's while its low
\emph{eigenvectors} do not. The no-go reconstruction breaks exactly at the unclosed step
(the floor survives; ceiling and $R$ grow $m^{1.994}$) --- the gap is real, not a tool loss;
the control ($L_P$ against itself) is $(1,1,1)$ to $3.5\times10^{-11}$.

\emph{The $\Psi$ map, and the honest dual reading.} $\Psi\le8.70\times10^{3}$--$6.24\times
10^{5}$ \emph{is} $1/\lambda_{\min}(E)$ to within $1.12$--$1.19$ --- the spectral bottom of
the whitened section and essentially nothing else; it grows $m^{2.18}$ because
$\mu^P_1=O(m^{-2})$, not because $\kappa$ degrades. The reserve: \textbf{$89$--$92\%$ of
$\operatorname{trace}(E^{-1})$ sits on the eight lowest modes --- exactly those the
certified ladder already controls}; rebuilding the subset supremum into a ladder sum is
worth $3.46$--$5.29\times$ end to end (it would lift T151's
$2.01\times10^{-2}$--$3.52\times10^{-2}$ into the
$8\times10^{-2}$--$1.4\times10^{-1}$ range) --- now the dominant loss, outranking any
further sharpening of $R$. The verdict is kept honest by printing both readings: on the
band estimator alone gate B would fail and the verdict would read \textsc{ratio-resists};
the quartile-median reading ($15$ windows per stratum, high variance of the band fit) is the
declared one, and both are in the output. Also on the record: T151's $R$ exponent $0.037$
was fitted against the zone variable (not monotone in $m$) --- the same quantity refit in
$m$ is $0.099$, so part of that flatness was the fit variable rather than the object. Ten
map entries are named; \emph{none is promoted} --- the strongest candidates consume the
unproven block positivity $B_{HH}\succeq tI$ and stay pending with exactly that reason.

\begin{keybox}[After T152: the floor is structural --- one term and one rebuild remain]
\emph{(M1)} \textbf{the block inequality}: $B_{HH}\succeq tI$ on the bulk block --- the
single unproven input the Schur certificate consumes; entrywise/Gershgorin bounds provably
cannot deliver it ($-8.99\times10^{5}$--$-58$ even after the cut), so it must be a block
statement. \emph{(M2)} \textbf{the load-bearing rest}: an $m$-free ceiling on
$K_{\mathrm{bot}}$ --- every structural route loses four to six orders of magnitude because
the low eigenvectors are not the model's, and the T145 no-go breaks exactly there
($m^{1.994}$). \emph{(M3)} the $\Psi$ rebuild, subset supremum $\to$ ladder sum --- worth
$3.46$--$5.29\times$ end to end, now the dominant loss and ahead of any further work on $R$.
Only after these: frames beyond A, zones beyond prime powers. The honest state, in one
sentence: after T152 the positivity of the odd sector is known to be a cancellation between
geometry and arithmetic rather than a property of either half, the pencil floor is certified
by a fixed-size Schur block instead of a fit, the ratio is certified on both ends with one
$m$-free ceiling missing --- and the largest remaining loss sits on modes the certified
ladder already controls.
T153 (\texttt{psi\_ladder\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that rebuild and that block
inequality.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The $\Psi$ pinning and the two remaining terms (T153)}
T153 (\texttt{psi\_ladder\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{PSI.LADDER}, $33/33$, $7.6$\,s on
$18$ prime-power windows $m=96\ldots1430$, block anatomy on $11$ and the ceiling on $12$ of
them, verdict \textsc{rebuild-resists}, the gates \emph{evaluated} rather than chosen) carries
out the rebuild T152 mapped --- and refutes it by exactly its own factor.

\emph{The pinning: the reserve was never there.} The rebuild itself is real and comes in three
rigorous forms, each an upper bound on the same $\Psi$: the mode-resolved ladder sum (cost
$1.195$--$1.761\times$ against the T149 route), the arithmetic-free operator form
$(\mathrm{const}/t)\,\Psi_P$ against the pure Kac--Murdock--Szeg\H{o} model (cost
$3.73$--$9.70\times$), and the theorem-grade collapse $\Psi\le\mathrm{const}/(t\,\mu^P_1)$
(cost $3.73$--$9.70\times$). But the decisive measurement is a \emph{lower} bound: for the
subset that maximises the first mode, every term of
$\sum_k\lambda_k^{-1}\langle v_k,\mathbf 1_S\rangle^2$ is nonnegative, so
$a_1/\lambda_1\le\Psi\le1/\lambda_1$ with $a_1=0.7695$--$0.8306$ --- exactly the
$8/\pi^2=0.8106$ of the first parity sine. \textbf{$\Psi$ is pinned to within
$1/a_1=1.204$--$1.300$, so T152's mapped $3.46$--$5.29\times$ reserve never existed}; the
head/tail replacement T152 proposed lies $2.31$--$6.12\times$ \emph{below} the hard lower
bound and is not an upper bound at all --- the maximising subset is aligned with $v_1$ itself,
so the head \emph{is} the value of $\Psi$, not a redundancy. The bookkeeping is exact: the
rebuild retires the level constant $c_0^{\mathrm{ap}}=4.157$--$4.870$ and pays the collapse
cost $3.73$--$9.70$; on this surface the two cancel, the net end-to-end gain is
$0.50$--$1.11\times$ (median $0.62$), and the certified fraction moves from
$2.01\times10^{-2}$--$3.52\times10^{-2}$ to $1.01\times10^{-2}$--$3.92\times10^{-2}$ --- not
the targeted $8\times10^{-2}$ band. The collapse cost is named exactly:
$\lambda_1(A)/(t\,\mu^P_1)=3.25$--$8.40$ --- the pencil minimum is not attained at the bottom
mode, so a floor uniform over all modes is lossy for mode $1$ alone. What the rebuild does buy
is \emph{uniformity}: the collapse form contains nothing beyond an explicit arithmetic sum,
the one open block inequality $t$, and the exact $\mu^P_1$ --- it retires Charikar's licence
and every per-window diagonalisation from the $\Psi$ step. \textbf{$\Psi$ is closed as a
term.} The balance is clean: $t$ is flat ($x^{-0.004}$, certified $0.2400$--$0.2500$ on all
$18$ windows) and the gain is flat ($x^{-0.039}$).

\emph{The sign reversal, the four dead candidates, and the live route.} $B_{HH}\succeq tI$ is
the original pencil inequality restricted to $\mathrm{span}\{t_k:k>16\}$ --- an identity, not
an analogy: the Schur route relocates the question onto a subspace, it does not ease it. The
find of the zone reverses a T152 sign: \textbf{on the bulk parity block the archimedean part
is positive} ($\lambda_{\min}(B^{\mathrm{arch}}_{HH})=1.0362$--$1.4143>t=0.25$), after an
$m$-free peeling of at most $8$ modes on every window --- the archimedean negativity lives
entirely in the lowest parity modes, and on the bulk it is the \emph{atoms} that are negative
($-484.6$--$-14.95$): the obstruction has moved from a negative smooth kernel to an
arithmetic remainder. Four block candidates die: entrywise Gershgorin ($-1929$--$-21.35$ on
the bulk), block Gershgorin (Feingold--Varga 1962) on a dyadic partition
($-333.4$--$-7.68$; the coupling is long-range), a mode-index Toeplitz-plus-Hankel fit
(residual $0.70$--$0.95$ of the Frobenius mass --- no second-level Szeg\H{o}/Widom argument
exists), and a recursive Schur step, which certifies the same $t=0.25$ at levels $0/1/2$ ---
\emph{scale-invariant}, so recursion alone can never terminate. The one route that produces
positivity at all is a relative (Kato-type) bound in the archimedean metric:
$1+\lambda_{\min}(C)=8.3\times10^{-4}$--$2.9\times10^{-2}$, certified positive on every window
where all three additive candidates return a negative number --- but it shrinks as
$x^{-1.778}$: positivity per window, not a uniform floor. The whole of $t$ sits in how far
inside $-1$ the arch-whitened atom operator lies, and that distance is of the same order as
$\mu^P_1$ itself.

\emph{The inverse-iteration find.} No family fixed in advance carries the $K_{\mathrm{bot}}$
ceiling: the fixed-size band replacement grows exactly like the bulk ceiling it replaces
($T_{\mathrm{band}}$ at $x^{2.017}$ against $T_{HH}$ at $x^{2.019}$), the parity sines and
the Hann taper grow at $x^{2.47}$ and $x^{2.46}$, and the Fej\'er family degenerates (a sine
is an eigenvector of every convolution). The atom overshoot is located exactly: the Toeplitz
half of the atom response of the first parity sine is $-1.99$ (the atoms \emph{help}) while
the Hankel reflection half is $+9.45$ --- a boundary-reflection effect, not a bulk-atom
effect. But \textbf{two inverse-iteration steps $(A^{-1}L_P)^2t_k$ give a flat
$K^F=1.432$--$2.369$} ($x^{0.116}$) against the certified true
$K_{\mathrm{bot}}=1.102$--$1.896$, on a certificate of fixed size $8$: the ceiling \emph{is}
carried by a fixed-size family --- just not by one written down in advance. Nine map entries
are named (v580--v588); \emph{nothing is promoted} --- the probe is exploration only, and the
T149--T152 candidates stay pending.

\begin{keybox}[After T153: $\Psi$ is closed --- two terms remain, and they are one object]
\emph{(N1)} \textbf{the block inequality} $B_{HH}\succeq tI$ on the bulk parity block ---
after T153 its sharp form is \emph{one} number: the distance of the arch-whitened atom
operator inside $-1$ ($8.3\times10^{-4}$--$2.9\times10^{-2}$, shrinking as $x^{-1.778}$), to
be bounded against the $m$-free archimedean reserve $1.04$--$1.41$ the bulk block now
carries. \emph{(N2)} \textbf{the Green/alignment estimate}: a structural description of
$(A^{-1}L_P)^2t_k$ for $k\le8$ --- it closes the ceiling (the flat $K^F=1.43$--$2.37$ is
already certified \emph{by} that family) \emph{and} recovers the collapse factor
$3.25$--$8.40$ that the uniform floor throws away: \textbf{both remaining terms lose to the
same missing object} --- where the bottom eigenvector of the section sits. The $\Psi$ rebuild
is off the list: the theorem-grade collapse needs no certificate beyond $t$. The honest
state, in one sentence: after T153 the hoped-for reserve is known to have been a mismap ---
$\Psi$ is pinned to within $1.204$--$1.300$ by the first parity sine and closed as a term ---
the archimedean part is positive on the bulk block after an $m$-free peeling of at most $8$
modes, and the two remaining terms are one and the same missing object, a Green estimate for
the bottom of the section.
T154 (\texttt{green\_align\_probe.py}) is running at exactly that estimate.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The ceiling closes at fixed size, and the obstruction becomes geometry (T154)}
T154 (\texttt{green\_align\_probe.py}, contract \texttt{GREEN.ALIGN}, $29/29$, $5.0$\,s on
$12$ prime-power windows $h=50\ldots1077$, the Kato anatomy on $10$ of them, verdict
\textsc{align-resists}, the gates \emph{evaluated} rather than chosen) attacks the
Green/alignment estimate both T153 terms lose to --- and the ceiling half closes, exactly.

\emph{The ceiling, closed with a direction lesson.} The part opens with a correction to its
own commission, stated explicitly because it is easy to get backwards: the ceiling needs
\emph{no residual argument at all}. Ritz values are \emph{upper} bounds for the eigenvalues
of the \emph{same index} (Courant--Fischer 1920 / Cauchy 1829 interlacing), so a Ritz
quotient is a ceiling by a theorem; Temple 1928 / Kato 1949 is a \emph{floor} device and is
needed only there. The certificate:
$\mathrm{span}\{t_1..t_8\}+A^{-1}L_P\,\mathrm{span}\{t_1..t_8\}$ --- sixteen columns,
\emph{fixed} size, the whole certificate $8$ completed Choleskys of matrices
$\le8\times8$ --- carries $K^F=1.1019$--$1.9964$, flat ($x^{0.094\pm0.058}$), and
\textbf{agrees with the inertia-certified $K_{\mathrm{bot}}$ to a relative
$5.17\times10^{-7}$ on every window}: the sixteen-column certificate does not merely bound
the true bottom ladder, it \emph{attains} it, and the eight LDL$^{\mathsf T}$ counts of
size $m$ per window are retired from the ceiling step
\veri{v551\_ritz\_ceiling\_certificate.py}. The parity sines alone overshoot as
$K^F=11.2$--$2.1\times10^{4}$ at $x^{2.47}$ (T152's $O(m^2)$ reproduced), and the geometry
says why the Green step repairs them: the true bottom eight of $A$ lie inside the
twice-iterated subspace to $\le0.33^\circ$, the Green columns have only
$1.8$--$4.9\%$ of their mass outside the sine block, and their pencil Rayleigh quotients
sit at $0.287$--$0.681$, just above the certified floor $t=0.25$ --- precisely what an
inverse-iteration step should do (Kaniel 1966 / Paige 1971, quoted as the explanation,
never used as a bound).

\emph{The floor half, refuted at fixed size and recovered at full size.} The Temple/Kato
residual argument --- with the separation $\beta=t\,\mu^P_9$ that the containment of the
parity sines makes free, $\mu^P_9/\mu^P_1\to81$ exact --- fails by
$\|R\|^2/(\beta\lambda_1)=6.3\times10^{3}$--$5.6\times10^{9}$, growing as $x^{4.50}$: any
sine-containing subspace has residual $O(\|A\|)=O(1)$ against a target
$\lambda_1=O(\mu^P_1)=O(m^{-2})$, for \emph{every} fixed containment depth (the
preregistered scan over $k_c=8\ldots48$ closes $1$ of $12$ windows, and that one needs
$k_c/m=0.64$ --- not fixed size in effect; the scalar Temple form is vacuous on $12/12$).
\textbf{The obstruction is named}: seven of the eight bottom directions of $A$ agree with
the bottom of $L_P$ to $0.15$--$1.35^\circ$ (median), \emph{one} sits at
$82.93$--$89.79^\circ$ --- and that single misalignment \emph{is} the collapse price.
One completed Cholesky of $A-\gamma I$ per window recovers it \emph{in full},
$4.408$--$7.985$ --- certified per window, size $m$, not $m$-free, labelled as such. The
missing fixed-size ingredient reduces to \emph{one arithmetic-free number}: the $L_P$
floor on the complement of the eight bottom Ritz directions --- a question about the
tridiagonal $L_P$ and one eight-dimensional subspace, with no prime-power input anywhere
--- measured flat at $5.93$--$8.25\,\mu^P_1$, and feeding the measured value into the
fixed-size Temple step returns $4.408$--$7.617$ of the price $4.408$--$7.986$: worth
$91$--$100\%$, conditionally.

\emph{The Kato anatomy, and the double refutation.} The loss of the live R1 route is
\emph{not} the floor: $\lambda_{\min}(B_{HH})=0.266$--$0.415>t=0.25$, yet the Kato product
delivers only a factor $1.97$--$121$ less ($x^{1.635}$), because the two minima are
attained in different directions, $24.9$--$89.8^\circ$ apart --- the minimiser that ruins
the relative bound is a perfectly harmless direction for the block inequality itself.
Its mass sits $96.5$--$100\%$ on the eight modes \emph{directly above} the peel cut (only
$0.9$--$11.9\%$ at the boundary), so T153's Hankel-reflection culprit is refuted
\emph{twice}: at the minimiser both halves hurt (Toeplitz $-100.3\ldots-0.248$, Hankel
$-72.69\ldots-0.90$), and removing the reflection destroys positive definiteness
altogether ($\lambda_1=-7.49\ldots-2.64$) --- the reflection half is load-bearing, not a
